# OTR v2.0 Bug Log

Active bug log for the v2.0 Visual Sidecar build.
Every bug gets logged the moment it is found. Entries are never deleted.

---

### BUG-LOCAL-125: `OTR_SceneSequencer.scene_manifest_json` output is a permanently-empty stub (`json.dumps([])`); slot is wired into RETURN_TYPES but never populated

- **Date:** 2026-05-01 ~06:30 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** YES (subtle: stub outputs that look real on the graph but emit empty data)
- **Symptom:** Wiring BatchHumoRender's `ledger_json` input to `Node 3.scene_manifest_json` (output slot 2, type STRING) delivers the literal string `"[]"`. BatchHumoRender's `_load_ledger_with_path` then tries `Path("[]")`, which doesn't exist on disk, raising `RuntimeError: BatchHumoRender: ledger path not found: []`.
- **Cause:** In `nodes/scene_sequencer.py`:
    - Line 689: `manifest = []` (initialized empty)
    - Line 890: `manifest_json = json.dumps(manifest, indent=2)` -- always produces `"[]"`
    - The `manifest` variable is NEVER appended to between init and serialize. `scene_manifest_json` is a declared output that ships with no implementation.
- **Fix:** Two paths possible:
  1. **Populate the manifest** -- have SceneSequencer write `{scene_id, shot_id, beat_id, start_s, dur_s, ...}` entries as it processes each item, then serialize. This is the "correct" fix for v2.0-beta but adds non-trivial code in a phase that's already complex.
  2. **Mark the output as deprecated/unused** in the docstring + RETURN_NAMES so future graph-editors don't get fooled into wiring it. Less work but loses the option of fixing it later.
- **Verify:** After fix #1, query `node3_outputs[2]` and confirm it's a non-empty JSON array with at least scene_id + start_s on each entry.
- **Tags:** stub-output, scene-manifest, sequencer, v2.0-beta, deferred-fix
- **Cross-reference:** Surfaced via the failed BUG-124 fix (see below).

### BUG-LOCAL-124: Workflow link "fix" was wrong -- QA report's static analysis didn't verify what the target output actually contained [REVERTED in commit pending]

- **Date:** 2026-05-01 ~06:30 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** YES (lesson: static analysis can't replace runtime verification)
- **Symptom (from earlier today):** External QA report flagged Node 12.video_path -> Node 51.ledger_json + Node 52.ledger_json as a misroute (mp4 path string going into a JSON-manifest input). Recommended wiring Node 3.scene_manifest_json (slot 2, "previously unused") in its place. Patch shipped in commit `d180b62`.
- **Actual result:** Crashed the next live run with `RuntimeError: BatchHumoRender: ledger path not found: []` because Node 3.scene_manifest_json is an unpopulated stub that always returns `"[]"`. See BUG-LOCAL-125.
- **Real root cause analysis:** The "misroute" the QA report flagged was actually a working pattern. BatchHumoRender's `_load_ledger_with_path` (lines 1694-1756) explicitly handles `.mp4` paths arriving as `ledger_json` input -- it derives the ledger filename from the .mp4 stem with a multi-tier fallback chain (exact match -> underscore-collapse variant -> directory scan with mtime + episode_id substring match). The "misroute" leveraged this fallback intentionally, even if the type label looks wrong on the graph.
- **Symptoms originally attributed to the "misroute" had a different cause:**
  - "Wanted radio still missing" was a real BUG-121 hypothesis: FLUX phase didn't render the bookend because of `open_close=true` gating, OR ledger stamping failed silently. The BUG-121 filesystem fallback (commit `cee4ebb`) is the right defensive fix and stays.
  - "No scene cuts in composited mp4" still needs investigation -- not the manifest misroute either.
- **Fix:** `git checkout fea2f23 -- workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_full.json` -- restore the workflow to its pre-d180b62 state. Verifier: `scripts/_verify_bug124_reverted.py` confirms links 79 + 82 are back, links 86 + 87 are gone, Node 12.video_path.links = {47, 79, 80, 82}.
- **Verify:** Live run completes BatchHumoRender phase without `ledger path not found` error. Cast bindings populate, lines dispatch to HuMo, mp4 generated.
- **Lessons (worth a memory note):**
  1. Static-analysis QA reports can confidently identify a problem's surface and prescribe a wrong fix if they don't verify the assumed semantics of the target node's output.
  2. ComfyUI's STRING type is permissive -- "wrong type label" sometimes means "the consumer has a defensive fallback for this case." Check the consumer's actual code before declaring something a misroute.
  3. The patch script `scripts/_fix_workflow_link_misroute.py` and the post-patch verifier `scripts/_verify_workflow_link_misroute_fix.py` are kept in repo as audit trail of the failed attempt; the new `scripts/_verify_bug124_reverted.py` is what we run going forward.
- **Tags:** workflow-revert, qa-report-wrong, static-analysis-limits, mp4-stem-fallback, audit-trail, bug-088-fallback-was-by-design

### BUG-LOCAL-124-original (pre-revert): Workflow JSON link misroute -- Node 12.video_path (mp4 path) wired into Node 51 + Node 52 ledger_json inputs (expected JSON manifest) [FIXED in commit pending]

- **Date:** 2026-05-01 ~05:00 (surfaced via external static-analysis QA report) | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** YES (this is the textbook ComfyUI silent-failure pattern: STRING type that doesn't differentiate file paths from JSON strings)
- **Symptom:** Live runs show HuMo dispatching with no scene-boundary cuts, radio still resolution failing even when the file exists, BUG-088 cast-still fallback chains firing for every line. `signal_lost_earth_is_splitting_open_beneath_the_paci_20260430_211312.mp4` shows base radar playing straight through with HuMo clips overlaid violently rather than cutting cleanly.
- **Cause:** ComfyUI's STRING type system is permissive: a file path and a serialized JSON manifest carry the same type tag, so the graph editor doesn't flag the mismatch. The break happens at runtime in the consumer:
    - **Link 79:** `Node 12 (OTR_SignalLostVideo) .video_path` -> `Node 51 (OTR_BatchHumoRender) .ledger_json` (input slot 5) -- **MISROUTE**
    - **Link 82:** `Node 12 .video_path` -> `Node 52 (OTR_VideoComposite) .ledger_json` (input slot 2) -- **MISROUTE**
  Both Node 51 and Node 52 call `json.loads(ledger_json)` and read scene timings, in/out marks, clip slot indices. They were instead handed the literal mp4 path string, fell through to "no manifest" defaults: HuMo skipped scene cuts, radio still resolver returned None on non-dict input, fallback chains kicked in incorrectly. This was the actual root cause behind several recent symptoms attributed to other bugs.
- **Fix:** Surgical workflow patch (`scripts/_fix_workflow_link_misroute.py`):
    1. Remove links 79 and 82 from `data.links` and from `Node 12.video_path.links`.
    2. Wire `Node 3 (OTR_SceneSequencer) .scene_manifest_json` (output slot 2, previously unused) to:
        - `Node 51.ledger_json` (input slot 5) via new link 86
        - `Node 52.ledger_json` (input slot 2) via new link 87
    3. Update `last_link_id` from 85 -> 87.
  Link 47 (`Node 12 -> Node 20.audio_gate` write-completion gate) and Link 80 (`Node 12 -> Node 52.procgen_video_path` base layer) remain untouched -- both correct as-is. Verifier script: `scripts/_verify_workflow_link_misroute_fix.py` (6 invariants, all pass post-patch).
- **Verify:** Open the workflow in ComfyUI, confirm Node 51 input 5 + Node 52 input 2 both show wires originating from Node 3 (not Node 12). Re-run a short test episode -- expect composite to cut cleanly between radar and HuMo clips at scene boundaries from `scene_manifest_json`. Spot-check Node 52's report output for any "ledger parse failed" or "manifest missing" warnings (should be absent). The "violent smash" overlay behavior should be gone.
- **Tags:** workflow-json, comfyui-string-type, link-misroute, silent-failure, ledger-manifest, scene-cuts, root-cause-of-bug-121, root-cause-of-bug-088-fallback-storm

**Cross-reference:** This finding revises the root-cause hypothesis on BUG-LOCAL-121 (radio still resolver). My earlier "radio bookend gated on open_close=true" hypothesis was wrong -- the actual cause was that BatchHumoRender was getting a video path string instead of a parseable ledger dict, so `_resolve_radio_still_path` correctly returned None on non-dict input, and the cast-still fallback chain took over. The BUG-121 filesystem fallback I shipped earlier today (commit cee4ebb) is still useful as a defensive layer (handles the case where ledger stamping fails silently), but the primary fix is this BUG-124 link surgery.

### BUG-LOCAL-123: ComfyUI keeps models resident across prompts; Run 2 OOMs because Run 1's HuMo+WanTE+WanVAE+Whisper never unloaded [FIXED in commit pending]

- **Date:** 2026-04-30 ~23:55 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** YES (clean repro, generic to any multi-stage ComfyUI pipeline that uses heavy models)
- **Symptom:** Run 1 completed clean (`Prompt executed in 02:48:31`, 14/14 HuMo clips, mp4 generated). Run 2 started immediately in the same ComfyUI process, succeeded through 3-spine evaluator (max_new_tokens=150-800), then OOM'd at the full-script generate (max_new_tokens=1024) inside Mistral-Nemo `_prefill` SDPA attention:
    ```
    torch.OutOfMemoryError: Allocation on device 0 would exceed allowed memory
    Currently allocated: 24.76 GiB
    Requested: 3.99 GiB
    Device limit: 15.92 GiB
    Free (according to CUDA): 0 bytes
    ```
  Memory summary at OOM: peak alloc 30026 MiB, peak reserved 30240 MiB on a 16 GiB device — same paging-counter overstatement pattern, but Free=0 was real because models from Run 1 were still resident.
- **Cause:** ComfyUI's `model_management` deliberately keeps loaded models resident across prompts to amortize cold-load time. For OTR runs that load HuMo (16.5 GB) + WanTE (6.4 GB) + WanVAE + Whisper, that means ~24 GB of "loaded models" stay in the LRU table between consecutive runs. The next run's small spine generations succeed (low KV cache pressure) but the 1024-token full-script generate's prefill K/V allocation requires more contiguous device memory than is free — OOM. No cudaMalloc retries because PyTorch's allocator doesn't know it can evict ComfyUI's loaded models without explicit `unload_all_models()`.
- **Fix:** `nodes/video_composite.py::execute()` wrapped in try/finally that calls new `_end_of_run_cleanup()` static method on every exit path (success, error, raise). Cleanup pipeline: `comfy.model_management.unload_all_models()` -> `soft_empty_cache(force=True)` -> torch fallback (`gc.collect()` + `cuda.synchronize()` + `cuda.empty_cache()`). Every step has its own try/except + log so a single failure never breaks the run. VideoComposite is the LAST node in every OTR workflow, so this catches all paths.
- **Verify:** Run two OTR episodes back-to-back in the same ComfyUI process. After Run 1 finishes, log should show `[VideoComposite] end-of-run cleanup: unloading all models` -> `unload_all_models() OK` -> `soft_empty_cache(force=True) OK`. Run 2's Mistral-Nemo prefill must NOT OOM on the 1024-token generate. Memory summary at start of Run 2's LLM phase should show Free > 8 GiB.
- **Tags:** vram, oom, end-of-run-cleanup, model-management, multi-prompt, comfyui-residency, mistral-nemo, prefill, sdpa

### BUG-LOCAL-122: Ledger not progressively updated during render — empty ledger mid-pipeline misleads observers

- **Date:** 2026-04-30 ~22:05 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** TBD (likely yes — affects every observability surface)
- **Symptom:** Live run mid-HuMo-render: `pending_20260430_210737_ledger.json` shows `cast=[]`, `lines=[]`, `scenes[].line_count=0`, `clips=[]` while BatchHumoRender console output reports `cast-still binding: 3/3 cast members matched` and `BUG-094 estimated 14 line timings across 177.8s episode`. Ledger is stale relative to actual pipeline progress. Owner expects real-time updates whenever possible.
- **Cause:** Ledger gets stamped only at specific milestones (post-news-selection, then again at episode finalize). LLM, Director, Bark, FLUX, and BatchHumoRender phases run with in-memory state; their outputs are not progressively written back to the pending ledger file. Any observer (the owner, the soak tailer, future watchdog UIs) sees an empty ledger until the run finishes — by which point the value of real-time observability is already lost.
- **Fix (proposed, multi-phase work):**
  1. Add `_ledger_write_stamp(ledger_path, phase_label, payload)` helper that does an atomic JSON merge-and-write (open, parse, deep-merge, write to .tmp, rename) so concurrent reads never see a half-written ledger.
  2. Wire stamps after each major phase: ScriptWriter (cast + scenes + lines populated), Director (sfx[] + music[] + beats[]), Step 4b/4c mirror (lines[] with speaker_role tagged), Bark (audio paths populated), FLUX (radio_bookend_path + cast_still paths), BatchHumoRender (clips[] with per-line render plan + timestamps), VideoComposite (final_video_path).
  3. Add `ledger.meta.last_phase_completed` and `ledger.meta.last_phase_stamped_at` so observers can tell at a glance how far along the run is.
- **Verify:** Tail the pending ledger every 30s during a long run. Confirm `cast` populates within ~3 min, `lines[]` within ~5-10 min, `clips[]` populating progressively as HuMo dispatches each clip. No file corruption from concurrent reads (atomic .tmp + rename).
- **Tags:** ledger, observability, real-time, atomic-write, multi-phase-fix, pipeline-instrumentation

### BUG-LOCAL-121: Radio bookend FLUX render gated on `open_close=true`, but ANNOUNCER role can exist without `open_close` — radio HuMo dispatch falls back to cast-still face

- **Date:** 2026-04-30 ~22:00 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** TBD (likely yes — clean repro pattern)
- **Symptom:** Live ComfyUI run with `gen_params_initial.open_close=false`, Mistral-Nemo + Pro Ultra Quality, target_words=420. Director generated cast `c03=ANNOUNCER` despite `open_close=false`. Step 4b/4c speaker_role tagging worked correctly (l001 tagged `speaker_role=announcer`). BatchHumoRender log:
    ```
    [BatchHumoRender] line l001 speaker_role=announcer wanted radio still but it's missing; using full_env_00154_.png (source=ledger-cast-fresh) instead -- audio will be muxed lossless either way, but visual won't be the radio
    ```
  Audio path holds (C7 lossless mux), but the visual intent for radio roles (announcer / music_* / sfx) collapses to "person face" rather than "1940s radio device prop." Tier-honest degraded state — pipeline does NOT crash.
- **Cause:** Radio bookend FLUX render is gated on `open_close=true` in the workflow. When `open_close=false`, no `radio_bookend_<ep>.png` is generated and no `ledger.radio_bookend_path` / `ledger.meta.radio_bookend_path` is stamped. But Director can still spawn an ANNOUNCER (and music/sfx rows) regardless of `open_close`, so the radio HuMo dispatch path activates without its source asset present. `_resolve_radio_still_path(ledger)` correctly returns None (file doesn't exist), and the BUG-088 cast-still fallback chain takes over gracefully.
- **Fix (proposed):** Decouple radio bookend render gating from `open_close`. Two options: (a) Render `radio_bookend_<ep>.png` whenever any line in `ledger.lines[]` has `is_radio_role(speaker_role) == True` — gate on script content, not the open_close framing toggle; (b) Always render a generic radio still as fallback regardless of `open_close`, since cost is one FLUX call per episode. Option (b) is simpler. Option (a) is more elegant.
- **Verify:** With `open_close=false` and a Director that spawns an ANNOUNCER role, confirm `radio_bookend_<ep>.png` exists on disk and BatchHumoRender log line shows `ref=radio_bookend_<ep>.png source=radio-still` for the announcer line, NOT a fallback message.
- **Tags:** radio-bookend, speaker-role, flux-gating, open_close, bug-088-fallback, tier-honest-degrade, non-fatal — `pending_20260430_061112` aborted in `OTR_SignalLostVideo.render_video` after 14:43 elapsed; writer never emitted a usable title token [AUTO-LOGGED]

- **Date:** 2026-04-30 06:25 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** TBD
- **Symptom:** ComfyUI log tail (last error before idle, run finished revision then crashed in video stage):
    ```
    [2026-04-30 06:25:55.780] [error] !!! Exception during processing !!! TITLE_RESOLVE_FAIL: no usable episode title. script_json_title='', widget=''. Writer must emit a {"type":"title"} token or the widget must be set to a non-stuck value.
    File "C:\...\ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio\nodes\video_engine.py", line 1271, in render_video
      raise RuntimeError(
    RuntimeError: TITLE_RESOLVE_FAIL: no usable episode title. script_json_title='', widget=''.
    [2026-04-30 06:25:55.783] [info]  Prompt executed in 00:14:43
    ```
  ScriptCritic verdict=REVISE score=87 ran cleanly (06:20:05) and the reviser successfully applied (1995 -> 2023 chars at 06:20:41). All audio phases completed (EpisodeAssembler stamped post_episode_assembler dur=95.0s at 06:25:55). The crash happened in `render_video` at the very end. Ledger `pending_20260430_061112_ledger.json` shows `final_audio_path=''`, `final_video_path=''`, `clips=0`. This is the loud failure designed in BUG-LOCAL-035; means upstream title-resolution path is broken again — the writer either did not stamp `{"type":"title"}` into `script_lines` after the revision pass, OR the revision pass dropped/overwrote the title token, OR the widget default was reset to empty. Impact: 14m43s of audio work discarded, no shippable artifact.
- **Cause:** TBD (auto-logged by scheduled tailer; manual triage required) — leading hypothesis: the revision pass (verdict=REVISE -> reviser at 06:20:41 added 28 chars to the script) may have rewritten `script_lines` without re-injecting the `{"type":"title"}` token that the writer prepended pre-critic. Alt: writer never emitted `TITLE: ...` as the first line on this run (Mistral-Nemo low-temp drift). Need TITLE_TRACE log line from this run to confirm which.
- **Fix:** TBD
- **Verify:** TBD
- **Tags:** unexpected_traceback, title-resolve-fail, video-engine, regression, script-revisions, auto-logged, soak-tailer

### BUG-LOCAL-118: BatchHumoRender ledger lookup fails when SignalLostVideo's title sanitizer doubles an underscore that the Ledger renamer left single [FIXED in commit pending]

- **Date:** 2026-04-30 08:51 | **Phase:** 0-3 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Episode `signal_lost_scientists_restore_memory_by_blocking_a__20260430_084741` (note: "_a__20260430") completed audio + video successfully through SignalLostVideo. BatchHumoRender then tried to load the ledger and crashed:
    ```
    RuntimeError: BatchHumoRender: derived ledger from .mp4 not found:
      ...signal_lost_scientists_restore_memory_by_blocking_a__20260430_084741_ledger.json
    ```
  The actual ledger on disk was named `..._a_20260430_084741_ledger.json` (SINGLE underscore between "a" and "20260430"), but BatchHumoRender derived the path from `final_video_path` which had `..._a__20260430...` (DOUBLE).
- **Cause:** SignalLostVideo's title sanitizer collapses certain characters into a different underscore sequence than the Ledger.rename_episode joiner. Title fallback to `news_seed.headline` produced "Scientists restore memory by blocking a [tau-tangle protein]" -> sanitized to a stem ending in "..._blocking_a" -> SignalLostVideo joined with timestamp using "_" while the ledger renamer used "__". The two filename conventions diverged.
- **Fix:** in `nodes/batch_humo_render.py::_load_ledger_with_path`, when the .mp4-derived path isn't found, try a fallback chain:
  (1) collapsed-underscore variant of the stem (`__` -> `_`)
  (2) directory scan for newest `*_ledger.json` whose normalized episode_id substring-matches the .mp4 stem (capped at 1-hour age so we don't bind to stale ledgers)
  (3) raise the original error only if all three fail
- **Verify:** next run with the same title-fallback path should produce a `[BatchHumoRender] BUG-LOCAL-118 underscore-mismatch fallback` WARNING and continue. Long-term: align SignalLostVideo + Ledger renamer on a single sanitization rule (deferred).
- **Tags:** batch-humo-render, ledger-lookup, signal-lost-video, title-fallback, BUG-115-cascade, soak-tailer, auto-logged

### BUG-LOCAL-117: SceneSequencer ran successfully but did NOT write back lines[].start_s / dur_s / bark_wav_path to ledger; downstream consumers see nulls [FIXED in commit pending]

- **Root cause:** the Reviser rewrites the script TEXT but doesn't update `ledger.lines[].text`. SceneSequencer matches dialogue_positions against `ledger.lines[].text` -- after revision, the texts diverge so NO matches succeed and the per-line write-back loop falls through silently.
- **Fix:** in `nodes/script_critic.py::execute()`, after a successful revision + re-parse, walk `ledger.lines[]` in order and replace each `.text` field with the corresponding revised dialogue line's `.line` text. Save the ledger. SceneSequencer's text-match now succeeds and per-line start_s/dur_s/start_s_space land correctly.
- **Verify:** next run's `signal_lost_*_ledger.json` should have non-null `lines[].start_s` for every dialogue line.

### BUG-LOCAL-117 (original entry preserved below for triage history):

### BUG-LOCAL-116: SFX render produces 3-millisecond WAVs (150 samples / 48000 sr) — entire SFX layer effectively silent [FIXED in commit pending]

- **Root cause:** two independent bugs stacked:
  1. `nodes/batch_audiogen_generator.py::generate()` always set `duration = default_duration` -- Director's `sfx_plan[i].dur_s` was IGNORED for every cue.
  2. The `audio_values[0, 0]` slice assumed a 3D `[batch, channels, samples]` output but newer transformers AudioGen returns `[batch, samples]` (2D) where `[0, 0]` becomes a SCALAR -- a single sample, ~3 ms WAV.
- **Fix:** in BatchAudioGenGenerator: (a) honor `sfx_plan[i].dur_s` / `.duration_sec` / `.duration` (any of the schema variants), clamped to [0.5, 10.0] sec; (b) shape-detect the AudioGen output (1D / 2D / 3D / wrapped); (c) sanity-check minimum sample count (>= 0.25 sec at 32 kHz) and fall back to silence at requested duration if generation produced obvious garbage; (d) log shape + duration of every render so future regressions are visible.
- **Verify:** next run's `ledger.sfx[].dur_s` should be > 0.5 s for every cue. Disk WAVs in `models/sfx_cache/sfx_*.wav` should be multi-second when ffprobed.

    ```
    sfx_004: wav_path set, dur_s = 0.003125
    sfx_005: wav_path set, dur_s = 0.003125
    sfx_006: wav_path = null,  dur_s = null
    ```
  All five rendered SFX cues are EXACTLY 3.125 ms -- math is `150 / 48000 = 0.003125`, suggesting BatchAudioGenGenerator is producing a constant 150-sample output regardless of the requested duration. The 6th cue ("Ominous rumble") didn't render at all (wav_path null).
- **Cause:** suspected -- BatchAudioGenGenerator has a bug where the sample-count or duration argument is being clamped or replaced by a constant default. Either:
  - (a) the duration request from Director (sfx_plan[i].dur_s) isn't being passed through to AudioGen's generate() call
  - (b) AudioGen is being called with a 150-sample max_new_tokens / num_samples that should be much higher (~3-10 seconds * 48000 = ~150k-480k samples)
  - (c) the generation succeeded at full length but the writeback truncates to a 150-sample header
- **Impact:** "TALKING RADIO" identity loses one of its three voices entirely. Music + ANNOUNCER survive; SFX (dramatic stings, environmental ambience, electrical surge, mechanical hum) all silent. Episodes feel hollow during the gaps.
- **Fix:** TBD -- BatchAudioGenGenerator code audit needed:
  - Find the sample/duration math in nodes/batch_audiogen_generator.py
  - Compare against Director's sfx_plan[i].dur_s values
  - Verify the actual on-disk WAVs (`models/sfx_cache/sfx_*.wav`) are 3ms or longer; if longer, the bug is in the writeback / dur_s stamp; if 3ms, the bug is in the generate call
- **Verify:** TBD -- next clean run's `ledger.sfx[].dur_s` should be > 1.0s for every entry; sox / ffprobe on the on-disk WAV should show multi-second duration.

### BUG-LOCAL-115: TITLE_RESOLVE_FAIL on overnight soak — auto-title-from-spine doesn't reach script_json, widget empty, run dies after ~3 min of writer work [PARTIAL FIX in commit pending: third fallback to news_seed.headline]

- **Date:** 2026-04-30 06:25 | **Phase:** 0-3 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Overnight Gemma-4 E4B soak run #5 crashed at SignalLostVideo with:
    ```
    RuntimeError: TITLE_RESOLVE_FAIL: no usable episode title.
    script_json_title='', widget=''.
    ```
  Log timeline showed:
    ```
    06:14:24 [ScriptWriter] AUTO_TITLE_FROM_SPINE: 'Gait Pattern Fracture' (was: '')
    06:21:02 "episode_title": "Signal Lost: Episode 1"     <- LLMDirector default-looking
    06:25:55 RuntimeError: TITLE_RESOLVE_FAIL              <- script_json title='', widget=''
    ```
  Auto-title computed a real title from the news spine but it never made it into the writer's script_json output as a `{"type":"title", "value":"..."}` token. SignalLostVideo's resolver requires either that token OR a non-stuck widget value; both empty -> hard fail.
  Earlier overnight runs (Crustal Echoes Beneath, Sound of Disintegration, Perception Versus Permanence) succeeded with auto-title intact, so this is intermittent — Gemma-4 E4B sometimes drops the title token in its script_json output even when the spine helper computed one.
- **Cause:** writer's auto-title-from-spine logic stamps the title to a transient variable but the LLM-generated script_json doesn't always carry it through. The LLM may be replacing it with a default ("Signal Lost: Episode 1") that's NOT in `_STUCK_TITLE_DEFAULTS` (the set is missing this prefix variant), so it slips past the writer's own validator.
- **Fix (band-aid for soak):** add a third fallback in `nodes/video_engine.py::SignalLostVideoRenderer.render_video` -- if both script_json title and widget title fail/are stuck, derive from `ledger.meta.news_seed.headline` (always populated by NewsFetcher). Final last-resort: timestamp-based unique title that always passes stuck-check. Run never crashes on title resolution again. Logs WARNING with all three sources so triage is clear.
- **Fix (root cause, deferred):** writer-side -- ensure `{"type":"title"}` token is always prepended to script_json regardless of LLM output behavior. Add stuck-check on the writer side too so "Signal Lost: Episode 1" gets caught before it reaches downstream.
- **Verify:** next overnight run that would have crashed on TITLE_RESOLVE_FAIL now logs WARNING `TITLE FALLBACK to news_seed: ...` and continues to FLUX/HuMo. Episode title becomes the news article headline (truncated to 80 chars).
- **Tags:** writer, auto-title, signal-lost-video, soak, gemma-4-E4B, news-seed-fallback, soak-tailer

### BUG-LOCAL-112: news-history dedup + reset path was a no-op — "All N candidates filtered" branch never restored the unfiltered pool, body-fetch processed 0 candidates [FIXED in commit 61a85b3]

- **Date:** 2026-04-29 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Run `pending_20260429_204318` showed:
    ```
    [NewsFetcher] Filtered 43 previously-used candidate(s) via news_history (0 remaining of 43)
    All candidates filtered out by history -- using the unfiltered pool
    [NewsFetcher] Body-fetching top 0 candidate(s) in parallel...
    [NewsFetcher] Body-fetch complete in 0.00s
    ```
  Pool went to zero; body-fetch saw nothing; writer fell back to no-news mode. News-seeded plot was lost.
- **Cause:** Original implementation had a comment admitting the no-op: *"we just clear used_urls so the LLM-rank step can still operate on the original (unfiltered) pool"* — but `pool` was already mutated in-place by the filter step. The reset cleared `used_urls` but never restored `pool`.
- **Fix:** Stash unfiltered pool before history-dedup filter; restore it on full wipe. Code at `nodes/story_orchestrator.py::_fetch_science_news`. Better to pick a recent repeat than starve the writer with zero context.
- **Verify:** Next run after the fix correctly logged `restoring unfiltered pool` and continued with 43 candidates.
- **Tags:** news-fetcher, history-dedup, reset-path, fallback-logic

### BUG-LOCAL-111: cudaErrorIllegalAddress on next phase after FuturesTimeout — orphan worker thread mutating cached LLM tensors after timeout [FIXED in commit referenced in story_orchestrator.py]

- **Date:** 2026-04-29 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** When NewsSummary or NewsCuration phase exceeded its 60-65s timeout, the orphan worker thread continued running an LLM forward pass on GPU. Python cannot safely terminate threads, and `executor.shutdown(wait=False)` does NOT kill the worker. The cached `_LLM_CACHE` instance thought it was idle, but the orphan was still mutating its tensors. Next phase that called `model.cpu()` or `_load_llm()` collided with the orphan's stale ops and Python aborted with `cudaErrorIllegalAddress`.
- **Cause:** `_run_with_timeout` raised `_LLMTimeout` on FuturesTimeout but did not invalidate the cache. Cached references kept the corrupt model alive across phase boundaries.
- **Fix:** On `FuturesTimeout`, set `_LLM_CACHE["model"] = None`, `["tokenizer"] = None`, etc. Frees our REFERENCES; the orphan worker's tensors get garbage-collected naturally once its frame returns. We don't try to forcibly empty CUDA cache (orphan is still using it; would race the kernel writes). Cost: ~10-15s extra reload time on next phase. Benefit: no crash.
- **Verify:** Subsequent runs that hit the 65s news-curation timeout now load fresh on the next phase instead of crashing.
- **Tags:** llm-cache, timeout-recovery, orphan-thread, cuda-illegal-address

### BUG-LOCAL-110: gemma-2-2b-it CUDA device-side assert during inference on Blackwell sm_120 + bitsandbytes 4-bit NF4 + torch 2.10.0 + CUDA 13.0; same NF4 path is what gemma-2-9b-it would use, so the entire Gemma 2 family is treated as not viable on this hardware [FIXED by removing Gemma 2 family from dropdown]

- **Date:** 2026-04-29 AM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** First edge-case test of gemma-2-2b-it (balanced / 350 / short) failed with `CUDA error: device-side assert triggered` during the OpenClose Spine inference. Console excerpt:
    ```
    Loading LLM model: google/gemma-2-2b-it (quantized=True)
    [StoryOrchestrator] Enabling 4-bit quantization (NF4) for Ultra-Low VRAM
    [StoryOrchestrator] Prompt truncated: 9623 -> 8042 tokens to fit context cap 8192
    [StoryOrchestrator] Starting inference (max_new_tokens=150)...
    [OpenClose] SPINE CHARACTER-DRIVEN failed: CUDA error: device-side assert triggered
    ```
    Model downloaded fresh (was not in cache, took ~4 min via Fetching 2 files). Loaded successfully through bitsandbytes 4-bit NF4. CUDA warmup completed cleanly (0.1s). The assert fires during the first generate() call on a quantized weight kernel.
- **Cause:** Most likely bitsandbytes 4-bit NF4 dequantize kernel does not have proper sm_120 (Blackwell) support on torch 2.10.0 + CUDA 13.0. bitsandbytes has historically lagged on newest GPU architectures; sm_120 is brand-new. Secondary hypotheses (Gemma 2 sliding-window attention boundary at 8042/8192, malformed control-token in truncated prompt) are less likely fingerprints for a device-side assert. Either way, the failure is reproducible on this combination, and gemma-2-9b-it would route through the same NF4 quantization path.
- **Fix:** Remove `google/gemma-2-2b-it` and `google/gemma-2-9b-it` from `model_id` and `cleanup_model_id` dropdowns in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py`, from `_LLM_MODEL_CHOICES` in `visual/llm_selector.py`, and from the test fixture in `tests/test_widget_drift_guard.py`. The `docs/2026-04-29-llm-edge-case-matrix.md` doc records the failure and prunes the Gemma 2 rows. Gemma 4 E2B / E4B are retained as the sole Gemma path -- they are Google's edge-targeted successor family with different kernel paths and have not been disqualified by any test yet.
- **Verify:** (a) Tests: 200 passed Windows-side after dropdown changes (widget_drift_guard 27, two_llm_split 15, dropdown_guardrails 50, test_core 108). (b) Saved workflow `otr_scifi_16gb_full.json` pins Mistral-Nemo only -- no JSON references gemma-2-* so no workflow breaks on load. (c) On the next run with any non-Gemma-2 model, the original lockup pattern should not recur.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **bitsandbytes 4-bit NF4 quantization paths on bleeding-edge GPU architectures (Blackwell sm_120, future Hopper-Next, etc.) require explicit version-pinned validation before being offered in production dropdowns.** Subsidiary rules: (i) "model loads from cache" does not imply "model can generate"; the assert fires during generate(), not during load; (ii) the appropriate failure response is to remove the model from user-facing surfaces, not to wrap the assert in try/except (the assert poisons the CUDA context and the next render in the same process will likely fail too); (iii) when removing a single problematic model, audit the same-family / same-kernel-path siblings for the same failure mode (gemma-2-9b would have used the same NF4 kernel); (iv) backward-compat check: confirm no saved workflow JSON pins the removed model before pruning -- if any does, leave the entry but tag (BROKEN-DO-NOT-USE) instead of removing. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `bnb-nf4-blackwell-incompat`; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-110; tags `bitsandbytes`, `nf4`, `blackwell`, `sm_120`, `cuda-device-side-assert`, `gemma-2`, `bible-candidate`.
- **Tags:** bitsandbytes, nf4-quantization, blackwell, sm_120, cuda-device-side-assert, gemma-2, dropdown-cleanup, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-102: HuMo's intrinsic ~3-6 frame motion-onset freeze produced a constant ~120-240 ms audio-leads-lips perception across every episode; the model anchors its first frame on the reference image and "warms up" before articulation begins, so the first audible phoneme always arrives during the freeze [FIXED by audio pre-pad with leading silence + matching trim before save]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** User reports on Stellar Shadows render: "Overall, the lip sync is behind the voice. The audio consistently arrives first, and the mouth movements catch up slightly after the words begin." Constant offset, not drift -- every line shows the same lag, regardless of position in the episode. Three flagged timestamps (0:34, 0:48, 1:02) all exhibit the same ~150-200 ms gap between audio onset and visible mouth motion. Architecturally HuMo is audio-to-video (lips cannot lead audio in the model itself), so the lag is an intrinsic model property, not a timeline misalignment.
- **Cause:** HuMo (Wan 2.1 + Whisper-conditioned audio cross-attention) holds the reference portrait static for its first ~3-6 output frames before motion onset. The first articulated lip motion lands at frame 3-6 (=120-240 ms at 25 fps). When the per-line audio is fed in starting at sample 0 and the per-line video is rendered starting at frame 0, the first audible phoneme aligns with the static-freeze frames; by the time the model's articulation kicks in, the listener has already heard 100-200 ms of speech.
- **Fix:** Round-robin consult 2026-04-28 PM (Gemini + ChatGPT both audio-engineering literate) converged on Option A: pre-pad each per-line audio with leading digital silence so HuMo burns its motion-onset freeze on the silence rather than the first phoneme. Implementation kept entirely inside `BatchHumoRender.execute()` so VideoComposite and the master audio mix are untouched. New helpers `_pad_audio_silence_lead(audio_dict, pad_samples)` and `_trim_audio_lead(audio_dict, pad_samples)` flank the HuMo render: (1) after slicing the line's audio from the master, prepend `humo_warmup_pad_ms * sr / 1000` zero samples; (2) extend `humo_length` to `humo_length_for_dur(dur_s + pad_s)` so HuMo has frames to render the silence + the dialogue; (3) after VAE decode but before save, drop the leading `pad_frames` from the image tensor and the matching leading silence from the audio. The on-disk clip is therefore the original `dur_s` long and starts at the first articulated motion + first dialogue word, leaving VideoComposite placement math unchanged. New optional widget `humo_warmup_pad_ms` (default 200, min 0, max 500, step 10) -- setting to 0 disables and reverts to pre-BUG-102 behavior. Each clip stamps its `warmup_pad_ms` into the ledger `clips[]` entry for post-mortem traceability. Per ChatGPT's Q3 answer: pure zeros (digital silence) are sufficient; Whisper / HuMo audio encoders treat zeros, low-amplitude noise, and room tone identically for "no speech here" purposes. Per ChatGPT's Q1 answer: silence-pad does NOT cause a second warm-up freeze when speech begins -- the lag is shifted onto the silence, not duplicated.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) Helper round-trip: `_pad_audio_silence_lead` of 48000-sample tensor with 9600 pad -> 57600 samples; `_trim_audio_lead` of 57600 with 9600 pad -> 48000 samples (back to original length, contiguous). (c) 215 passed, 1 skipped, 2 xfailed across OTR core+humo+composite+post_audio+cast_fuzzy + Bug Bible regression. (d) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: runtime log will show `clips[].warmup_pad_ms = 200` in each ledger entry; saved HuMo clips will be exactly `dur_s` long (not `dur_s + 0.2`); user perception of lag should drop from "audio always leads" to "alignment within ~30 ms" across the whole episode.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **audio-to-video models with image-anchored first frames have intrinsic motion-onset latency; pad the audio input with leading silence so the freeze burns down before content arrives, then trim symmetrically before save so downstream timeline math is unchanged.** Subsidiary rules: (i) the pad lives inside the per-line render boundary -- the master audio mix MUST stay byte-identical (CLAUDE.md C7); (ii) the pad amount should match observed lag (~200 ms is HuMo-specific, may differ for other a2v models); (iii) the saved clip should be at the original `dur_s` so timeline placement (clips[].start_s) is portable across pad changes; (iv) add a tunable widget with 0 = disabled so the change is fully reversible; (v) zeros are sufficient -- audio encoders don't distinguish silence-floor textures for "no speech"; (vi) `humo_length` must grow by the pad's frame count (4n+1 ceiling reapplies); (vii) round-robin consult before any audio-path change per CLAUDE.md, even when the change is local to one node. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `audio-to-video-motion-onset-pad`; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-102; tags `humo`, `audio-to-video`, `motion-onset-freeze`, `silence-pad`, `clip-boundary`, `bible-candidate`.
- **Tags:** humo, audio-to-video, motion-onset-freeze, silence-pad, clip-boundary, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-101: Bark `_clean_text_for_bark` translated parentheticals to non-verbal tokens (`(panting)` -> `[pants]`), and Bark's rendered tokens added 200-500 ms of breath/throat audio before the first dialogue word; listener perceived this as garbled words leading the line ("Let's work I guess..." in Stellar Shadows 0:34) [FIXED by dropping all parens]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Stellar Shadows render at 0:34 contained the line "Let's work I guess. We've lost too much time already, Stanley!..." -- the leading "Let's work I guess" did not exist in the script. The actual ledger line l004 was `(panting) We've lost too much time already, Stanley!`. Whisper transcription of the audio at that timestamp showed Bark had rendered the `[pants]` token (which `_clean_text_for_bark` synthesized from `(panting)`) as articulated phonemes that the listener heard as nonsense words. Same pattern at 2:03 (l015 `(grinning)` + l016 `(grabbing the mic)` produced "Um... [clears throat] ... this is... this is... uh...") and 2:48 (l022 `(interrupting)` + l023 `(whispering to himself)` produced "You know, come on... come on...").
- **Cause:** `_clean_text_for_bark` (in both `batch_bark_generator.py` and `scene_sequencer.py`) maintained a `_PAREN_TO_BARK` translation table that mapped `(panting) -> [pants]`, `(laughing) -> [laughs]`, `(sighs) -> [sighs]`, etc. The Bark non-verbal tokens are themselves valid (Bark renders breath, laughter, throat-clears) but at the *start* of a clip they bleed audio milliseconds before the first dialogue word, and Bark's audio decoder occasionally over-articulates the token into something Whisper / a human ear interprets as speech. For an OTR narrative pipeline where dialogue clarity matters more than performance flourish, the cure was worse than the disease.
- **Fix:** Both `_clean_text_for_bark` implementations now drop ALL parenthetical content via `re.sub(r'\([^)]{1,80}\)\s*', '', text)`. The `_PAREN_TO_BARK` table and `_translate_paren` helper are removed. Bark valid tokens written explicitly in `[brackets]` (e.g. `[laughs]`, `[sighs]`) still pass through Step 4's whitelist unchanged. If a writer wants vocal flourish in the output, they write it inline in the dialogue text (`"Hahaha! That's rich."` rather than `"(laughs) That's rich."`).
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) `_clean_text_for_bark('(panting) We have lost too much time')` returns `'We have lost too much time'` -- no `[pants]` token, no `(panting)` text. (c) `test_supported_token_laughs_preserved` still passes (bracketed `[laughs]` survives the whitelist; only paren-form is dropped). (d) `test_scene_sequencer_clean_matches_batcher` still passes (both implementations updated identically). (e) 192/192 OTR core+humo+composite+post_audio+cast_fuzzy tests green; 23/23 Bug Bible regression green.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **non-verbal TTS tokens at clip boundaries leak audio into adjacent dialogue; for narrative pipelines prefer dropping stage directions to translating them to model non-verbal tokens.** Subsidiary rules: (i) parenthetical performance notes are stage-direction shorthand for human actors, not for TTS engines; (ii) if vocal flourish is desired, write it inline in the actual dialogue text where TTS will tokenize it as part of the utterance rather than a clip-boundary side-effect; (iii) two functions with identical bodies in different modules need a contract test (we have `test_scene_sequencer_clean_matches_batcher`); (iv) before adding more model-token translations, measure clip-boundary audio output -- a "clean" lookup table can still produce 200-500 ms of unwanted leading audio. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `tts-paren-clip-boundary-leak`; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-101; tags `bark-tts`, `clip-boundary-audio`, `paren-stage-direction`, `nonverbal-token-leak`, `bible-candidate`.

### BUG-LOCAL-100: Script parser `_parse_script` v1/v2 inline patterns captured third-person stage direction as the dialogue line, sending narrative prose to Bark TTS where it was read aloud as character dialogue (Stellar Shadows: l002 + l003 are `Lev bursts onto catwalk deck...` / `Stanley follows closely...` instead of the actual quoted dialogue) [FIXED via inline-narration heuristic with next-line look-ahead]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Stellar Shadows audio at 0:15 contained "Lev bursts on the catwalk deck connecting habitat pods, hurrying toward Central Operations Center" spoken in Lev's Bark voice. At 0:23 contained "Stanley follows closely, hunched against the swirling red grit. His voice carries thinly over the roar." spoken in Stanley's voice. These are stage directions, not dialogue. Cross-referenced against ledger: `lines[].text` for l002 and l003 contained the narration verbatim. The actual intended dialogue lines ("Got the newest spec results yet?" and "Not encouraging. Shadow overlap keeps pushing our collection window.") were on the LINES BELOW the VOICE tag in the LLM's output, but the parser never reached them.
- **Cause:** The LLMs we use (Mistral-Nemo, Captain-Eris-Violet) sometimes emit production-script-style formatting where a `[VOICE: NAME, traits]` tag on one line is followed by a third-person action line, with the actual dialogue on the line below: `[VOICE: LEV SHAW, traits] Lev bursts onto catwalk deck...` newline `Got the newest spec results yet?`. The `_parse_script` v1 and v2 inline patterns greedily captured everything after the `]` bracket as dialogue, treating the action line as the spoken line and silently dropping the actual dialogue line (which was just plain text without a tag, so no parser variant matched it). The parser HAD a v3 tag-only branch that already does next-line look-ahead, but it only fires when the VOICE tag is alone on the line; the LLM's "tag + action on same line" pattern fell into v1/v2.
- **Fix:** Added `_is_inline_narration(speaker_name, text)` helper in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` near the existing parser regex constants. Heuristic: returns True when the captured "dialogue" starts with the speaker's first word in third-person form (`"Lev "` for `LEV SHAW`, `"Stanley "` for `STANLEY CRANSTON`). Real dialogue almost never starts with the speaker's own name in third person, so false positives are rare. v1 (`voice_inline_pat`) and v2 (`voice_notrait_pat`) match arms now invoke the helper before committing the dialogue. When True, look ahead to the next non-empty line; if it is non-empty, non-bracket-tag, non-scene-marker, treat THAT as the dialogue and consume both source lines (`i = j + 1`). When the look-ahead finds nothing usable, log a warning and fall through to the original behavior (degraded but unchanged). Logged at `info` level on success and `warning` on look-ahead-failure so we can audit production runs.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Windows. (b) Sanity check: `_is_inline_narration('LEV SHAW', 'Lev bursts onto catwalk deck...')` -> True; `_is_inline_narration('LEV SHAW', "We've lost too much time already, Stanley!")` -> False; `_is_inline_narration('STANLEY CRANSTON', 'A hunch?')` -> False; 8/8 helper checks pass. (c) 192/192 OTR core+guardrails+humo+composite+post_audio+cast_fuzzy tests green. (d) 23/23 Bug Bible regression green. (e) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: when LLM emits the tag-then-narration-then-dialogue pattern, runtime log will show `[ScriptParser] BUG-100: rejected inline narration for LEV SHAW, using next-line dialogue`; `lines[].text` should contain the actual quoted dialogue, NOT the stage direction.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **multi-format LLM outputs need parser variants that detect format mismatch heuristically, not just structurally.** When an LLM has multiple ways to emit the same logical structure (tag-inline, tag-then-content-on-next-line, narration-then-dialogue), the parser must check the captured content for "shape mismatch" against the role it was assigned, not just trust the regex match. Subsidiary rules: (i) in screenplay-style outputs, third-person mention of the speaker's own name in their own line is a strong narration signal; (ii) when a parser variant succeeds but the captured value looks wrong, prefer next-line look-ahead with a guarded fallback over hard-rejecting (degraded > absent); (iii) log heuristic decisions at `info` level so production runs are auditable; (iv) keep the helper pure (no side-effects, no I/O) so it can be unit-tested without ComfyUI. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `script-parser-inline-narration-leak`; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-100; tags `script-parser`, `llm-output-shape`, `tts-narration-leak`, `screenplay-format`, `next-line-lookahead`, `bible-candidate`.
- **Tags:** script-parser, llm-output-shape, tts-narration-leak, screenplay-format, next-line-lookahead, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-099: Director JSON parse failure dropped to minimal fallback that LOST all the LLM's actual content (SFX cues, music prompts, voice notes); should retry the LLM as a JSON-repair pass first to recover content [FIXED via three-tier resilience chain]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live first_contact run at 12:07:50 hit Director JSON parse failure (`DIRECTOR: JSON parse failed -- BUG-090 fallback active`). The BUG-090 fallback synthesized a minimal `voice_assignments` from script `[VOICE: NAME]` tags but **discarded all the SFX, music, and per-character voice notes** that Captain-Eris had actually generated in its 770-token output. The "Saving content from a failed parse" gap was real: the LLM had spent 50 seconds producing rich Director content (8 cast voice notes, full sfx_plan with descriptions+generation prompts, music_plan with prompts) and we threw it away as soon as the JSON syntax was malformed. Jeffrey called it: *"so I think we need an llm parse, we need content"*.
- **Cause:** Two-tier resilience (parse → minimal fallback) was the wrong shape. Parse failures usually mean structural malformation (unclosed brace, embedded markdown, JS-style comment leakage), not a model that doesn't understand the task. The LLM's content is sitting RIGHT THERE in the raw output -- we just need to ask the LLM to re-emit it as valid JSON. Skipping that retry step meant Director's actual narrative intent (which SFX cues to fire, what music style for opening/closing, voice characterization notes per character) was lost on every parse-failure run.
- **Fix:** Three-tier resilience chain in `LLMDirector.direct()`: **(1) Parse the LLM's first output directly** -- happiest path, no extra tokens. **(2) BUG-LOCAL-099 LLM-repair retry**: when parse fails, build a strict-JSON repair prompt via new `_build_director_json_repair_prompt(raw_output, script_text)` helper -- the prompt instructs the LLM "you produced this output, it failed parsing, re-emit as VALID JSON ONLY" with the schema spelled out and the failed raw output included as the repair source. Generated with `temperature=0.1` (very low for JSON fidelity), `max_new_tokens=2000`, and using the **cleanup model** (`_effective_cleanup_id`) when set -- typically smaller/faster than the story model and well-suited for structural reformatting. The repair output is parsed via the same `_extract_json` (so it benefits from comment-stripping, brace-repair, etc.). **(3) BUG-LOCAL-090 minimal fallback**: only fires if BOTH the original parse AND the repair pass fail. Builds a minimal voice_assignments from script `[VOICE: NAME]` tags -- the safety net of last resort.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 103/103 OTR core tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: when Director JSON parse fails, runtime log will show `DIRECTOR: BUG-099 JSON-repair retry (preserve content)` followed by either `DIRECTOR: BUG-099 LLM-repair pass recovered content` (success) or `DIRECTOR: BUG-099 repair failed (...) -- BUG-090 minimal fallback active` (both failed). The recovered plan should have non-empty `sfx_plan` + `music_plan` instead of empty arrays, and per-character voice notes preserved from the original LLM output.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **when an LLM produces content but the syntax is malformed, retry the LLM with a structural-repair prompt BEFORE falling back to a content-less default. Don't throw away content because of a brace mismatch.** Subsidiary rules: (i) repair prompts should use very low temperature (0.05-0.15) to maximize JSON-syntax fidelity; (ii) prefer a smaller / cheaper "cleanup" model for repair when one is available -- structural reformatting doesn't need the story model's creativity; (iii) include the failed raw output in the repair prompt so the LLM can see what to fix; (iv) the repair-prompt schema should be spelled out exactly (top-level keys, value types, required vs optional) so the LLM doesn't have to guess; (v) preserve a final minimal-fallback tier so an unrepairable double-failure still ships the run rather than crashing. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `llm-output-three-tier-resilience` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-099; tags `llm-json-repair`, `content-recovery`, `cleanup-model-reuse`, `three-tier-fallback`, `temperature-0.1-for-json`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed the repair-prompt builder a known-bad raw output (like a JSON missing its final closing brace and with a `// comment` inline); assert the prompt contains the schema and the raw excerpt; mock the LLM to return valid JSON; assert the chain returns the parsed dict.
- **Tags:** llm-json-repair, content-recovery, cleanup-model-reuse, three-tier-fallback, temperature-0.1-for-json, director-resilience, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-098: Cast-namespace dedup left dangling `char_id` references in `ledger.lines[]`; merged cast row was removed but lines kept pointing at the dropped char_id, causing Bark voice resolution to fail [FIXED via alias-rewrite step]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Arcadia run live ledger showed cast `c01 ANNOUCNER (typo) / c03 CAPTAIN / c04 HENRY CORBEN / c05 KEVIN STENDAHL / c06 KEVIN VOICEOBER / c07 KEVIN VOICEOVER` — `c02` missing entirely. But `lines[].char_id` still referenced `c02` for the bookend ANNOUNCER lines (`l001`, `l033`). Bark would then fail voice resolution for those lines (no preset under `c02`), routing them to the announcer fallback bus or fallback silence.
- **Cause:** `_consolidate_similar_cast_rows()` ran fuzzy-merge on the cast list (collapsing `ANNOUNCER` + `ANNOUCNER` typo → one entry, etc.) and dropped the loser cast row from the list. The function returned the deduplicated list but had no mechanism to surface a `loser_char_id → winner_char_id` alias map. The caller in `LLMDirector.direct()` then called `led.set_cast(existing_cast)` with the deduped list but never rewrote the obsolete `char_id` references in `ledger.lines[]` / `ledger.sfx[]` / etc.
- **Fix:** New `_consolidate_similar_cast_rows_with_aliases()` returns `(consolidated_list, alias_map)`. The alias map is built incrementally during dedup: each merge appends `loser.char_id → winner.char_id`. After all merges, a transitive flatten pass collapses chained aliases (`A → B → C` becomes `A → C`). The original `_consolidate_similar_cast_rows()` is now a thin compat shim around the new function for back-compat callers. In `LLMDirector.direct()`, the call site was rewritten to use the new function and apply the alias map: walk `ledger.data["lines"]` and `ledger.data["sfx"]`, for each row whose `char_id` is in the alias map, rewrite it to the winner's `char_id`. Logs `BUG-098 cast-merge alias rewrite: N row(s) updated, aliases={...}` so post-mortem can verify which char_ids got remapped.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 153/153 OTR tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: when the cast has fuzzy-merge candidates (LLM typos like ANNOUCNER, prefix-overlaps like LLOYD KAPOOR vs LLOYD), the runtime log will show `DIRECTOR: BUG-098 cast-alias rewrite (N ledger refs updated; aliases={'c02': 'c01', ...})`. Subsequent `ledger.lines[].char_id` should ALL resolve to entries that exist in `ledger.cast`. Bark voice resolution should succeed for every line.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **whenever a dedup or merge step REMOVES a row from a primary table, every referencing table MUST be rewritten to point at the surviving row in the same atomic operation**. The "I'll just dedup the cast and trust nothing references the dropped IDs" pattern is a foot-gun: lines, sfx, music, beats, shots, EVERY downstream consumer is an implicit foreign-key holder. Subsidiary rules: (i) the dedup function should produce the alias map as a return value, not as a side effect on a global — explicit handoff makes the responsibility legible; (ii) chains of aliases must be flattened so any reference resolves in one lookup, not by walking a chain at read time; (iii) log the alias map at INFO level so post-mortem can verify the rewrite touched every reference. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `dedup-with-foreign-key-rewrite` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-098; tags `cast-dedup`, `char_id-alias-map`, `dangling-foreign-keys`, `chained-alias-flatten`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed the consolidator a 3-row cast with `c01 ANNOUNCER, c02 ANNOUCNER, c03 KEVIN`; assert returned tuple is `([c01 ANNOUNCER, c03 KEVIN], {"c02": "c01"})`. Then feed a tmp ledger with `lines[].char_id` values `c01, c02, c03, c02`; apply the alias map; assert all four lines now reference char_ids that exist in the consolidated cast.
- **Tags:** cast-dedup, char_id-alias-map, dangling-foreign-keys, chained-alias-flatten, bark-voice-resolution, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-097: Parser BUG-091 blocklist did not catch `VOICEOVER`, `VOICEOBER` (LLM typo), `NARRATOR`, or `V.O.` / `O.S.` screenplay meta-direction tags; Captain-Eris emitted lines like `[KEVIN VOICEOVER]` that registered as cast members alongside the real `KEVIN STENDAHL`, polluting the cast and inflating Bark voice count [FIXED via blocklist expansion]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Arcadia run cast had 6 entries: real characters (CAPTAIN, HENRY CORBEN, KEVIN STENDAHL) PLUS `KEVIN VOICEOVER` (Male, en_speaker_3, 3 lines) AND `KEVIN VOICEOBER` (LLM typo of VOICEOVER, en_speaker_? 5 lines) AND `ANNOUCNER` (typo for ANNOUNCER). Two of those (VOICEOVER + VOICEOBER) are screenplay meta-direction tags, not characters — Captain-Eris emitted them mid-script as a stylistic flourish ("KEVIN VOICEOVER" = "KEVIN speaking via voice-over"). Director assigned voice presets to them and Bark would render extra "voices" that should have been merged with KEVIN STENDAHL.
- **Cause:** BUG-LOCAL-091's `_SFX_CAST_BLOCKLIST_PATTERNS` filter caught classic SFX cues (`ALARM BLARING`, `CHAMBER FLICKERS`, `BACK AT THE LAB`) and one specific narrator pattern (`MYSTERIOUS VOICE`) but not the screenplay-craft meta tags. `VOICEOVER` is the canonical Hollywood term, `V.O.` is its abbreviation, `O.S.` ("off-screen") is its sibling. Captain-Eris-Violet's instruction-tuning leans into screenplay convention more than Mistral-Nemo, so it surfaces these tags more often. The typo `VOICEOBER` is just an LLM stutter, but it has the same root cause -- the model thinks it's marking a voice-over cue, not naming a new character.
- **Fix:** Extended the BUG-091 blocklist with: `\bVOICEOVER\b`, `\bVOICE\s?OVER\b` (handles "VOICE OVER" with space), `\bVOICEOBER\b` (the LLM typo seen in the Arcadia run), `\bNARRATOR\b`, `\bV\.O\.\b`, `\bO\.S\.\b`, `\bSCREEN\b`, `\bOFF.SCREEN\b`. All are screenplay meta-direction. Filter still routes rejected names to a `type=sfx` ledger entry (preserves the cue for AudioGen) rather than dropping silently.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 153/153 OTR tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue with Captain-Eris-Violet: when LLM emits `[KEVIN VOICEOVER]` style tags, runtime log will show `[ScriptParser] BUG-091: rejected 'KEVIN VOICEOVER' as cast (SFX/stage-direction); routing to sfx`. Cast list stays at the real character count; Bark renders only real characters; Director's voice_assignments target only real characters.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **content-based blocklists for "is this a real character?" must be expanded as new LLM emission patterns surface; treat the blocklist as a regression-test asset that grows over the project lifetime**. Subsidiary rules: (i) screenplay-craft conventions (V.O., O.S., NARRATOR, VOICEOVER, MONTAGE, FLASHBACK) are emitted by instruction-tuned LLMs that lean into screenplay format; add patterns for them upfront; (ii) typos of structural tokens (VOICEOBER for VOICEOVER) are common in long-context generation -- when a real word appears in the blocklist, also add common typo variants if observed in production; (iii) blocklist additions should be commit-bound to a captured user-visible failure (this commit referencing the Arcadia run's polluted cast) so the rule's history is traceable. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `screenplay-meta-direction-blocklist` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-097; tags `parser-blocklist`, `voiceover-tag`, `narrator-tag`, `screenplay-meta`, `llm-typo-tolerance`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed `_looks_like_non_character_cast_name` the strings `KEVIN VOICEOVER`, `JANE V.O.`, `MIKE O.S.`, `THE NARRATOR`, `KEVIN VOICEOBER` (typo); assert all return True.
- **Tags:** parser-blocklist, voiceover-tag, narrator-tag, screenplay-meta, llm-typo-tolerance, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-096: BatchBark never wrote per-line `dur_s` / `start_s` back to ledger; HuMo had to fall back to BUG-094 word-share heuristic instead of using actual Bark playback timings [FIXED via text-match write-back]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** BatchBark generated WAV audio for every dialogue line and computed `dur = len(audio_np) / sr` per line as part of its assembly, but never wrote those durations back to the ledger. Every consumer downstream (BatchHumoRender, VideoComposite, post-mortem) had to fall back to estimation heuristics. After BUG-LOCAL-094 added word-share spreading in BatchHumoRender, HuMo's timings were "directionally correct" but still approximate -- HuMo audio slices for each line were sliced from the assembled audio at heuristic offsets, not at the actual Bark playback positions.
- **Cause:** BatchBark works in script-index (`script_idx`) space, while the ledger works in `line_id` space (l001, l002, ...). The two were never bridged. ANNOUNCER lines complicate the mapping (BatchBark skips them; ledger keeps them), so a naive positional pairing would mis-align.
- **Fix:** End-of-`generate_batch` write-back in `nodes/batch_bark_generator.py`. Auto-discovers the most recent `*_ledger.json` in `output/otr/audio/`, builds a `dialogue_text -> [unmatched ledger indices]` map (handles duplicate texts by consuming one match at a time), then walks `dialogue_items` in script order. For each item it finds the corresponding ledger row by exact text match, stamps `row["dur_s"] = len(audio_np) / sr`, stamps `row["start_s"] = cumulative_start` (running sum of preceding dialogue durs), and writes the ledger atomically. ANNOUNCER lines that are skipped by Bark stay with their original `start_s = null` -- the cumulative offset only counts Bark-rendered dialogue, which is the right baseline for HuMo audio slicing later. Errors logged in batch_log + warning, never crash.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 155/155 OTR tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: after BatchBark exits, `output/otr/audio/<ep>_ledger.json` should have non-null `dur_s` AND `start_s` on every voiced (non-ANNOUNCER) entry of `ledger.lines[]`. BatchHumoRender's plan-build will then read those real values via the BUG-094 priority chain (`ledger.start_s/dur_s > estimated_timings > legacy idx*7`) instead of falling back to the word-share heuristic. HuMo audio slices align with actual Bark playback positions; lip-sync drift across the episode is eliminated.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **when an upstream node knows authoritative per-item state (Bark wav durations) and an existing downstream consumer falls back to a heuristic, the upstream node MUST write the authoritative values back to the source-of-truth ledger before returning -- this auto-upgrades downstream from "approximate" to "exact" with no further code changes**. Subsidiary rules: (i) text-match is a robust bridge between script-position spaces and ledger-id spaces when a stable text field exists on both sides; (ii) for cumulative timings, walk in script order to keep start_s monotone increasing; (iii) skip-list nodes (Bark skipping ANNOUNCER) should leave un-matched ledger rows untouched rather than synthesizing fake values. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `bark-ledger-write-back` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-096; tags `bark-timing-write-back`, `text-match-bridge`, `cumulative-start-s`, `ledger-traceability`, `humo-audio-slice-alignment`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed BatchBark a 3-line script + a tmp ledger seeded with matching text fields; assert post-run all three rows have non-null `dur_s` AND a monotone-increasing `start_s` chain (0, dur1, dur1+dur2).
- **Tags:** bark-timing-write-back, text-match-bridge, cumulative-start-s, ledger-traceability, humo-audio-slice-alignment, bible-candidate

**BUG-LOCAL-096-followup**: BatchFluxRender stamping of `shots[].png_path` deferred. Issue: shot_id doesn't flow into the node (script_json's env_tokens have no `shot_id` field), AND SaveImage downstream owns the actual on-disk filenames (auto-incrementing counter, not shot-id-keyed). Fix path requires either: (a) extending `_parse_env_prompts` to track shot_id from VideoPlan's pass3 output AND adding a parallel disk-save in BatchFluxRender to per-shot canonical filenames; OR (b) adding a new `OTR_LedgerStillsAttach` node that runs AFTER SaveImage and maps the latest N stills to ledger.shots[] positionally. Either is medium-effort; not blocking tonight's run since BUG-088 mtime-cutoff + BUG-091 freshness logic are working in HuMo's portrait selection.

### BUG-LOCAL-095: Audio-side ledger write-back gap; MusicGen + AudioGen produced WAV files but never recorded their paths in ledger.music[] / ledger.sfx[] -- post-mortem and downstream traceability blind to which audio assets shipped [FIXED via in-node write-back]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** After BUG-089 wired ledger write-back from BatchHumoRender + VideoComposite, the audio-side nodes (MusicGen, AudioGen) were still leaving `ledger.music[].wav_path` and `ledger.sfx[].wav_path` as `None` in every completed episode. Jeffrey's architectural commitment ("all images videos and audio need to trace back to the ledger") couldn't be verified from the ledger alone; the only way to find which WAV file backed a given music or SFX cue was to glob the cache directory by hash. Audit also flagged BatchBark missing `lines[].bark_wav_path` and BatchFluxRender missing `shots[].png_path` write-backs, both higher-effort.
- **Cause:** Both audio nodes computed the deterministic `cache_path` for each cue before generation (used for cache lookup) and the actual sample-count after generation (used to build the AUDIO tensor output), but neither wrote either value into the ledger. The Ledger class has `apply_music_timings()` and `apply_sfx_timings()` methods defined explicitly for this purpose -- nobody invoked them.
- **Fix:** Two-piece in-node write-backs, both at the end of `execute()` after all cues are rendered + cached. **(1) `nodes/musicgen_theme.py`**: after the music cues dict is fully populated, find the most recent `*_ledger.json` in `output/otr/audio/`, load it, walk `ledger.music[]` matching by `cue_id` (canonical: opening / closing / interstitial), stamp `wav_path = cue.cache_path` and `dur_s = waveform.shape[-1] / sample_rate` per cue, write atomic. **(2) `nodes/batch_audiogen_generator.py`**: after the batched_waveform is built, same auto-discover logic; iterate `render_queue` positionally against `ledger.sfx[]`, stamp `wav_path = item.cache_path` and `dur_s = clip.shape[2] / AUDIOGEN_SAMPLE_RATE` per cue, write atomic. Both wrapped in try/except so a missing ledger / write failure logs a warning + surfaces in render_log but never crashes the node. Bark wav-path write-back deferred (script_idx → line_id mapping requires careful filtering against ANNOUNCER skip list); Flux shot_id write-back deferred (no shot_id flows into the node currently). Logged as BUG-LOCAL-094-followup.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on both files. (b) 155/155 OTR tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: after MusicGen completes, `output/otr/audio/<ep>_ledger.json` should have non-null `wav_path` on every entry of `ledger.music[]` (3 cues: opening / closing / interstitial); after AudioGen completes, the same ledger should have non-null `wav_path` on every entry of `ledger.sfx[]` (one per script `[SFX:]` tag). Each entry should also have a real `dur_s` value matching the actual generated WAV duration.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **every node that renders a deterministic on-disk artifact -- WAV, PNG, MP4, JSON -- MUST write the path back to the canonical ledger before the node returns**. The Ledger class's `apply_*_timings()` accessors exist for this purpose; "the artifact is on disk and named via cache hash" is not a substitute for "the ledger names the artifact by ID". Subsidiary rules: (i) write-back failure must NEVER fail the node -- artifact is real, metadata is recoverable; log a warning and surface in the report STRING; (ii) auto-discover the latest `*_ledger.json` (including pending) when the node has no ledger-path input; this lets independent nodes write back without graph-wiring changes; (iii) write-back position should be POST-generation but POST-cache-rebuild so cache-hits ALSO get their path stamped (not just freshly-generated cues). Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `audio-ledger-write-back` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-095; tags `ledger-traceability`, `wav-path-write-back`, `auto-discover-ledger`, `cache-hit-stamping`, `audio-side-pipeline`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: simulate a 3-cue MusicGen run against a tmp ledger seeded with the three cue_ids; assert post-run all three rows have non-empty `wav_path` and a positive `dur_s`.
- **Tags:** ledger-traceability, wav-path-write-back, auto-discover-ledger, cache-hit-stamping, audio-side-pipeline, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-094: HuMo per-line timings clumped clips into the first `N * 7s` of the episode because no upstream node wrote actual Bark TTS timings back to the ledger; HuMo coverage stopped early instead of spanning the full episode duration [FIXED via word-share heuristic in BatchHumoRender]

- **Date:** 2026-04-28 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Jeffrey reported HuMo coverage on prior runs ended around 4:37 even when the episode audio was longer. Audit confirmed `lines[i].start_s` and `lines[i].dur_s` were always `null` in completed ledgers; `BatchHumoRender.execute()` plan-builder fell back to `start_s = idx * clip_length` (idx * 7.0s) for every line. With 30 dialogue lines that placed all HuMo clips at 0, 7, 14, ..., 203s -- only the first 3:23 of the episode timeline got HuMo overlay; the back portion was procgen-only. Worse, the per-line audio slice for clip i was sliced from idx*7 to idx*7+7 of the assembled audio, which drifted progressively out of sync with the actual Bark TTS playback positions (Bark dialogue can be 4-12s per line, not a fixed 7s).
- **Cause:** No node in the current pipeline writes per-line timings back to the ledger after Bark TTS or SceneSequencer assembly. The Ledger class has `apply_line_timings()` defined but it's never called. So every downstream consumer of `lines[i].start_s` / `dur_s` reads `null` and falls back to its own heuristic. BatchHumoRender's heuristic was the simplest possible (idx * clip_length) and was wrong on both axes -- temporal placement clumped at front, audio slice misaligned with real dialogue.
- **Fix:** Added a word-share timing estimator in `BatchHumoRender.execute()` section 5.7 (between cast-still binding and plan-build). Algorithm: read `total_episode_dur_s` from the ledger (or fall back to `audio.waveform.shape[-1] / sample_rate` when missing); compute each voiced line's share = `total_episode_dur_s * (line.word_count / sum(all_voiced_words))`; assign `start_s = cumulative_share`, `dur_s = proportional` (still capped to `clip_length` at the per-clip planning stage, BUG-082 VRAM ceiling). Per-line plan now consults this estimate when the ledger has no explicit timings: ledger `start_s` (real, from a future write-back) > `estimated_timings[idx]` (this BUG-094 heuristic) > legacy `idx * clip_length` (last-resort, only if `total_episode_dur_s` missing AND audio tensor unreadable). Result: HuMo clips spread proportionally across the full episode duration, with longer dialogue lines getting more on-screen time than short ones, and audio slices roughly track where each line actually plays in the assembled audio.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 155/155 OTR tests pass. Updated `test_find_portrait_falls_through_to_indexed_pass1` to stagger mtimes explicitly so the BUG-087 mtime-DESC sort is deterministic in tests. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: runtime log will show `[BatchHumoRender] BUG-094 estimated N line timings across XXs episode (avg Y.YYs/line)` where avg matches `total_episode_dur_s / voiced_line_count`. The per-line `start_s` values in the plan should distribute across the full episode duration, not stop at `N * 7s`.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **when an upstream node knows authoritative per-line timings (Bark TTS lengths, SceneSequencer assembly cursor positions) and a downstream node depends on those timings, the upstream node MUST write them back to the canonical source-of-truth ledger before downstream nodes execute -- otherwise downstream falls back to heuristics that are wrong on both temporal placement and content alignment**. Subsidiary rules: (i) when a heuristic is needed at all (because the proper write-back doesn't exist yet), prefer one that scales WITH the available data (word_count, char_count, total duration) over fixed-interval slots that ignore content; (ii) preserve a `start_s` / `dur_s` priority chain so the heuristic auto-disables once the proper write-back lands -- don't bake heuristics into the primary path; (iii) log the heuristic's outputs so post-mortem can spot the drift before users do. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `ledger-timing-write-back` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-094; tags `humo-timing`, `word-share-heuristic`, `ledger-write-back`, `clip-distribution`, `audio-slice-alignment`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed BatchHumoRender a ledger with `total_episode_dur_s=300` and 30 voiced lines (each word_count=20); after plan-build, assert no clip's `start_s > 295` and assert the spread across timeline is roughly even (max start_s - min start_s within 5% of 300).
- **Tags:** humo-timing, word-share-heuristic, ledger-write-back, clip-distribution, audio-slice-alignment, full-episode-coverage, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-092: VideoComposite filter graph blanket-blended procgen on top of canvas+HuMo at `addition`@50%, drowning the composite in pink/magenta and washing out HuMo skin tones [FIXED via procgen-as-base architecture + lighten/0.0 defaults]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Resonance Chamber smoke composite (`output/episodes_for_obs/signal_lost_resonance_chamber_20260427_164718/...mp4`) was visually broken: pillarbox sides showed solid magenta where they should have shown the audio-reactive CRT visualizer; the HuMo character (an old man with grey hair, white lab coat) appeared washed in pink/purple. Jeffrey's diagnosis: "horendously pink". Visible in the screenshot at t=0:20: solid `~(180,30,180)` magenta on left/right pillarbox bars, pink-tinted HuMo character in the center, "SIGNAL LOST" CRT branding barely legible top-left.
- **Cause:** Filter graph architecture was upside-down. `_build_filter_graph` built: (1) a black canvas at 1920x1080, (2) overlaid each HuMo clip on the black canvas at center pillarbox during its time window, then (3) **blanket-blended the procgen video on TOP of canvas+HuMo at `addition`@50%**. With procgen heavily red/magenta-tinted (audio-reactive CRT runs hot during dialogue peaks), the additive top-layer pushed everything pink: pillarbox sides went from black to half-saturation magenta; HuMo skin tones + half-magenta went pinkish-white via additive clamping. The intent of the original design was an "audio-reactive sheen" but with default `addition`@0.5 it became a magenta wash.
- **Fix:** Three-piece in `nodes/video_composite.py`. **(1) Architectural restructure**: `_build_filter_graph` now uses procgen as the BASE layer (full opacity, 1920x1080) and overlays HuMo clips on top at the center pillarbox during each line's time window. The pillarbox sides ALWAYS show full procgen (audio-reactive CRT visible); the center swaps to HuMo only while a character is speaking. The black-canvas-base layer is gone. **(2) Optional sheen pass**: the previous final blanket-blend is now optional. When `blend_opacity > 0` it emits `[last_overlay][procgen]blend=all_mode=BLEND:all_opacity=O[v]` for the additive flash effect; when `blend_opacity == 0` (new default) the output is just `[last_overlay]copy[v]`. **(3) Defaults flipped**: `blend_mode` first option / default is now `lighten` (per Jeffrey's recommendation -- only takes max(procgen, humo) per channel; preserves HuMo skin tones; lets procgen audio-peaks flash through without saturating); `blend_opacity` default is `0.0` (sheen disabled by default; user dials up for the flash). Updated INPUT_TYPES choice list ordering, execute() kwarg defaults, and the workflow JSON widget pins for both `otr_humo_smoke.json` and `otr_scifi_16gb_full.json`.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 155/155 OTR composite + core + humo tests pass. Three test pins updated to match new architecture: `test_filter_graph_includes_all_required_primitives` (drops `color=black` assertion, adds `[procgen]` base assertion + `color=black` NOT in graph); new `test_filter_graph_skips_blend_pass_when_opacity_zero`; renamed `test_default_blend_mode_is_addition` -> `test_default_blend_mode_is_lighten_with_zero_opacity`. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL/smoke queue: pillarbox sides should show the actual audio-reactive CRT visualizer (Signal Lost branding readable, scanlines visible, color shifts with audio), HuMo character should appear in NORMAL skin tones (not pink/magenta), and if the user dials `blend_opacity > 0` the procgen will only flash THROUGH on bright peaks (lighten mode) rather than tinting the whole frame.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **in a multi-layer composite, the layer that should be VISIBLE in the negative-space areas (pillarbox, letterbox, transparent regions) MUST be the BASE, not a blanket overlay**. Subsidiary rules: (i) "additive blend on top of everything" is almost always wrong unless the top layer is intended as a transient highlight pass -- if it's the persistent background, it belongs at the bottom; (ii) blend modes vary wildly in saturation behaviour; default to `lighten` or `screen` (preserve underlying tones) rather than `addition` (tints/saturates) when the user expects "audio-reactive flash" semantics; (iii) any blend pass with a non-zero default opacity should be tested visually with the actual procgen / texture content, not just unit-tested for filter-graph string presence -- a graph that produces correct ffmpeg syntax can still produce a visually broken composite. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `composite-blend-architecture` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-092; tags `ffmpeg-filter-graph`, `blend-mode-default`, `procgen-as-base`, `pillarbox-composite`, `lighten-vs-addition`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: build filter graph with default blend_opacity=0.0; assert `blend=all_mode=` does NOT appear in graph; build with blend_opacity=0.5; assert `blend=all_mode=lighten:all_opacity=0.500` appears.
