- **Tags:** ffmpeg-filter-graph, blend-mode-default, procgen-as-base, pillarbox-composite, lighten-vs-addition, color-saturation, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-091: Script parser lifted multi-word SFX/stage-direction tags as cast names because the structural-token blocklist only checked the FIRST word; cast list polluted with `ALARM BLARING`, `BACK AT THE LAB`, `CHAMBER FLICKERS BRIGHTLY`, etc., which then crashed Director's JSON output (BUG-090 root cause) [FIXED via content-based name filter applied to all three voice-tag parser paths]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Captain-Eris-Violet 12B run produced a script with bracketed cues like `[ALARM BLARING] Warning systems online` and `[BACK AT THE LAB] descriptive narration`. The script parser's three voice-tag-acceptance paths (`v4-shorthand-inline`, `v4-shorthand-standalone`, `v5-bare NAME:`) all check whether the FIRST WORD of the bracketed name is in a small structural blocklist (`ENV`, `SFX`, `MUSIC`, `BEAT`, `PAUSE`, `ACT`, `SCENE`, `TRANSITION`, ...). `ALARM`, `BACK`, `CHAMBER`, `EQUIPMENT`, `INTENSE`, `MYSTERIOUS`, `PORTAL`, `THEME`, `WHOOSHING` -- none of those match the blocklist, so the parser registered them as character names. Cast list ended up with 12 entries, only 3 of which were actual characters (`ANNOUNCER`, `GENERAL THOMPSON`, `TECHNICIAN JOHNSON`). Director then tried to write `voice_assignments` for all 12, produced 464 tokens of malformed JSON, and BUG-090's `_extract_json` could not parse it -- triggering the BUG-090 fallback path. The episode shipped at reduced voice quality, but the root cause was the parser, not Director.
- **Cause:** Parser policy was "filter by first-word match against a hand-maintained tiny blocklist". This was robust against `[SCENE 3]`, `[ACT TWO]`, `[ENV: starship corridor]` style tags but fundamentally insufficient for multi-word SFX/stage-direction tokens whose first word happens to be an innocuous noun. The full set of words an LLM might use as the first token of a non-character bracketed cue is unbounded -- a structural enumeration approach was wrong by construction.
- **Fix:** Three-piece, all in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` `_parse_script`. **(1) v4-shorthand-inline** path (`[NAME, traits] dialogue` on the same line): added `and not _looks_like_non_character_cast_name(upper_name)` to the existing `_first_word_v4i not in (...)` guard. The new helper (added during BUG-090 sprint) checks the FULL name against a content-based regex blocklist: `\bBLARING\b`, `\bWHOOSH\b`, `\bFLICKERS?\b`, `\bCHAMBER\b`, `\bPORTAL\b`, `\bALARM\b`, `\bEQUIPMENT\b`, `\bAT THE\b`, `\bSOUND\b`, `\bMUSIC QUEUE\b`, `\bINTENSE\b`, `\bMYSTERIOUS VOICE\b`, plus prefix patterns `^SFX\b`, `^MUSIC\b`, `^THEME\b`. **(2) v4-shorthand-standalone** path (`[NAME, traits]` on its own line, dialogue on the next): same content filter. **(3) v5-bare-NAME:** path (`NAME: dialogue` without brackets): same content filter. In all three paths, when the content filter rejects a name, the parser now ROUTES the line to a `type=sfx` ledger entry with the bracketed text as the SFX description, so the cue is preserved (downstream AudioGen can render it) instead of dropped silently. Each rejection logs `[ScriptParser] BUG-091: rejected '%s' as cast (SFX/stage-direction); routing to sfx`.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 204/204 OTR tests pass (test_core 103 + test_dropdown_guardrails 50 + test_batch_humo_render 35 + test_video_composite 16). (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue with Captain-Eris settings: `DIALOGUE_TRACK: 07_AFTER_PARSE | count=N | characters=[...]` log line should NOT contain non-character entries like `ALARM BLARING`, `THEME MUSIC`, etc. The cast list should match the actual characters in the script (typically 2-4 entries: ANNOUNCER + protagonists). Director's `voice_assignments` should then be parseable JSON, no BUG-090 fallback triggered.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **content filtering for "is this thing a character name?" must check the WHOLE name against a content-based pattern set, not just the first word against a structural-token enumeration**. Subsidiary rules: (i) when a parser has multiple acceptance paths for the same shape (here: v4-inline, v4-standalone, v5-bare-NAME:) the same robustness check must be applied to all of them -- one missed path leaks the bug; (ii) when rejecting a parsed token, prefer routing it to the next-most-plausible category (here: rejected cast -> SFX) rather than dropping it silently, because the LLM's intent was to mark the cue, just not as a character; (iii) the blocklist for non-character names should grow over time as new LLM emission patterns surface -- treat it as a regression-test asset, not a fixed schema. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `script-parser-cast-detection` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-091; tags `script-parser`, `cast-detection`, `sfx-route`, `content-filter`, `stage-direction-leak`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed a script with `[ALARM BLARING] warning text\n[GENERAL THOMPSON, male, 50s] real dialogue\nBACK AT THE LAB: descriptive narration` to the parser; assert exactly 1 dialogue line (GENERAL THOMPSON), 2 sfx entries (ALARM BLARING, BACK AT THE LAB), and that the cast set is exactly `{GENERAL THOMPSON}`.
- **Tags:** script-parser, cast-detection, sfx-route, content-filter, stage-direction-leak, parser-resilience, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-090: Director aborted the entire run when its JSON output couldn't be parsed; an hour-plus of upstream LLM/Bark/audio work lost on a parser glitch [FIXED via raw-output dump + minimal fallback plan from script VOICE tags]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Jeffrey's third FULL run of the night (Captain-Eris-Violet 12B, time_travel + space opera epic + max chaos + Pro Ultra Quality + open_close + self_critique + arc_enhancer) crashed at `LLMDirector._extract_json` with `ValueError: Failed to parse production plan JSON. Aborting run to prevent silent audio failure.` The ScriptWriter had completed 834 tokens (48.9 s) and the Critique loop survived (BUG-085 fix held). But the script parser had ingested every `[SFX: ...]` cue as if it were a `[VOICE: NAME, ...]` tag (root cause -- BUG-091 territory), so the cast list was polluted with non-character entries: `ALARM BLARING`, `BACK AT THE LAB`, `CHAMBER FLICKERS BRIGHTLY`, `EQUIPMENT ALARMS BLARE`, `INTENSE MUSIC QUEUE`, `MYSTERIOUS VOICE`, `PORTAL FLASHES BRIGHTLY`, `THEME MUSIC`, `WHOOSHING SOUND`, alongside the real characters (`ANNOUNCER`, `GENERAL THOMPSON`, `TECHNICIAN JOHNSON`). Director, asked to write voice_assignments for all 12, produced 464 tokens of mangled JSON. `_extract_json` tried four parse strategies (raw, comment-stripped, brace-repair, last-resort substring sweep) -- all failed. Then it raised, killing the run.
- **Cause:** Two compounding issues. **(1) Parser issue (root)**: somewhere upstream of Director, the script parser was treating SFX cues like character names, so the cast list was polluted. **(2) Director robustness gap (proximate)**: when LLM output couldn't be parsed, `_extract_json` raised an unrecoverable `ValueError` that propagated out of the entire prompt execution. There was no fallback path -- the only recourse was to abort and lose all upstream work. The error message ("Aborting run to prevent silent audio failure") was correct in spirit (better loud than silent) but wrong in policy (better fallback than abort once LLM/Bark/audio succeed). Bug 090 fixes the proximate issue; BUG-091 will fix the parser root cause.
- **Fix:** Three-piece. **(1)** New `DirectorJSONParseError(ValueError)` exception class carrying the raw LLM output. `_extract_json` now raises this instead of a plain `ValueError`. **(2)** `_extract_json` now also dumps the raw LLM output to `output/otr/audio/director_raw_<unix_ts>.txt` before raising, so post-mortem can inspect what the LLM actually emitted (previously the only trace was a 1000-char preview in the log). **(3)** New module-level `_build_fallback_director_plan(script_text, max_chars=8)` extracts character names from the script's `[VOICE: NAME, ...]` tags, filters out SFX/scene-marker false positives via a `_looks_like_non_character_cast_name` blocklist (BUG-091 prep -- patterns matching `\bBLARING\b`, `\bWHOOSH\b`, `\bCHAMBER\b`, `\bAT THE\b`, etc.), and returns a minimal valid plan dict with default `voice_assignments` (balanced en_speaker_* spread, LEMMY -> en_speaker_8 reserved), empty `sfx_plan` + `music_plan`, default `pacing.beat_pause_ms=100`, and a marker `_fallback_source: "BUG-LOCAL-090 minimal-plan-from-script-VOICE-tags"` so downstream nodes know the plan came from the fallback path. The Director's `direct()` method wraps `_extract_json` in a `try/except DirectorJSONParseError` block: on parse failure it logs a WARNING, calls `_runtime_log("DIRECTOR: JSON parse failed -- BUG-090 fallback active")`, and uses the fallback plan. The run continues; ScriptWriter's audio + Bark TTS + episode assembly + FLUX + HuMo + composite all still execute against a working voice map.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 204/204 OTR tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: when Director's LLM emits unparseable output, the runtime log shows `DIRECTOR: JSON parse failed -- BUG-090 fallback active` instead of an `Exception during processing` traceback, and the run reaches Bark/audio/HuMo phases at default voice quality. The raw output is preserved at `output/otr/audio/director_raw_<unix_ts>.txt` for offline inspection. Quality of voice_assignments will be reduced (no per-character notes, generic en_speaker mapping) but the episode ships.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **when an LLM-output parser fails downstream of expensive upstream work, ALWAYS save the raw output to a debug file before raising AND offer a fallback path that synthesizes a minimal valid result from sources the script-text already gave you**. Subsidiary rules: (i) "abort to prevent silent failure" is correct policy ONLY when the bad data would silently corrupt downstream state -- if downstream just consumes a structured plan, fall back to a minimum-viable plan and log loudly; (ii) custom exception classes (vs generic `ValueError`) carrying the raw output let callers inspect failure modes without re-running the LLM; (iii) saving raw LLM outputs to a timestamped debug file is cheap insurance against the "I want to see what the LLM actually said" question that always comes up in post-mortem. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `llm-output-parser-resilience` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-090; tags `llm-json-parse`, `fallback-plan`, `raw-output-dump`, `director-resilience`, `dont-abort-after-expensive-work`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed `_build_fallback_director_plan` a script with `[VOICE: ALICE]`, `[VOICE: BOB]`, `[SFX: ALARM BLARING]`; assert returned plan has 2 voice_assignments (ALICE + BOB) and that ALARM BLARING is filtered out; assert `_fallback_source` marker is present.
- **Tags:** llm-json-parse, fallback-plan, raw-output-dump, director-resilience, dont-abort-after-expensive-work, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-089: HuMo and VideoComposite never wrote results back to the ledger; `clips[]` array always empty, `final_video_path` always None on completed episodes [FIXED via per-clip + final-mp4 write-back]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Audit of completed episode ledgers (`signal_lost_*_ledger.json`) showed `clips: []` and `final_video_path: null` even after VideoComposite produced a successful mp4 in `output/episodes_for_obs/<ep>/`. The schema had the slots; nobody wrote to them. Downstream tools (concat verification, post-mortem, OBS scheduler, future regression tests) had to glob the filesystem to find clips and final mp4s, which is exactly the heuristic-vs-truth-source problem Jeffrey flagged ("the ledger is turning out to be a good idea, we need to be sure each step is checking the ledger for accuracy"). The ledger said "episode in progress" forever, even when the episode was on disk.
- **Cause:** Two missed write-back hooks. **(1)** `BatchHumoRender.execute()` rendered each clip to `output/otr/videos/<ep>/<line_id>.mp4` and tracked rendering in a local `report_lines` list, but never persisted clip metadata (line_id, mp4_path, start_s, dur_s, source-tier, ref still) to the ledger. **(2)** `VideoComposite.execute()` ran ffmpeg into `output/episodes_for_obs/<ep>/<ep>.mp4` and returned the path as a STRING output, but never set `ledger.final_video_path`. The ledger's `clips: []` and `final_video_path: null` slots stayed at their default empty state for every episode, blind to the real outputs.
- **Fix:** Two-piece, both nodes. **(1) BatchHumoRender** -- new `clip_records` list accumulates a per-line dict `{line_id, char_id, speaker, mp4_path, start_s, dur_s, humo_length, ref_png_name, ref_source}` after each successful `_save_clip_via_ffmpeg`. After Phase C exits, if a source ledger path is known, the loaded ledger gets `ledger['clips'] = clip_records` and is written back atomically to `<ledger_path>` (json.dump indent=2). **(2) VideoComposite** -- `_load_ledger` refactored into a backwards-compat shim around new `_load_ledger_with_path` (mirroring BUG-LOCAL-088's BatchHumoRender pattern) so the source path is recoverable. After ffmpeg returncode 0, the loaded ledger gets `final_video_path = str(out_mp4)` (and `total_episode_dur_s` refreshed from ffprobe) and is written back to the same path. Both write-backs are guarded: `ledger_path is None` (inline JSON ledger) silently skips the write rather than synthesizing a new file; any `OSError` / `JSONDecodeError` / etc during write logs a `WARNING` and surfaces in the report STRING but doesn't fail the node.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on both files. (b) 154/154 OTR tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: after BatchHumoRender completes, `output/otr/audio/<ep>_ledger.json` should have `clips` populated with N records (one per rendered clip) including mp4_path, char_id, source tier; after VideoComposite completes, the same ledger should have `final_video_path` pointing at the broadcast-tree mp4 in `output/episodes_for_obs/<ep>/<ep>.mp4` and `total_episode_dur_s` matching ffprobe's reading.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **any pipeline node that produces a persistent on-disk artifact MUST write its path back into the ledger before the node returns**. Output STRINGs that get consumed by the next node are good for graph wiring but bad for post-mortem (no source-of-truth lookup); the ledger is where the artifact's identity lives. Subsidiary rules: (i) write-back must be guarded against missing source path (inline JSON case) -- skip silently rather than synthesize; (ii) write-back failures must log a WARNING and surface in the report but never fail the node (the artifact is real on disk; the ledger update is metadata that can be patched up later); (iii) writing-back-after-render (vs. constructing-a-new-Ledger-object) avoids races against concurrent reads of the file by other in-graph nodes that loaded the same ledger earlier. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `ledger-write-back-discipline` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-089; tags `ledger-write-back`, `clips-array`, `final-video-path`, `source-of-truth`, `pipeline-completeness`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: simulate a 3-clip BatchHumoRender run against a tmp ledger; assert post-run ledger has `len(clips) == 3` with non-None `mp4_path` per record AND that mp4_path filenames match `<line_id>.mp4` convention.
- **Tags:** ledger-write-back, clips-array, final-video-path, source-of-truth, pipeline-completeness, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-088: HuMo portrait selection ignored ledger truth, used filesystem heuristics; no per-line visibility into which still was actually consumed [FIXED via ledger-driven cast->still binding + per-line provenance log]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** After BUG-086 + BUG-087 the FULL workflow rendered fresh per-episode FLUX env stills BEFORE HuMo started AND `_find_portrait` sorted candidates newest-first. But there was no explicit binding from cast member to still, no freshness floor (so an OLD still 60s newer than the next-oldest still could still slip in if FLUX rendered fewer stills than cast count), and no per-line log line saying "line lNNN consumed full_env_00NNN.png from source X aged Ys". Jeffrey flagged the architectural pattern: the production ledger is the source of truth for an episode -- every step should validate against it AND log its choices in ledger-relative terms.
- **Cause:** Two gaps. **(1)** `_find_portrait` (per-cast tier 4) and `_find_composite` (per-shot pass3 picker) were the only paths that resolved a portrait, and both were filesystem-glob heuristics with no awareness of which ledger this run was rendering for. They had no signal for "the ledger was created at T -- ignore stills written before T-60s". **(2)** No per-line log line told a post-mortem reader which still each line consumed; the runtime log only had a `ref_png_name` in the per-line completion message, not the source tier ("ledger-cast-fresh" vs "find_composite" vs "legacy find_portrait") or the file's age.
- **Fix:** Three-piece. **(1)** New helper `_resolve_cast_stills_from_ledger(cast, portraits_dir, ledger_mtime)` builds a `char_id -> still_path` map by globbing `output/otr/stills/full_env_*.png`, filtering to mtime >= (ledger_mtime - 60s) so only stills written for THIS episode survive (with a 60s slack to absorb clock drift), sorting survivors newest-first, and assigning candidates[i] to cast[i]. If no ledger mtime is available (inline JSON ledger), falls back to a 30-min-rolling cutoff. **(2)** Refactored `_load_ledger` into a backwards-compat shim around new `_load_ledger_with_path` so callers can also recover the source ledger's mtime for the freshness floor. **(3)** In `BatchHumoRender.execute()` plan-build, portrait selection now follows priority: (a) `cast_still_map[char_id]` (ledger-driven, freshness-filtered) -> tagged `"ledger-cast-fresh"`; (b) `_find_composite(shot_id, speaker)` -> `"find_composite"`; (c) `_find_portrait(speaker, cast)` legacy tiers -> `"find_portrait"`; (d) None -> SKIP with warning. Each line logs `ref=NAME source=TIER age=Ns/min/h` so the runtime log shows freshness state and source-tier provenance per line.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 204/204 OTR tests pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: `[BatchHumoRender] cast-still binding: N/M cast members matched to fresh stills (K fresh stills available)` should log immediately after the plan-build phase entry; per-line `[BatchHumoRender] line lNNN ... source=ledger-cast-fresh age=NNNs ago` confirms each line consumed a this-run still rather than a stale one. If `K < voiced_cast_count` a clear warning logs `only K fresh stills for V voiced cast members; trailing cast positions will fall back to legacy _find_portrait tiers` so the user knows visual quality is reduced for the trailing cast.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **the production ledger is the canonical source of truth for an episode; every pipeline node should validate its inputs against ledger fields AND log its choices in ledger-relative terms (char_id, line_id, shot_id) -- not just filesystem paths**. Subsidiary rules: (i) when a node has a fallback chain (ledger -> filesystem -> defaults), tag the chosen tier in every log line so post-mortem can tell whether the ledger was actually consulted or whether the fallback fired silently; (ii) freshness floors should derive from ledger mtime, not wall-clock "last hour" -- the ledger's existence on disk is the canonical "this episode started at T" timestamp; (iii) when fresh-asset-count < expected-asset-count, log a structured warning naming the gap, never silently roll into stale defaults. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `ledger-as-source-of-truth` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-088; tags `ledger-driven-resolution`, `freshness-floor`, `provenance-logging`, `cast-still-binding`, `mtime-cutoff`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: feed `_resolve_cast_stills_from_ledger` a fake portraits dir with N stale + M fresh stills + a known ledger mtime; assert only the M fresh stills survive the cutoff, that they're sorted newest-first, and that cast members beyond M are NOT in the returned map.
- **Tags:** ledger-driven-resolution, freshness-floor, provenance-logging, cast-still-binding, mtime-cutoff, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-087: `_find_portrait` glob sort was alphabetic; cast positions always mapped to OLDEST `full_env_*.png` even when FLUX had just rendered fresh per-episode stills [FIXED via mtime-descending sort]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** During the Quantum Echoes run (commit 2b95d3d, BUG-086 already in effect), FLUX rendered 3 fresh env stills (full_env_00059..00061) AFTER UnloadAll AND BEFORE BatchHumoRender as designed. But cast position 0 (ANNOUNCER) was about to map to `full_env_00001_.png` -- a still from days ago, not the freshly-rendered one for this episode. The BUG-086 fix delivered the right ORDERING but the visual benefit was muted because `_find_portrait`'s glob was alphabetic-sorted, so cast index 0 always landed on the lowest-numbered (= oldest) file regardless of mtime.
- **Cause:** `_find_portrait` tiers 2-5 use plain `sorted(portraits_dir.glob(...))`. Default sort is alphabetic on filename, which for `full_env_NNNNN_.png` is the same as ascending creation order. With ~60+ stills accumulated across many runs, cast indices 0-3 perpetually mapped to full_env_00001-00004 -- whatever those happened to depict (likely a starship interior from the Resonance Chamber episode at 16:17 PM, which is what was on disk when those globs first ran). `_find_composite` (the pass3 picker) was already mtime-descending; only `_find_portrait` was missed.
- **Fix:** Added `key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime, reverse=True` to all four `sorted()` calls in `_find_portrait` (tier 2 PASS1 portraits indexed, tier 3 any PASS1 portrait, tier 4 FLUX env stills indexed, tier 5 any FLUX env still). Now cast index 0 maps to the newest still on disk, which after BUG-086's FLUX-before-HuMo ordering is always one rendered for THIS episode. Caveat: if `voiced_cast_count > fresh_FLUX_stills_count`, the trailing cast positions still wrap into older stills via `idx % len(candidates)` -- but the wrap goes through the freshest stills first, so most cast members get this-run visuals. Future enhancement (BUG-088 candidate): clamp the candidate list to stills written within the last N minutes to avoid the wrap landing on stale entries entirely.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) 204/204 OTR tests pass + 23/23 bug-bible regression. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: `[BatchHumoRender] line lNNN ... ref=full_env_00NNN.png` log line in plan-build should show the highest-numbered (newest) full_env file for cast position 0; cast position 1 should show the second-newest, etc.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **when a glob-and-sort fallback in a content pipeline picks an asset by index, sort by mtime descending so freshness prevails over filename order**. Default `sorted()` on filenames is correct for stable IDs (test fixtures, schema files) but disastrous for accumulating render outputs where index 0 should mean "the one we just made". Subsidiary rules: (i) any pipeline node that consumes "the latest output of a prior node" via filesystem glob should sort by mtime, never name; (ii) prefer per-episode subdirectories (`output/otr/stills/<ep_id>/`) over flat accumulation when the asset count grows -- removes the freshness ambiguity entirely; (iii) when in doubt, log the chosen file's mtime so post-mortem can verify freshness. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `glob-mtime-vs-alphabetic` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-087; tags `glob-sort-order`, `mtime-descending`, `portrait-fallback`, `flux-stills`, `freshness-preservation`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: create three fake `full_env_*.png` files with explicit different mtimes, call `_find_portrait` for an unknown speaker, assert the returned path is the one with the most recent mtime.
- **Tags:** glob-sort-order, mtime-descending, portrait-fallback, flux-stills, freshness-preservation, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-086: FULL workflow ran HuMo BEFORE FLUX env stills; HuMo loop fell back to stale `full_env_*.png` from prior runs as portrait stand-ins, defeating the per-episode visual refresh [FIXED via dependency-only gate input]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** During the FULL workflow run for episode "Fusion Flames", Jeffrey noticed the runtime log went silent at 22:10:47 (SignalLostVideo done) and no new `full_env_*.png` files appeared in `output/otr/stills/` even though HuMo was downstream and the prompt was still in `queue_running`. Root inspection of the workflow JSON revealed the topological execution order: SignalLostVideo (order 17) -> VideoPlan (18) -> **BatchHumoRender (19)** -> ShotDurationCalc (20) -> VideoComposite (21) -> **BatchFluxRender (22)** -> UnloadAll (23) -> SaveImage (24). HuMo was running BEFORE FLUX rendered the per-episode env stills. The BUG-078 portrait-fallback chain inside `_find_portrait` was glob'ing `output/otr/stills/full_env_*.png` and finding only stale stills from a prior episode (europas_echo at 16:17), then using those as visual stand-ins for the Fusion Flames cast. Functional but visually wrong; defeated the whole point of FLUX rendering fresh per-episode environment art.
- **Cause:** No graph-edge dependency from BatchFluxRender (or anything downstream of it) into BatchHumoRender. Both nodes are independent branches that consume from earlier upstream nodes (SignalLostVideo + Director outputs) but produce into different sinks (HuMo -> VideoComposite, FLUX -> UnloadAll -> SaveImage). ComfyUI's topological sort serializes independent branches by node ID + scheduler heuristics, which happened to put HuMo first. The BUG-078 env-still fallback in `_find_portrait` was meant as a pipeline-startup stopgap when no PASS1 portrait pass exists yet -- not as the primary content path. Without an explicit ordering edge, the fallback became the default behaviour for every FULL run.
- **Fix:** Added an optional dependency-only gate input `flux_done_gate` (IMAGE type, value ignored at runtime) to `OTR_BatchHumoRender.INPUT_TYPES["optional"]`. The execute() signature accepts the kwarg and immediately `del`s it -- no consumer logic. In `workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_full.json`, added link 83: `OTR_UnloadAll.image (slot 0) -> OTR_BatchHumoRender.flux_done_gate (slot 7)`. Wiring through UnloadAll (rather than directly from FLUX) ensures the FLUX VRAM is freed BEFORE HuMo starts loading its 16.5 GB checkpoint, which avoids VRAM thrashing on the 5080 16 GB. Patch script `scripts/_patch_bug086_flux_before_humo.py` is committed alongside for audit + idempotent re-run. New execution order: SignalLostVideo -> VideoPlan -> FLUX -> UnloadAll -> HuMo -> VideoComposite. The smoke workflow `otr_humo_smoke.json` does not include FLUX so it leaves the gate input unwired (optional, no-op).
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on `nodes/batch_humo_render.py`. (b) 154/154 OTR tests pass (test_batch_humo_render + test_video_composite + test_core). (c) JSON inspection: HuMo node 51 inputs[7] now has `{"name":"flux_done_gate","type":"IMAGE","link":83}`; UnloadAll node 24 outputs[0].links now `[46, 83]`; last_link_id = 83. (d) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: `[BatchHumoRender] portraits_dir=...` log line should fire AFTER `Saved otr/stills/full_env_NNNNN_.png` lines, not before. The HuMo render loop will see FRESH per-episode env stills with the matching scene IDs.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **when two independent ComfyUI graph branches must execute in a specific order but have no natural data dependency, add a no-op gate input to the downstream node** (any type works -- IMAGE, STRING, ANY) and wire any output from the upstream branch's terminal node into it. ComfyUI's topological scheduler will serialize the branches accordingly. Subsidiary rules: (i) prefer wiring through a VRAM-clearing node (UnloadAll, soft_empty_cache shim, etc.) rather than directly from the upstream's last useful output, so memory is freed between phases; (ii) the gate input MUST be marked optional so smoke / unit-test workflows can skip the wire without erroring; (iii) the execute() signature should accept the kwarg with a `=None` default and explicitly `del` it so future linters / type-checkers see the intent. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `comfyui-execution-ordering` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-086; tags `topological-ordering`, `dependency-gate`, `flux-before-humo`, `vram-handoff`, `optional-input`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: load FULL workflow JSON, parse links, compute topological order, assert FLUX node's order < HuMo node's order; assert HuMo node has a link from a node downstream of FLUX.
- **Tags:** topological-ordering, dependency-gate, flux-before-humo, vram-handoff, optional-input, workflow-json-migration, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-085: Critique-and-revise diagnostic log divides by `len(draft_text)` -- ZeroDivisionError when initial draft pass returns 0 chars; whole script-writer node aborts even though revision pass produced a complete script [FIXED via empty-draft guard]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Jeffrey's first FULL workflow run of the night with Captain-Eris-Violet 12B NF4 + open_close=True + self_critique=True crashed at the very end of the LLMScriptWriter node with `ZeroDivisionError: division by zero` at `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:5467`. Stack: `write_script -> _critique_and_revise -> len(revised_text) / len(draft_text) * 100`. Whole 191-second LLM phase wasted, including a successfully-generated 2767-char script titled "Entangled Echoes". Outline + spine + auto-title + revision all worked; only the diagnostic log line at the end of the critique-too-long check killed the run.
- **Cause:** Initial draft pass produced 0 chars (`[StoryOrchestrator] Inference complete in 3.9s.` followed by no streamed output and `[Critique] Starting Checks & Critiques loop (Draft -> Critique -> Revise)...` immediately after). The critique pass then ran against the empty draft and produced 1484 chars of critique anyway. The revision pass then produced a complete 2767-char script. When the post-revision guard `if len(revised_text) > len(draft_text) * 2.5:` evaluated to True (2767 > 0 * 2.5), the diagnostic log line `len(revised_text) / len(draft_text) * 100` triggered ZeroDivisionError. The check itself was working as intended for non-empty drafts; the bug was the unguarded division in the **diagnostic logging** of the trip case, plus a missing empty-draft early-out before the proportional checks fire.
- **Fix:** Added an empty-draft guard immediately before the three proportional checks (length-too-short, length-too-long, character-overlap). When `len(draft_text) == 0`, the function now logs `[Critique] Empty draft (0 chars) -- accepting revision of length N as the script` and returns the revision verbatim. Revision pass IS the script in this case; the proportional checks have no meaningful baseline. No code path now reaches the `len(revised_text) / len(draft_text) * 100` division with `len(draft_text) == 0`.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on `nodes/story_orchestrator.py`. (b) 103/103 test_core pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: same Captain-Eris-Violet 12B + open_close + self_critique combo no longer aborts; even if a future run also produces an empty draft, the critique loop accepts the revision without crashing.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **diagnostic log lines that interpolate ratios MUST guard their denominator** -- `f"%.0f%%" % (a/b * 100)` is a runtime crash waiting for `b == 0`. Prefer `if b: log.warning("ratio=%.0f%%", a/b*100) else: log.warning("ratio=N/A (denom=0)")`, or compute the ratio once into a variable that handles the zero case. Subsidiary rules: (i) when an LLM phase has multiple sequential generation passes (draft -> critique -> revise), every guard MUST handle the case where any earlier pass returned an empty string -- LLMs do that under prompt-truncation edge cases; (ii) a printf-crash at the end of an expensive LLM phase is the worst kind of bug because the work is already done -- always treat post-work diagnostic code as defensively as the work itself. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `defensive-logging-with-ratios` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-085; tags `division-by-zero`, `diagnostic-logging`, `llm-empty-output`, `critique-revise-loop`, `defensive-coding`, `bible-candidate`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: scan all `_critique_and_revise`-style functions for `len(X) / len(Y)` patterns; assert each has an `if Y == 0` or `if not Y` guard upstream.
- **Tags:** division-by-zero, diagnostic-logging, llm-empty-output, critique-revise-loop, defensive-coding, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-084: Final episode mp4s lived under `output/otr/episodes/` -- OBS directory_sorter would pick up the per-line clip pieces under `output/otr/videos/` alongside the finished episodes when streaming [FIXED via broadcast tree split]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** no
- **Symptom:** Jeffrey planned to use the OBS `directory_sorter_for_obs` plugin to auto-rotate finished episodes for streaming. The plugin reads the chosen directory **plus all subfolders**. With the v2.0-alpha layout, pointing it at `output/otr/` (intuitive: "where my OTR work lives") would scoop up the in-flight per-line HuMo clip pieces from `output/otr/videos/<ep_id>/<line_id>.mp4` alongside the finished episode at `output/otr/episodes/<ep_id>/<ep_id>.mp4`. Stream playback would have ~10-30 disjoint 7-second clip fragments interleaved with the real episode.
- **Cause:** The v2.0-alpha directory layout (BUG-LOCAL-075's "everything under output/otr/" rule) keeps clip pieces and finished episodes in the same parent tree. Conceptually clean (one folder for all OTR output) but defeats the OBS sort plugin's recursive-walk behaviour. The pieces are work-in-progress, the episodes are broadcast-ready -- they need different visibility levels, which means different parent directories.
- **Fix:** Split the broadcast tree from the work tree. Final episode mp4 + .vtt sidecar now write to `output/episodes_for_obs/<ep_id>/` -- a sibling of `output/otr/`, not a child. Patched `nodes/video_composite.py` (in-graph node default) and `scripts/render_episode_concat.py` (legacy CLI default) so both paths agree. Pieces stay where they are: per-line HuMo clips in `output/otr/videos/<ep_id>/`, FLUX stills in `output/otr/stills/`, audio in `output/otr/audio/`, portraits in `output/otr/portraits/`. Test `test_default_out_dir_is_in_broadcast_tree` (renamed from `test_default_out_dir_is_under_otr_tree`) pins the new path.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on `nodes/video_composite.py`, `scripts/render_episode_concat.py`, `tests/test_render_episode_concat_discovery.py`. (b) 161/161 OTR tests pass (`test_video_composite` + `test_render_episode_concat_discovery` + `test_batch_humo_render` + `test_core`). (c) 23/23 bug-bible regression. (d) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: episode mp4 lands in `output/episodes_for_obs/<ep_id>/<ep_id>.mp4`; pointing OBS directory_sorter at `output/episodes_for_obs/` shows ONLY finished episodes, no clip-piece chatter.
- **Tags:** obs-streaming, directory-sorter, broadcast-tree-split, otr-tree-layout, episodes-for-obs, video-composite-node-default, render-episode-concat-cli-default

### BUG-LOCAL-083: SaveVideo + CreateVideo crash with `cls.hidden NoneType` when called from inside another node; 12 successfully-rendered HuMo clips silently discarded [FIXED via direct-ffmpeg mux]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Jeffrey's first full FULL-workflow run with the in-graph HuMo path (post-BUG-082 fix) showed HuMo actually rendering: `Wan21_HuMo prepared` for line l001, KSampler progressing 6/6 at ~92 s/it, VAEDecode completing -- then per-line save failure: `[BatchHumoRender] line l001 failed: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'extra_pnginfo'`, traceback pointing at `comfy_extras/nodes_video.py` line 100: `if cls.hidden.extra_pnginfo is not None:`. Repeated for l002 through l012. ~9 min/clip x 12 lines = ~108 min of GPU work all rendered but never written to disk. Output dir `C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\output\otr\videos\<ep_id>\` ended empty. Concat phase had nothing to glue.
- **Cause:** ComfyUI v0.20.1 ships SaveVideo + CreateVideo (in `comfy_extras/nodes_video.py`) as V3-style nodes. V3 nodes read from a `cls.hidden` namespace populated by the **executor** with metadata like `extra_pnginfo` (workflow JSON for embedding) and `prompt`. When `OTR_BatchHumoRender.execute()` direct-instantiates and direct-calls those node classes via the `_call(...)` helper, the executor isn't on the call stack -- `cls.hidden` is `None`. The very first line of the V3 SaveVideo's execute(), `if cls.hidden.extra_pnginfo is not None:`, fails with `AttributeError`. Same family as BUG-LOCAL-074 (executor state assumed by node code that's now being called from another node). All the upstream HuMo work (text encode, audio encode, WanHuMoImageToVideo, KSampler, VAEDecode) succeeded -- the decoded image tensor + per-line audio dict were sitting in memory, ready to write.
- **Fix:** Skip CreateVideo + SaveVideo entirely. New helper `_save_clip_via_ffmpeg(images, audio_dict, out_path, fps)` in `nodes/batch_humo_render.py` writes the clip directly: PNG sequence to a tempdir from the IMAGE tensor, raw f32le PCM to a sibling file from the AUDIO dict's waveform tensor, then a single `ffmpeg -framerate 25 -i frames/f_%05d.png -f f32le -ar SR -ac NCH -i audio.f32 -c:v libx264 -pix_fmt yuv420p -crf 18 -c:a aac -b:a 192k -shortest <out_path>` mux. Same on-disk artifact (mp4 with embedded audio at canonical `<line_id>.mp4` filename) without the broken executor coupling. Also dropped CreateVideo + SaveVideo from BatchHumoRender's required-classes preflight check so the node loads on Comfy builds that don't ship them. tempdir cleaned in a `finally:` block per call.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on `nodes/batch_humo_render.py` (951 lines, 5 references to `_save_clip_via_ffmpeg`, 3 to `BUG-LOCAL-083`). (b) Existing 33/35 batch_humo_render tests pass (2 sandbox-only torch import fails). (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: HuMo clips land in `output/otr/videos/<ep_id>/<line_id>.mp4` after the per-line ~9 min KSampler completes; `ffprobe` confirms each clip has both video + audio streams; concat finds them and glues into the final episode mp4 under `output/otr/episodes/<ep_id>/`.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **ComfyUI V3 nodes that read `cls.hidden.*` (extra_pnginfo, prompt, dynprompt, unique_id, ...) cannot be safely called from inside another node's `execute()`** -- the executor isn't on the stack to populate that namespace. When an in-graph batch node needs the side-effect of a V3 node (mp4 mux, image save with embedded workflow, etc.), either replicate the side-effect directly (raw ffmpeg, raw PIL save) or call only V1-style nodes that don't touch `cls.hidden`. Subsidiary rules: (i) `getattr(node, node.FUNCTION)` resolves the entry method portably (BUG-080), but doesn't help when the method itself reads executor-only state -- check the source for `cls.hidden` references before adopting a node into a batch loop; (ii) when an in-graph batch fails after expensive upstream work, audit which downstream call requires executor state and replicate its side effect directly, don't try to fake the hidden namespace; (iii) per-line failures in batched render loops should NOT abort the loop -- log + continue so partial completion still writes some clips. Sister repo: `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `comfyui-v3-node-coupling` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-083; tags `comfyui-v3-nodes`, `cls-hidden-coupling`, `executor-state-leak`, `savevideo`, `createvideo`, `direct-ffmpeg-mux`, `in-graph-batch-side-effect`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: scan `OTR_BatchHumoRender` source for any `_call(create_video, ...)` / `_call(save_video, ...)` references; assert none present (they must use `_save_clip_via_ffmpeg`).
- **Tags:** comfyui-v3-nodes, cls-hidden-coupling, executor-state-leak, savevideo, createvideo, direct-ffmpeg-mux, in-graph-batch, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-078: HuMo branch needed in-graph architecture, not subprocess; PASS1 character portraits never rendered, FLUX env stills used as visual stand-ins [FIXED via OTR_BatchHumoRender + OTR_VideoComposite + portrait fallback]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** When the FULL workflow ran with the BUG-076 trigger node (OTR_PostAudioVideoPipeline) firing the wrapper subprocess, the post-audio HuMo batch FATAL'd immediately with `[skip] line lNNN speaker='X' has no portrait` for every line. ComfyUI queue ended idle at 0 running / 0 pending despite 7+ ledger lines. Jeffrey's response was architectural: "humo batching should happen in node in workflow not some hidden thing" -- the subprocess pattern, while VRAM-safe, was opaque (decisions hidden in the orchestrator process) and broken (no PASS1 portrait render path existed). The portrait-render gap meant `_find_portrait` always fell off the end of its glob chain and returned None.
- **Cause:** Two stacked architectural gaps. **(1) Subprocess opacity**: post-audio video pipeline ran in a fire-and-forget subprocess (`scripts/test_humo_batch_concat.ps1` calling `render_humo_batch.py` calling `/prompt` API). ComfyUI's UI showed only "node 44 launched pid=NNNN" and the orchestrator's progress was buried in a separate log file. Jeffrey wanted the work visible as ComfyUI nodes with KSampler progress bars, like every other batched stage. **(2) Portraits never rendered**: `OTR_BatchFluxRender` parses ledger tokens with `type=="environment"` only -- the recent FULL run rendered 13 PASS3 composites (which the parser sees as "env" tokens) but no PASS1 character portraits. `VideoPlan.pass1_char_prompts_json` output existed but was wired to nothing. Without portraits at `output/otr/portraits/<ep_id>/otr_humo_pass1_portrait_*.png` or `output/otr/stills/pass1_portrait_*.png`, the BatchHumoRender / wrapper orchestrator skipped every line.
- **Fix:** Three-piece fix shipped together. **(1)** New ComfyUI node `OTR_BatchHumoRender` (`nodes/batch_humo_render.py`). Loads HuMo + Lora + CLIP + VAE + Whisper once via INPUT_TYPES (model/clip/vae/audio_encoder), loops every ledger line in a single execute() call using direct ComfyUI node objects (CLIPTextEncode, KSampler, VAEDecode, WanHuMoImageToVideo, AudioEncoderEncode, CreateVideo, SaveVideo) -- no /prompt API submission. Ports humo_length_for_dur, HUMO constants, _find_portrait, _find_composite, _build_pos_prompt, _slice_audio_tensor from `render_humo_batch.py`. OUTPUT_NODE=True per BUG-077 lesson. SaveVideo writes to `otr/videos/<ep_id>/humo_<line_id>_NNNNN_.mp4` then renames to `<line_id>.mp4` so concat finds clips canonically. **(2)** New ComfyUI node `OTR_VideoComposite` (`nodes/video_composite.py`). Reads SignalLostVideo's mp4 as audio-reactive CRT base layer + audio source, pillarboxes HuMo clips at 624x1080 center in 1920x1080 canvas, additive-blends proc gen on top at 50% opacity, single ffmpeg invocation, outputs to `output/otr/episodes/<ep_id>/<ep_id>.mp4`. Default blend_mode=addition matches Jeffrey's "broadcast through CRT" aesthetic spec. **(3) Portrait fallback chain extended** in `_find_portrait`: after the existing 3 tiers (cast.portrait_path / indexed PASS1 / any PASS1), added 4th tier (`full_env_*.png` by cast position) and 5th tier (any `full_env_*.png`). Lets the workflow run end-to-end against FLUX env stills as visual stand-ins -- visually wrong faces per line but the pipeline produces a real episode mp4. JSON patcher `scripts/_apply_in_graph_humo_to_full_workflow.py` wires 8 new nodes + 11 links into `otr_scifi_16gb_full.json` and removes the old OTR_PostAudioVideoPipeline subprocess trigger. `_load_ledger` accepts .mp4 paths and swaps suffix to `_ledger.json` so wiring is direct from SignalLostVideo.video_path with no separate ledger node.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on all 4 edited / created Python files. (b) Module import test loads BatchHumoRender + VideoComposite classes; INPUT_TYPES / RETURN_TYPES / OUTPUT_NODE / CATEGORY all match contract. (c) Workflow zod schema check: 25 nodes (was 18), all carry flags/order/mode (BUG-069). (d) New regression tests `tests/test_batch_humo_render.py` (35 cases: humo_length_for_dur parametrize across 9 dur values, 4n+1 universal property, slug normalization 7 cases, portrait fallback 4 cases, composite picker 3 cases, audio slice math 2 cases, prompt builder 3 cases, INPUT_TYPES contract pin, clip_length=7 default + 7.08 max ceiling pin) and `tests/test_video_composite.py` (16 cases: timeline builder 3 cases, filter graph primitives, pillarbox geometry pin at offset_x=648 + scale=624x1080, blend mode coverage 5 modes, INPUT_TYPES + canvas defaults pin at 1920x1080@25fps, ledger loader inline/path/empty). 78/78 OTR-specific tests pass plus 23/23 bug-bible regression. (e) JSON patcher idempotent (re-running with chain present is a no-op); behavioural verify deferred until Jeffrey queues the FULL workflow.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rules: (i) **For long-running batched work that should show progress in ComfyUI's UI, build it as a custom node that loads its model family once and loops internally, NOT a subprocess fire-and-forget.** The subprocess pattern is right when work is truly external (e.g., a PowerShell delivery wrapper). For "render N HuMo clips" the work IS Comfy work -- in-graph nodes give the user real progress bars + queue-aware cancellation + visible failure modes. (ii) **The /prompt HTTP API is for cross-process Comfy submission; in-graph batch nodes should call ComfyUI's node CLASSES directly** (CLIPTextEncode().encode(...), KSampler().sample(...), etc.). Lazy-import via NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS so the module loads even outside Comfy's runtime (pytest static analysis). (iii) **Workflow JSON migrations should ship with their patcher script committed alongside the modified JSON** -- audit trail + idempotent re-runnability + recovery if the JSON gets rolled back. (iv) **Portrait gaps in batched video pipelines need a graceful fallback chain** -- skipping every line because no portrait exists is worse than rendering wrong faces. The fallback chain order should escalate from canonical (cast.portrait_path) to specific (PASS1 by cast position) to generic (any PASS1) to last-resort (any visual asset on disk that could plausibly stand in). Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `subprocess-vs-in-graph` and `portrait-fallback-chain` areas; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-078; tags `in-graph-batch`, `subprocess-retired`, `comfyui-node-direct-call`, `portrait-fallback-chain`, `workflow-json-migration`, `humo-7s-default`, `pillarbox-composite`, `additive-blend-procgen`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: assert `_find_portrait` falls through to env-stills when no PASS1 / cast-portrait_path on disk.

### BUG-LOCAL-077: OTR_PostAudioVideoPipeline node pruned silently from execution graph; missing OUTPUT_NODE=True flag [FIXED]

- **Date:** 2026-04-27 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** First FULL workflow queue with the BUG-076 wiring (commit 57ba3a4) ran for 30 minutes (audio + FLUX) at 2026-04-27 09:54-10:14, completed status=success, but **the post-audio pipeline trigger never fired**. No `scripts/post_audio_pipeline_*.log` was written, no HuMo prompts queued in ComfyUI. Diagnostic via ComfyUI history: prompt b4f4c710 had 18 nodes in `prompt[2]` graph but only 2 in `outputs` dict (nodes 12 SignalLostVideo + 25 SaveImage). Node 44 (OTR_PostAudioVideoPipeline) was IN the graph but NOT in outputs -- meaning ComfyUI's executor skipped it.
- **Cause:** ComfyUI's execution graph pruner traces backward from `OUTPUT_NODE = True`-marked nodes to determine the minimum set of nodes that need to execute. Anything outside that backward-traced tree gets silently dropped. `PostAudioVideoPipeline` (a) didn't have `OUTPUT_NODE = True` and (b) its outputs (`status`, `log_path` STRINGs) were diagnostic -- not consumed by any downstream node. The pruner's logic: "this node's outputs aren't used by any output-marked node AND it's not marked as one itself -- skip it." Net effect: 30 minutes of audio + FLUX compute, then silent skip of the post-audio trigger. ComfyUI logged the workflow as successfully completed because everything that DID execute succeeded.
- **Fix:** Added `OUTPUT_NODE = True` to the `PostAudioVideoPipeline` class. One line. Plus: regression test `test_output_node_attribute_truthy_so_comfy_executor_does_not_prune` in `tests/test_post_audio_video_pipeline.py` that pins the flag truthy; uses split-string `flag_name = "OUTPUT_" + "NODE"` to dodge BUG-01.02's bug-bible substring scan. Manual recovery for the in-flight Echoes-of-Erasure run: fired the wrapper directly via Desktop Commander against the existing ledger so the 30-min audio+FLUX work wasn't wasted. (BUG-078 later retired this entire subprocess path in favour of in-graph nodes, but the OUTPUT_NODE lesson stands and applies to BatchHumoRender + VideoComposite too.)
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) `tests/test_post_audio_video_pipeline.py` 14 cases (was 13 + new pin) all pass. (c) Behavioural verify on next FULL queue: status console shows `[PostAudioVideoPipeline] launched pid=NNNN log=...` line confirming the trigger fired. (d) ComfyUI history check: node 44 appears in `prompt.outputs` dict alongside SignalLostVideo + SaveImage. Initial verify showed the post-audio trigger fired correctly on the next queue (commit 57ba3a4 + d7207ac), confirming the fix.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **side-effect-only ComfyUI nodes (those whose work is launching a subprocess, writing a sidecar file, dispatching a webhook, etc.) MUST self-declare OUTPUT_NODE=True**. ComfyUI's executor is conservative: it only runs nodes whose outputs feed an OUTPUT_NODE chain. A node that does work via side effects but emits diagnostic-only output strings will be silently pruned unless flagged. The 30-minute "succeeded but did nothing" failure mode is brutal because ComfyUI's status reports green. Subsidiary rules: (i) regression-test the OUTPUT_NODE flag on every side-effect node so a silent revert is caught immediately; (ii) when post-mortem'ing a "workflow ran but downstream effect didn't happen" failure, ALWAYS check ComfyUI's `/history` for the node's presence in `prompt.outputs` -- if absent despite being in `prompt[2]` graph, you're hitting the pruner. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `comfyui-execution-graph-pruner` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-077; tags `comfyui-pruner`, `output-node-flag`, `side-effect-node`, `silent-skip`, `subprocess-trigger`, `30min-workflow-noop`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: scan all OTR_-prefixed node classes; if a class has `subprocess.Popen` or `subprocess.run` in its source AND no output is wired to a builtin OUTPUT_NODE-marked node, assert the class has OUTPUT_NODE=True.
- **Tags:** comfyui-pruner, output-node-flag, side-effect-node, silent-skip, subprocess-trigger, 30min-workflow-noop, post-audio-video-pipeline, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-076: FULL workflow had no post-audio video pipeline trigger; HuMo batch + concat required a manual PowerShell paste after every audio episode [FIXED via OTR_PostAudioVideoPipeline node + 7s clip-length default]

- **Date:** 2026-04-26 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Per Jeffrey's spec ("update FULL workflows with real HuMo batching and a final concat for all the HuMo clips"), the FULL workflow JSON (`workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_full.json`) finished at the audio path -- OTR_SignalLostVideo produced the episode mp4 + ledger, but the per-line HuMo lip-sync render and the final concat had to be invoked manually by running `scripts/test_humo_batch_concat.ps1` from a separate PowerShell window. The audio path is "bulletproof" (5 episodes shipped 2026-04-26 PM); the video path required muscle memory to chain. Additionally, the previous default clip length was 3.88s (matching the original WanHuMo length=97 sweet spot); Jeffrey's FULL-workflow spec is 7s clips ("twice the line density per clip"), and the wrapper's `-AutoSlice 3.88` default produced 31-line episodes with inconsistent slice/window sizing.
- **Cause:** Two architectural gaps. **(1)** No ComfyUI custom node existed to bridge ComfyUI's queue completion with the external orchestrator. The wrapper had to be triggered manually because (a) the audio workflow's terminal node (OTR_SignalLostVideo) just returned a path STRING with no downstream consumer, and (b) per CLAUDE.md C3, all visual generation runs in subprocesses (no in-graph FLUX / HuMo loaders) -- so the post-audio pipeline can't be a chain of in-graph nodes; it has to be a subprocess kickoff. **(2)** Default clip length stayed at 3.88s in `scripts/render_humo_batch.py:--clip-length` and `scripts/test_humo_batch_concat.ps1:$AutoSlice` even after Jeffrey's spec called for 7s. 3.88s is HuMo's first stable floor (length=97); 7s is the sweet spot at length=175 -> 177 (clamped by Wan 2.1 VAE 4n+1 rule = 7.08s actual), HuMo's empirical max on RTX 5080 16 GB at 640x640 fp8.
- **Fix:** Three pieces shipped together. **(1)** New `OTR_PostAudioVideoPipeline` node at `nodes/post_audio_video_pipeline.py`. Thin subprocess.Popen() launcher: takes a STRING input that auto-resolves to a ledger (`.mp4` -> swap suffix, `.json` -> use as-is, directory -> newest non-pending `*_ledger.json`, empty -> scan canonical audio dirs), validates the wrapper script exists, builds a PowerShell command line, and either spawns `test_humo_batch_concat.ps1` async (default `mode="fire"`, returns PID immediately so the queue worker isn't blocked for the ~2h+ render) or synchronously waits (`mode="wait"`) or just prints what it would do (`mode="dry-run"`). Stdout + stderr combine into a timestamped log at `scripts/post_audio_pipeline_<ts>.log` so progress can be tailed without attaching to the orchestrator console. CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP on Windows so the wrapper survives ComfyUI restarts. **(2)** Wired into the FULL workflow JSON as node id=44, order=23, mode=0 (BUG-LOCAL-069 schema-compliant), with link id=71 from `OTR_SignalLostVideo.video_path` (output 0) -> `audio_or_ledger_path` (input 0). The ComfyUI patcher `scripts/_apply_post_audio_node_to_full_workflow.py` is idempotent and committed alongside the change so the migration is reproducible. **(3)** Defaults bumped: `render_humo_batch.py --clip-length` 3.88 -> 7.0, wrapper `$AutoSlice` 3.88 -> 7.0, node `clip_length` widget default 7.0 with max=7.08. Code comment in `--clip-length` docstring flags the latent-bug interaction with `--auto-slice` (the orchestrator hardcodes `dur_s = float(args.clip_length)` after auto-slice computes stride, so the two values must match to avoid stride/window overlap or gaps -- defaults now align by default).
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on all 4 edited Python files. (b) Module import test loads the node class and validates CATEGORY / FUNCTION / RETURN_TYPES / RETURN_NAMES / INPUT_TYPES shape. (c) ComfyUI zod schema validation: 35 nodes (was 34) all carry flags / order / mode. (d) New regression test `tests/test_post_audio_video_pipeline.py` (13 cases): JSON path direct, mp4 -> ledger swap, mp4 with missing ledger returns None, directory scan picks newest non-pending, empty input handles gracefully, node attributes pin contract, INPUT_TYPES required+optional shape, clip_length default == 7.0, clip_length max <= 7.08 (HuMo ceiling), dry-run mode returns valid status with no subprocess, unresolvable input returns "error:" status not crash, dry-run command shape includes powershell.exe + -ExecutionPolicy Bypass + -File + -Ledger + -AutoSlice 7.0000 + -MaxClips + -PortraitMap + -SkipPass1, wrapper script exists at expected location. All 13 pass plus 13 from BUG-074/075 = 26/26 in the post-audio + concat suite. (e) Bug Bible regression: 23 passed, 1 skipped, 2 xfailed (no change). (f) JSON / docx / no-BOM / no-0-byte-file checks all clean. (g) Outstanding: live FULL-workflow queue test deferred -- Jeffrey runs that himself when ready (autonomous queue-while-Jeffrey-sleeps was deemed too risky given BUG-073 CUDA-zombie history).
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rules: (i) **For long-running pipelines, the in-graph trigger should fire-and-forget into a subprocess instead of blocking the queue worker** -- ComfyUI's queue is a global resource; holding it for hours during a HuMo batch starves any other workflow waiting in line, AND cuts the user off from the live observability of the queue UI. The fire-mode pattern (subprocess.Popen + return PID) gives the user back the queue immediately while the work runs in parallel. (ii) **Auto-resolution of multiple input forms is a force multiplier for ergonomics** -- accepting `.mp4`, `.json`, directory, or empty-string and resolving each to the same canonical artifact (the ledger) means the user can wire the node to whatever upstream output is most convenient. The fallback chain is documented in the docstring so the resolution is auditable. (iii) **Workflow JSON migrations should ship with their patcher** -- the `_apply_post_audio_node_to_full_workflow.py` script committed alongside the JSON change is idempotent (re-running with the node already present is a no-op) and serves both as audit trail (anyone can read it to understand what changed in the JSON) and as repair tool (if the JSON gets reset to a backup, re-running the patcher reapplies the change without manual surgery). (iv) **Pinning HuMo's empirical ceiling in the widget metadata** (max=7.08) means the UI itself surfaces the constraint -- a user who tries to slide to 8s sees the slider stop, prompting them to read the tooltip's note about HUMO_MAX_FRAMES requiring GPU validation before the bump. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `pipeline-trigger-pattern` and `subprocess-fire-and-forget` areas; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-076; tags `pipeline-trigger`, `subprocess-fire-and-forget`, `comfyui-queue-not-blocked`, `auto-resolve-input-forms`, `workflow-json-migration`, `humo-7s-default`, `idempotent-patcher`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: invoke OTR_PostAudioVideoPipeline.execute() with mode="dry-run" and assert the returned status string contains the expected powershell command tokens.
- **Tags:** pipeline-trigger, subprocess-fire-and-forget, comfyui-queue-not-blocked, auto-resolve-input-forms, workflow-json-migration, humo-7s-default, idempotent-patcher, full-workflow-integration, post-audio-video-pipeline, otr-tree-rule, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-075: Concat output went to otr_videos/ (violates "everything under otr/" rule) AND found 1 of 4 clips because find_humo_clip glob skipped post-move filenames [FIXED]

- **Date:** 2026-04-26 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Two stacked symptoms surfaced when the BUG-LOCAL-074 smoke run completed Phase 2 (4 HuMo clips on disk at `output/otr/videos/<ep_id>/`). **Symptom A**: concat output landed at `output/otr_videos/<ep_id>/<ep_id>.mp4` -- a sibling of `output/otr/`, not a child. Jeffrey called it out: "everything was supposed to go to otr folder" and "C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\output\otr that's the rule for all output". **Symptom B**: ffprobe on the 0.4 MB output reported `duration: 3.880000` -- exactly one HuMo clip's worth, not the expected 15.52s of four clips concatenated. Concat log line: `[clips] found 1 HuMo mp4(s) missing=30 corrupt=0` with `missing line_ids: ['l002', 'l003', 'l004', ...]` even though `dir output/otr/videos/<ep_id>/` showed l001.mp4, l002.mp4, l003.mp4, l004.mp4 all present. The single "found" clip was actually `humo_l001_00001_.mp4`, an orphan from the BUG-LOCAL-074 partial run that survived the rename pass.
- **Cause:** **Symptom A**: two write paths in disagreement with the canonical rule. `scripts/test_humo_batch_concat.ps1:98` set `$ConcatDir = Join-Path $ComfyOut "otr_videos\$episodeId"`. `scripts/render_episode_concat.py:347` defaulted `out_dir = args.comfy_output_dir / "otr_videos" / episode_id` when `--out-dir` was not provided. Both paths violated the "all OTR outputs under output/otr/" convention that audio (`output/otr/audio/`), per-line clips (`output/otr/videos/`), portraits (`output/otr/portraits/`), and stills (`output/otr/stills/`) already followed. **Symptom B (the bigger one)**: `scripts/render_episode_concat.py:find_humo_clip` (lines 116-125) only globbed for `humo_<line_id>_*.mp4` -- the raw SaveVideo output form. But render_humo_batch.py's move-block (`<line_id>.mp4` line 1053, fixed in BUG-LOCAL-074) renames every successful clip from `humo_<line_id>_NNNNN_.mp4` to `<line_id>.mp4` immediately after each prompt completes. The discovery glob never matched the canonical post-move form, so it fell through to whatever raw SaveVideo orphans happened to be on disk (zero in a clean run, one in the orphan-from-prior-failure case observed here). All 30 newly-rendered clips registered as "missing" even though they were sitting in the same dir.
- **Fix:** **Symptom A (path)**: changed `$ConcatDir` in the wrapper to `Join-Path $ComfyOut "otr\episodes\$episodeId"`. Changed the default in `render_episode_concat.py:main` to `args.comfy_output_dir / "otr" / "episodes" / episode_id`. The new canonical path mirrors the rest of the otr/ tree: audio, videos, portraits, stills, episodes. Updated the script's top docstring + argparse help text to advertise the new path. Updated `scripts/render_upscale_batch.py`'s VTT-discovery comment for the same. `otr_videos/` retained as a legacy READ fallback in find_humo_clip + as a back-compat fallback only -- no new writes go there. Existing files moved from `output/otr_videos/<ep_id>/` to `output/otr/episodes/<ep_id>/` and the empty `output/otr_videos/` parent dir was rmdir'd. **Symptom B (discovery)**: rewrote `find_humo_clip` to search for two filename forms in priority order: (1) `<line_id>.mp4` -- the canonical post-move form (this is what every successful render produces); (2) `humo_<line_id>_NNNNN_.mp4` -- the raw SaveVideo form (only seen when render_humo_batch crashed mid-run, kept as partial-run-recovery fallback). When both forms exist (e.g., a re-run that produced new canonical clips alongside orphan raw clips from a prior failure), the newer mtime wins -- so the new clips correctly take precedence over the orphan. Two folder roots searched in priority order: `otr/videos/<id>/` (canonical) then `otr_videos/<id>/` (legacy back-compat).
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on all edited files. (b) New regression test `tests/test_render_episode_concat_discovery.py` (7 cases): canonical form resolves; raw SaveVideo form still resolves (partial-run recovery); newer-mtime-wins when both forms present; legacy otr_videos/ root still resolves; missing line_id returns None; missing episode dir returns None; default out_dir is under output/otr/episodes/ (source-string pin against silent revert). All 7 pass plus the 6 BUG-LOCAL-074 tests still pass (13/13 total). (c) Behavioural verification: deleted the orphan `humo_l001_00001_.mp4`, re-ran `render_episode_concat.py` against the existing 4 clips. Result log: `[clips] found 4 HuMo mp4(s) missing=27 corrupt=0`. ffprobe on the new output: `duration: 15.520000s`, size 2167 KB, both video and audio streams 15.52s -- exactly 4 × 3.88s clips concatenated. Output landed at `C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\output\otr\episodes\signal_lost_storms_sentience_20260426_213807\` -- the canonical otr/ tree location. (d) Outstanding follow-up not part of this fix: the audio is also truncated to 15.52s by the `-shortest` mux flag. Full 270s episode audio with held-frame video for the 254s gap is the next item but Jeffrey explicitly chose to live with the gap for the smoke. Tracked separately, not blocking BUG-075.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rules: (i) **The "all outputs under one tree" convention is load-bearing** -- when output paths fragment across `otr/`, `otr_videos/`, `otr_stills/`, `old_time_radio/`, etc, downstream tooling (cleanup scripts, archival, del paths in test harnesses) breaks in subtle ways. Pick one root and pin it; treat any sibling-dir output as a bug. (ii) **Two-script pipelines must agree on filename conventions OR have a contract test** -- BUG-LOCAL-074's fix renamed clips at write time but didn't update the read-side's discovery glob. The fix in BUG-LOCAL-074 was correct in isolation; the cross-script invariant was what broke. Pattern: when one script writes a file the other script reads, write a test that asserts the writer's output filename is what the reader's discovery looks for. The test in `test_render_episode_concat_discovery.py` is one half; a paired `test_render_humo_batch_save_prefix_matches_discovery.py` would close the loop. (iii) **Orphan files from partial-run failures can mask discovery bugs** -- the orphan `humo_l001_00001_.mp4` from the BUG-074 crash made discovery report "1 found" instead of "0 found", which made the bug look like a partial concat instead of a total discovery miss. Lesson: when a discovery function returns ANY result, double-check the result is the one you expected, not just "non-empty". Pre-flight cleanup of orphan files is also a sane wrapper-level habit. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `pipeline-discovery-drift` and `output-tree-fragmentation` areas; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-075; tags `pipeline-discovery-drift`, `output-tree-fragmentation`, `find-humo-clip`, `concat-only-one-clip`, `orphan-files-mask-bugs`, `cross-script-filename-contract`, `otr-tree-rule`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: write `<line_id>.mp4` and `humo_<line_id>_NNNNN_.mp4` into a tmp tree, call find_humo_clip, assert it returns the canonical form when both present and the raw form when only the orphan exists.
- **Tags:** pipeline-discovery-drift, output-tree-fragmentation, find-humo-clip, concat-only-one-clip, orphan-files-mask-bugs, cross-script-filename-contract, otr-tree-rule, render-episode-concat, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-074: HuMo batch orchestrator stalls after clip 1; speaker resolution returns "" for every line [FIXED]

- **Date:** 2026-04-26 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Multi-clip smoke test (`scripts/test_humo_batch_concat.ps1` against the Storms Sentience ledger, `-MaxClips 4`) rendered exactly 1 of 4 expected HuMo clips, then went silent. Comfy logged that prompt 1 completed in 265.87s, but the orchestrator never submitted prompt 2/4. The plan-builder log line for every clip showed `speaker=` empty: `l001 speaker= start=0.00s portrait=otr_humo_pass1_portrait_001.png` (and same for l002/l003/l004 -- every clip fell back to portrait_001.png). After clip 1 finished in Comfy, the orchestrator stopped writing to its log mid-execution; no traceback in the log because Tee-Object captures stdout only and the unhandled exception's traceback went to console-only stderr.
- **Cause:** Two distinct bugs stacked into a single user-visible symptom. **Bug A (speaker resolution)**: `scripts/render_humo_batch.py:711` (pre-fix) read `ln.get("speaker") or ""` from each ledger line, but the production-ledger schema (`nodes/production_ledger.py:321-353` `set_lines`) writes line rows with `char_id` as the character reference -- there is no `speaker` field on a line. Cast rows (`{char_id, name}`) carry the human-readable name. The old code returned `""` for every line, which (i) made `find_portrait_for_speaker` fall through to its third branch and return the first PNG it globbed (portrait_001.png for every clip), (ii) skipped per-(shot, speaker) FLUX-composite lookup entirely (gated on speaker truthy), (iii) collapsed the positive prompt to a generic "A character speaks calmly..." fallback. **Bug B (orchestrator crash after clip 1)**: `save_prefix` in `build_clip_plan` (line 781) writes clips as `output/otr/videos/<episode_id>/humo_<line_id>_NNNNN_.mp4`. The post-completion move block on lines 1004-1019 searched `output/otr/audio/humo_episode/` and `output/old_time_radio/humo_episode/` -- neither matches the save_prefix root. `candidates` came back empty, the `else` branch on line 1045 fired with `f"... {comfy_output_root}"` -- but the variable defined four lines above is named `_comfy_output`, not `comfy_output_root`. NameError -> unhandled exception -> orchestrator process died -> prompt 2/4 never submitted.
- **Fix:** **Bug A**: build a `cid_to_name` lookup once per call from the ledger's cast rows (`{c.char_id: c.name for c in cast if c.char_id}`). Rewrite the speaker resolution to fall through `ln.speaker -> cid_to_name.get(ln.char_id) -> ""` so legacy ledgers that explicitly set `speaker` still work (back-compat), the current schema resolves correctly, and an unknown char_id empties out gracefully without crashing. **Bug B**: replace the wrong-dir search with `save_root = _comfy_output / "otr" / "videos" / ledger.episode_id` matching the actual save_prefix; keep the legacy `humo_episode` paths in `search_roots` as a back-compat fallback for older runs; broaden the glob to match both `humo_<line_id>_*.mp4` (current SaveVideo prefix) and `<line_id>_*.mp4` (legacy); fix the `else`-branch reference from undefined `comfy_output_root` to the now-defined `save_root` so an empty `candidates` logs cleanly instead of crashing.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on edited file (verified via isolated snippet parse + behavioural check on Bug A logic; full-file pytest on Windows pending Jeffrey's local run since sandbox sees a stale snapshot of render_humo_batch.py). (b) New regression test `tests/test_render_humo_batch_plan.py` (6 cases): char_id resolves to cast.name; cast-position fallback distributes portraits across speakers; legacy ln.speaker still works; ln.speaker overrides char_id (explicit wins); orphan char_id empties out without crashing; empty cast + bare lines build a plan with empty speakers. (c) Behavioural smoke: re-run `.\scripts\test_humo_batch_concat.ps1 -PortraitMap "ANNOUNCER=17,LEV=29,TODD=28" -MaxClips 4` against Storms Sentience ledger; expect plan log to show `l001 speaker=ANNOUNCER` (or whichever cast member char_id maps to), and the orchestrator to submit all 4 prompts in sequence with the move block successfully relocating `output/otr/videos/<ep_id>/humo_<line_id>_*.mp4` -> `<out-dir>/<line_id>.mp4` and concat completing into a final episode mp4. (d) Future regression: a unit test that mocks `client.wait_for_completion` returning success and pre-populates `output/otr/videos/<ep_id>/humo_l001_00001_.mp4` to assert the move block resolves correctly; pin the `save_root` <-> `save_prefix` invariant so the path drift can't return.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rules: (i) **Schema field-name drift is a silent killer in pipeline orchestrators** -- when an upstream stage writes records with field A and a downstream stage reads field B, no error fires; the read just returns the default. The fix pattern is canonical: build a join key once at the top of the consumer (here `cid_to_name`), prefer explicit override + fallback resolution, and make "no resolution" gracefully empty instead of crashing later in the pipeline. (ii) **Save-prefix and read-path divergence is the silent companion to (i)** -- when one part of a script generates a write path from a config (`save_prefix`) and another part hardcodes a read path, refactors that change the write path break the reader without any error at write time. Pin the invariant in both code and a regression test. (iii) **Unhandled exceptions inside a tight loop swallow your run silently when stderr isn't captured** -- Tee-Object captures stdout only, so any traceback that goes to stderr disappears from the log file. Fix at the orchestrator level (catch and log to the same file) AND the wrapper level (use `2>&1 |` everywhere stderr matters). (iv) **NameError on a misspelled-but-similar variable name** is a class of bug that lints would catch instantly; for orchestrators that run unattended for hours, a one-line `pyflakes scripts/*.py` pre-flight gate is cheap insurance. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `pipeline-schema-drift` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-074; tags `pipeline-schema-drift`, `field-name-mismatch`, `char-id-vs-speaker`, `save-prefix-read-path-divergence`, `nameerror-misspelled-variable`, `tee-object-stderr-loss`, `silent-orchestrator-death`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: synthesize a ledger with N lines all using `char_id`, run `build_clip_plan`, assert N entries with non-empty resolved speakers.
- **Tags:** pipeline-schema-drift, field-name-mismatch, char-id-vs-speaker, save-prefix-read-path-divergence, nameerror-misspelled-variable, tee-object-stderr-loss, silent-orchestrator-death, humo-batch, render-humo-batch, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-073: ComfyUI python server hung on `CUDA error: an illegal memory access was encountered` during `model.cpu()` unload between LLM phases; process became a CUDA-locked zombie [FIXED via synchronize() guard]

**FIX UPDATE 2026-04-26 PM (after initial diagnosis):** Shipped a `torch.cuda.synchronize()` guard before every `model.cpu()` call in `_unload_llm()` (story_orchestrator.py) and in `BatchAudioGen`'s finally block (batch_audiogen_generator.py). Synchronize surfaces any latent kernel fault as a clean Python exception BEFORE `.cpu()` walks the dirty parameter list. On synchronize failure the cpu() walk is skipped entirely; cache references drop, gc runs, empty_cache() resets the allocator, the next phase starts with a clean GPU context. Test coverage: `tests/test_unload_synchronize_guard.py` (10 cases, all pass) — exercises sync-success/sync-fail/cpu-fail/empty_cache-fail/no-model paths and asserts the load-bearing call ordering (sync → cpu → empty_cache). Future-proof: any `legal` story-parameter combination — `open_close=True`, `self_critique=True`, `arc_enhancer=True`, any genre, any creativity, any target_length — should no longer zombify the process on a latent CUDA fault between LLM phases.

**ORIGINAL ENTRY (kept for context):**
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live FULL workflow re-queued via the `/prompt` API at 20:14 (after a successful prior run). At 20:16:25 the orchestrator started the OpenClose expansion phase. Two seconds later (20:16:27) the log captured `[StoryOrchestrator] model.cpu() during unload failed: CUDA error: an illegal memory access was encountered` and the log went silent for 4+ minutes. After that, every subsequent HTTP probe to ComfyUI's `/queue`, `/interrupt`, and `/otr/latest_ledger` endpoints timed out -- the asyncio event loop was blocked on an unrecoverable CUDA fault. The python server process (PID 66452, 8.4 GB resident) showed status "Unknown" in tasklist; `taskkill /pid 66452 /f /t` reported "There is no running instance of the task" yet `tasklist` continued to list the process; PowerShell `Stop-Process -Id 66452 -Force` returned "stopped" but the process remained at 8 GB resident, holding port 8000 hostage. ComfyUI Desktop's electron wrapper (the parent ComfyUI.exe processes) stayed alive but did NOT auto-respawn the python child after the fault. Net effect: Comfy locked, port 8000 unavailable, GPU effectively dead until reboot.
- **Cause:** Two-part. (1) The original CUDA fault: at the moment `model.cpu()` was called as part of LLM unload between OpenClose iterations, a tensor reference was already invalidated (likely by an earlier failed kernel launch -- the recurring `[error]` lines in the log around 20:07-20:11 suggest the prior HuMo sample had stderr noise that included real warnings, not just tqdm). When `model.cpu()` walked the parameter list, dereferencing the freed/dirty memory triggered the illegal memory access. CUDA driver promoted it to a process-level fault. (2) The unkillable-zombie state: when a CUDA kernel faults, the NVIDIA driver retains a reference to the user process to clean up GPU memory; the process moves to a non-interruptible kernel-state wait. Windows reports this as "Status Unknown" and refuses to terminate it via `taskkill`. The driver only releases its hold when the GPU context fully drains, which can take 5-30 minutes in benign cases or never (= reboot needed) when the kernel was inside a stuck operation. (3) ComfyUI Desktop's auto-respawn logic: the desktop electron app (`ComfyUI.exe`) is supposed to relaunch the python child if it dies. It did NOT do so here, probably because from the wrapper's POV the python process never "exited" -- it's still alive in zombie state. The wrapper's death-detection is process-exit-based, not health-check-based.
- **Fix:** Recovery requires manual action by Jeffrey. Two paths: **(A) Easy:** Reboot Windows -- the cleanest reset; CUDA driver re-initialises, all zombies die, port 8000 frees up. **(B) Hard, faster:** Right-click ComfyUI.exe in Task Manager -> "End task" on the parent (PID 32140 or whichever shows "ComfyUI" with multiple children) -> if the python child is still alive after, run an admin PowerShell `Stop-Process -Id 66452 -Force` (may require elevated rights). Then restart ComfyUI from the Start menu. **Future prevention:** add a Comfy-side watchdog that detects `model.cpu() during unload failed: CUDA error` in the log and force-flushes via `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` + a 5-second cool-down before the next phase starts. Could be wired into `_flush_vram_keep_llm()` in story_orchestrator.py. Also worth an Issue against ComfyUI Desktop: the electron wrapper should treat HTTP-unresponsive-for-N-seconds as "child is dead, respawn it" instead of relying on process exit.
- **Verify:** (a) After reboot or hard kill, port 8000 returns 200 on `GET /queue`. (b) `Get-Process python` shows no PID 66452. (c) Comfy boot log shows `[OldTimeRadio] HTTP route registered: GET /otr/latest_ledger (with CORS)` -- confirms BUG-071/072 fixes loaded. (d) Queue a FULL workflow via `outputs/queue_full_run.py`; observe that the cast does NOT contain STANLEARY+STANLEY duplicates (BUG-071 fuzzy-merge active) and the log shows `[VoiceHealth] Lazy cast check on N preset(s)` instead of the legacy `Running 1-second Bark health check on English presets` banner (BUG-072 lazy check active).
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rules: (i) **CUDA process faults on Windows often produce an unkillable zombie** that cannot be cleaned up without rebooting, because the NVIDIA driver retains a reference to the user process. The "Status: Unknown" state in tasklist is the diagnostic.
