Plan around this: (a) any orchestrator that calls `model.cpu()` near a known kernel-fault risk should wrap it in try/except with `torch.cuda.synchronize()` BEFORE the cpu() call to surface latent faults early; (b) the orchestrator should periodically write a heartbeat file to disk so a separate watchdog process can detect a dead inner loop without HTTP probing the ComfyUI server (which uses the same dead asyncio loop). (ii) **Electron-wrapper child-process death detection should be HTTP-health-based, not process-exit-based**, because zombies look "alive" to the OS while serving zero requests. (iii) **Sage-attention disabled DOES NOT eliminate all CUDA fault risk** -- the original BUG-070 was Sage+FLUX, but BUG-073 is `model.cpu()` between Mistral phases, which has nothing to do with Sage. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `cuda-fault-recovery` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-073; tags `cuda-illegal-memory-access`, `model-cpu-unload`, `comfyui-desktop-respawn-broken`, `windows-cuda-zombie`, `unkillable-process`, `port-held-hostage`. Suggested bug_bible_regression test: (deferred until reboot) -- a unit test that wraps `model.cpu()` with synchronize-then-cpu and asserts a controlled exception comes out instead of a process-killing fault.
- **Tags:** cuda-illegal-memory-access, model-cpu-unload, comfyui-desktop-respawn-broken, windows-cuda-zombie, unkillable-process, port-held-hostage, manual-recovery-required, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-072: Voice health check ran eagerly on full 10-preset Bark catalog before Director assigned voices, costing ~120s on first run after Comfy boot regardless of cast size [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live OTR FULL workflow run @ 18:38 logged ten sequential `[VoiceHealth] v2/en_speaker_N OK (Xs)` lines totalling ~120s (4.9 + 6.0 + 24.6 + 19.1 + 19.0 + 14.0 + 14.9 + 7.1 + 9.3 + ?), running BEFORE the Director phase even started. Subsequent queues in the same Comfy session were no-ops (idempotent flag), so the cost was once-per-process; but every Comfy restart paid the full tax. The check tested all ten English speaker presets even when the cast only used five. Jeffrey called this out as architecturally backwards: "why does it check the voices before parsing the variables for the story?" The catalog is exhaustively pre-flighted for an episode that may use 3-5 voices total.
- **Cause:** `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:_bark_health_check()` was wired into `LLMDirector.direct()` at line 7857 (BEFORE the LLM inference call that produces voice_assignments). Because Director hadn't run yet, the check had no way to know which presets the cast would actually need, so it tested the full union of `_VOICE_PROFILES + _ANNOUNCER_PRESETS + _LEMMY_PROFILE`. The original design intent was defensive: ensure Director's voice picker only chooses from validated presets, preventing a 4-minute audio render from crashing on a corrupt voice file. Trade-off: ~120s of guaranteed up-front cost vs. 0% chance of mid-render Bark crash.
- **Fix:** Refactored into three pieces: (1) extracted pure tester `_bark_test_presets(presets) -> (passed, disabled, reason)` with no global state mutation; (2) kept legacy `_bark_health_check()` exported for any external caller, but no longer called from the orchestrator; (3) added `_bark_health_check_for_cast(cast_rows)` that runs AFTER cast-merge with the cast's actual `voice_preset` values, filters Bark presets only (`startswith("v2/")`), tests just those, and on failure remaps the cast row's `voice_preset` to a known-good fallback from `_VOICE_PROFILES` matching the row's gender (preferring not-already-in-use). Wired the lazy call into the cast-merge path right after `_consolidate_similar_cast_rows`. Net change: -~95s on first run after Comfy boot (5-voice cast no longer pays for 10-voice catalog warmup), +~25s on every subsequent queue (the lazy check runs each queue, not once-per-process), and individual preset failures now self-heal via in-cast remap instead of requiring a global pool prune.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) Existing 11 BUG-068 cast-namespace tests still pass; new 23 BUG-071 fuzzy-merge tests still pass (34/34 total in cast suite). (c) Behavioural verification on next FULL run: log should show `[VoiceHealth] Lazy cast check on N preset(s)` (where N is the cast size, not 10); should NOT show the prior `Running 1-second Bark health check on English presets` legacy banner; the cast row remapping on any individual preset failure should land in the runtime log as `VOICE_HEALTH_LAZY: remap NAME old -> new`. (d) Future regression: a unit test that builds a synthetic cast with one Bark preset value flagged as failing in `_BARK_HEALTH_DISABLED` and asserts `_bark_health_check_for_cast` remaps it to a non-disabled gender-matched fallback (deferred until after v2.0 ships, captured in `tests/test_voice_health_lazy_remap.py` placeholder).
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **never pre-flight the entire catalog of any expensive resource (voices, models, GPU contexts, network connections) before knowing which subset the request actually needs.** When the catalog has N items but a request uses K << N, the savings are O((N-K)/N) ≈ huge. The defensive argument ("what if the picker picks a bad one") is solved cheaper by post-pick validation + remap: cost is K test calls instead of N, and the remap logic is straightforward (pool by attribute, prefer not-in-use, fall back to global). Subsidiary rules: (i) when refactoring eager pre-flight to lazy-after-pick, keep the eager function exported as a manual-tool entry point so any external caller still works; (ii) when remapping a failed pick, prefer same-attribute (gender, model-class, etc.) over generic fallback, but always have a generic last-resort; (iii) the `_BARK_HEALTH_DISABLED` global set is the persistence channel that lets failures from one queue inform fallback selection in the next without re-testing. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `eager-vs-lazy-validation` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-072; tags `eager-validation`, `pre-flight-cost`, `lazy-validation`, `voice-health`, `bark`, `architectural-refactor`. Suggested bug_bible_regression test: stub `_bark_test_presets` to return one disabled preset, run `_bark_health_check_for_cast` on a 3-row cast that uses that preset, assert the row's voice_preset is remapped and gender is preserved.
- **Tags:** voice-health, eager-validation, pre-flight-cost, lazy-validation, bark, architectural-refactor, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-071: Cast-namespace dedup misses prefix-overlap and LLM typo divergence cases [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 PM | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Two distinct cast-fragmentation shapes observed live in two consecutive runs. Shape A (Captain-Eris-Violet creative + Mistral-Nemo cleanup, "Mars Surface Colony Habitat" run @ 19:23): cast had 5 rows where it should have had 3 -- `c02 LLOYD lines=6 voice=(empty)` co-existed with `c03 LLOYD KAPOOR lines=6 voice=v2/en_speaker_1`, same for `c04 PHYLLIS` vs `c05 PHYLLIS SHAW`. Captain-Eris drafted using first names in dialogue tags (`[LLOYD]`); Mistral cleanup pass added full names in character descriptions (`LLOYD KAPOOR`); the Director cast-merge saw two separate characters per person. Net effect: the speaker rows had dialogue but no voice; the description rows had voice but no dialogue; downstream Director got confused, generated 1 scene / 0 shots; FLUX rendered 1 fallback PNG instead of the expected 7. Shape B (single-LLM Mistral, observed live in next run @ 19:43): Mistral mid-stream typoed `[STANLEY]` as `[STANLEARY]` and alternated for the rest of the script -- 7+ occurrences of each variant -- which the parser treats as two separate characters. Both shapes are NOT covered by the original BUG-068 fix which only handled space/underscore variants (`KAEL VAUGHN` vs `KAEL_VAUGHN`).
- **Cause:** The post-BUG-068 cast-merge in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` around line 7921 normalises names with `_norm_name_key()` (strip / upper / underscore -> space), then does an exact-equality lookup. Both new failure shapes pass through the normaliser unchanged because they're not space/underscore variants -- they're prefix overlaps (LLOYD vs LLOYD KAPOOR) and typo divergences (STANLEY vs STANLEARY). The original fix solved exactly one shape; the larger problem -- LLM compliance is non-deterministic and emits the same character with multiple spellings within a single script -- has at least three observed shapes and probably more in the wild. Naive equality dedup only catches one of them.
- **Fix:** Added module-level helpers `_norm_cast_key()`, `_cast_names_should_merge()`, `_consolidate_similar_cast_rows()` near `_bark_health_check`. The new fuzzy-merge runs as a final pass after the existing voice_assignments merge, before the cast is written to the ledger. Decision rules: (1) exact equality after normalisation -> merge (existing behaviour); (2) shorter is whitespace-aligned token prefix of longer -> merge, longer wins (catches LLOYD ⊂ LLOYD KAPOOR); (3) both single-token AND `difflib.SequenceMatcher.ratio() >= 0.85` -> merge, longer wins (catches STANLEY vs STANLEARY at ratio=0.875). Multi-token names are exempt from the typo rule to avoid ROBERT FROST + ROBERT FORD collapsing incorrectly. Loser data folds into winner: voice_preset (any non-None wins), description / gender (any non-empty wins), line_count + word_count summed. Idempotent.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) New regression test `tests/test_cast_fuzzy_consolidate.py` -- 23 cases covering both bug shapes, edge cases (empty / single-char / single-row), and explicit no-over-merge guards (ROBERT FROST vs ROBERT FORD; PHYLLIS vs LLOYD KAPOOR). All 23 pass. (c) Existing BUG-068 test `tests/test_director_cast_namespace_merge.py` still passes (11/11) -- new logic doesn't break the old space/underscore behaviour. (d) Live: next FULL run with two-LLM split or single-LLM Mistral should show clean cast (no LLOYD + LLOYD KAPOOR pair, no STANLEY + STANLEARY pair) and Director should generate the full set of scenes/shots/env-tokens, FLUX should render the full grid (3-7 PNGs depending on preset).
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: any LLM-driven character-extraction pipeline must dedup against three name divergence shapes simultaneously: (1) format/case (KAEL_VAUGHN vs KAEL VAUGHN), (2) prefix overlap (LLOYD vs LLOYD KAPOOR), (3) typo divergence (STANLEY vs STANLEARY). Treating "name normalisation" as a single regex/lower/strip operation captures only shape 1; the other two need pair-wise comparison with `difflib.SequenceMatcher` plus explicit guard rules. Subsidiary rules: (i) when fuzzy-merging names, a winner-rule prevents ambiguity ("longer wins" works for both prefix-overlap and typo-divergence); (ii) multi-token names are exempt from typo rules because surname divergence (FROST vs FORD) has high ratio but represents distinct people; (iii) idempotent consolidation (running twice yields the same result) is a hard requirement because the orchestrator may call it after parser AND after director-merge. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `cast-namespace-dedup` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-071; tags `cast-namespace`, `fuzzy-merge`, `prefix-overlap`, `typo-divergence`, `llm-non-determinism`, `sequence-matcher`. Suggested bug_bible_regression test: feed the helper a fixture with all three divergence shapes plus distinct-character control rows and assert the right rows survive with the right winning names.
- **Tags:** cast-namespace, fuzzy-merge, prefix-overlap, typo-divergence, llm-non-determinism, sequence-matcher, bug-068-expansion, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-070: SageAttention's `sageattn_qk_int8_pv_fp16_cuda` backend is incompatible with FLUX-fp8 inference on Blackwell sm_120 + cu130 + torch 2.10 -- patching the kernel onto the FLUX model via `PathchSageAttentionKJ` kills the ComfyUI Python process at the first KSampler forward call regardless of whether the call shape is `OTR_BatchFluxRender`'s stacked-conditioning fast-batch OR the standard ComfyUI KSampler one-cond-at-a-time pattern. The HuMo (Wan 2.1) attention path is unaffected; the bug is FLUX-specific because FLUX uses RoPE-style attention with a custom tensor packing that SageAttention's int8 PV kernel does not handle [WORKAROUND APPLIED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live FULL workflow run on `v2.0-alpha @ ff341c2` immediately after wiring `PathchSageAttentionKJ` into both the FLUX (id=42) and HuMo (id=43) branches. The audio path completed cleanly (35 dialogue lines, 3 cast, 4:44 episode duration). The graph then crashed inside `OTR_BatchFluxRender`. **Initial misdiagnosis (later corrected):** thought the crash was BatchFluxRender's `_try_fast_batch` stacked-conditioning shape interacting badly with Sage. **Re-diagnosed via standalone smoke test (`otr_flux_smoke_sage.json`)** which uses ZERO custom code -- only stock CheckpointLoaderSimple + CLIPTextEncode + EmptySD3LatentImage + standard KSampler one-cond-at-a-time + VAEDecode + SaveImage, with a single `PathchSageAttentionKJ` node patched on the FLUX model. **The smoke test crashed Python with the same native fault.** Stack trace: `sage_func (KJNodes:35) -> attention_sage (KJNodes:83) -> attention_override_sage (KJNodes:125) -> comfy/ldm/flux/math.py:14 attention -> comfy/ldm/flux/layers.py:235 forward -> comfy/ldm/flux/model.py:243 forward_orig -> nodes.py:1556 common_ksampler`. That's stock ComfyUI KSampler, no BatchFluxRender, no batched conditioning -- crash still happens. So the bug scope is NOT BatchFluxRender-specific; it is SageAttention's int8 PV kernel vs FLUX-fp8 attention math in general.
- **Cause:** SageAttention 2.2.0 `sageattn_qk_int8_pv_fp16_cuda` kernel is **incompatible with FLUX's RoPE-style attention layout on Blackwell sm_120 + torch 2.10 + cu130**. FLUX uses a custom q/k packing in `comfy/ldm/flux/math.py:14` that interacts with positional encoding via apply_rope before the attention call; the int8 PV kernel makes assumptions about q/k tensor layout (head dimension ordering, packing format, possibly head_dim%64 alignment) that hold for SDXL-family attention and Wan 2.1 (HuMo) attention but NOT for FLUX's packed-RoPE form. The kernel hits a CUDA out-of-bounds access that Blackwell's driver promotes to SIGSEGV, translated to a Windows native fault. No Python traceback because the C kernel terminates the process before unwinding. **The HuMo (Wan 2.1) branch is unaffected** -- Wan uses standard MultiHeadAttention layout that SageAttention is well-tested against, and the in-graph HuMo demo + the HuMo orchestrator both successfully use the same Sage backend without crashing. Tested without BatchFluxRender (smoke workflow) -- crash reproduces. So the integration was never going to work for FLUX-fp8 regardless of what feeds it.
- **Fix:** Workaround = **permanent disable on FLUX**. The FLUX-side `PathchSageAttentionKJ` node (id=42) `sage_attention` widget is set to `"disabled"`. The HuMo-side patch (id=43) stays active at `sageattn_qk_int8_pv_fp16_cuda` -- HuMo benefits the full 20-30% speedup. **No "permanent fix path" exists short-term** -- the bug is in the SageAttention kernel itself vs FLUX's RoPE-packed attention shape. The three theoretical fixes are all upstream: (i) thu-ml/SageAttention adds a FLUX-aware code path in `sageattn_qk_int8_pv_fp16_cuda` that handles the RoPE packing -- could file an issue with the smoke-test JSON as a minimal repro; (ii) ComfyUI's `comfy/ldm/flux/math.py` could un-pack q/k before the attention call so the kernel sees standard layout (would slow FLUX overall, doubt they'd accept); (iii) wait for SageAttention 3 (FP4 path) which has a different kernel layout that may handle FLUX correctly -- but Sage3 has no Windows wheel and requires WSL2 or source compilation per the install guide. Recommended action for OTR: keep Sage disabled on FLUX permanently; recover the lost 25-35% speedup elsewhere. The 60-second smoke workflow `otr_flux_smoke.json` confirms FLUX itself runs at 1.32s/it baseline with no Sage -- that's the baseline we live with.
- **Verify:** (a) JSON parse clean; all 34 nodes still carry `flags`/`order`/`mode` (re-validated post-edit). (b) FLUX patch widget value is `"disabled"`; HuMo patch widget value is `"sageattn_qk_int8_pv_fp16_cuda"`; node titles updated to reflect state. (c) Live: next FULL run should complete the audio path AND the FLUX environment-stills path AND the HuMo demo without the native crash. The FLUX path will run at the pre-SageAttention speed (no 25-35% speedup); the HuMo path keeps its 20-30% SageAttention speedup. (d) Future regression test idea: a fixture that exercises `BatchFluxRender._try_fast_batch` with a mock SageAttention-patched model and asserts no crash.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: **custom-batched-conditioning code paths in inference orchestrators (any code that calls `torch.cat` on conditioning tensors and feeds a single sampler call rather than looping) are the highest-risk integration point for any attention kernel optimisation library** (SageAttention, FlashAttention, xFormers, custom Triton kernels, etc). The shape assumptions in those kernels are tested against the ComfyUI standard call pattern (one cond at a time), not against the batched pattern. When adding ANY attention-kernel patcher to a graph that includes a custom batched-call node, exercise that node's hot path BEFORE relying on the run -- a smoke test of one shot through the patched node catches the integration crash in seconds, before betting a full overnight run. Subsidiary rules: (i) when a Python process exits with no traceback and the C-stack ends inside a CUDA kernel, suspect a kernel-shape-assumption violation, not a Python error; (ii) keep node-level patchers separate per branch (one for FLUX, one for HuMo) so a single-branch failure does not force a full SageAttention disable -- the existing two-node split paid off here; (iii) document the crash signature ("native abort, no Python error, comfyui.log ends with native stack dump") in the bug entry so future debugging starts at the right diagnosis. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `attention-kernel-integration` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-070; tags `attention-kernel-integration`, `sageattention`, `batchfluxrender`, `fast-batch-conditioning`, `native-process-abort`, `no-python-traceback`, `int8-pv-kernel-shape-assumption`, `flux-fp8-blackwell`. Suggested `bug_bible_regression.py` test: scaffold a minimal `PathchSageAttentionKJ` patched onto a custom-batched-conditioning fixture, assert sampler call returns without abort.
- **Tags:** attention-kernel-integration, sageattention, batchfluxrender, fast-batch-conditioning, native-process-abort, no-python-traceback, int8-pv-kernel-shape-assumption, flux-fp8-blackwell, workaround-applied, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-069: Hand-edited ComfyUI workflow JSON inserted new nodes without the required `flags` / `order` / `mode` metadata fields, so ComfyUI's zod schema validator rejected the entire workflow at load time with `Invalid workflow against zod schema: Required at "nodes[N].flags"; Required at "nodes[N].order"; Required at "nodes[N].mode"` -- the canonical ComfyUI node shape includes three metadata fields beyond the obvious id/type/pos/size/title/properties/widgets_values/inputs/outputs and any new node missing any one of them is rejected by the frontend before the graph ever runs [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live FULL workflow load on `v2.0-alpha @ d9c440b` (the SageAttention activation commit that inserted two `PathchSageAttentionKJ` nodes via Python JSON surgery rather than ComfyUI's drag-and-drop UI). On loading the workflow JSON in ComfyUI Desktop, the canvas rendered every node correctly but a yellow Alert popped up reading: `Invalid workflow against zod schema: Validation error: Required at "nodes[32].flags"; Required at "nodes[32].order"; Required at "nodes[32].mode"; Required at "nodes[33].flags"; Required at "nodes[33].order"; Required at "nodes[33].mode"`. Indices [32] and [33] were the array positions of the two newly-inserted patch nodes (id=42 and id=43 respectively). Queue Prompt was effectively blocked while the alert stood -- ComfyUI tolerates the partial graph for display but won't execute a workflow that fails its load-time schema check. Same Alert would have surfaced on any other hand-authored JSON edit that adds nodes without the three required metadata fields.
- **Cause:** ComfyUI's frontend uses a zod schema (TypeScript-side) to validate workflow JSON shape before letting the graph queue. Every node in the canonical workflow JSON carries three metadata fields beyond the obvious geometry / wiring fields: `flags` (object, typically `{}` -- exists for ad-hoc per-node UI flags like `pinned` or `collapsed`), `order` (int, the topological execution order index assigned by the canvas auto-layout), `mode` (int enum: `0`=ALWAYS, `2`=MUTE, `4`=BYPASS). When ComfyUI's UI saves a workflow it always writes these fields. When humans (or scripts) hand-author a new node from scratch, the natural minimal shape `{id, type, pos, size, title, properties, widgets_values, inputs, outputs}` looks complete on inspection but the zod schema considers it invalid. The validator fails fast on the first missing field but lists all of them in the error -- which is helpful but the error doesn't say WHICH fields are missing, only WHERE in the array the bad nodes live (`nodes[32]`, `nodes[33]`), so a first-time hitter has to grep the existing-node shape to figure out what's missing. The patch_workflow_sage.py edit script in the prior commit invented the two new nodes from scratch with the minimal shape and shipped them without the metadata, even though every other node in the same JSON file demonstrated the correct shape one screen above.
- **Fix:** Two parts. (1) Hotfix at `nodes/scripts/fix_patch_node_metadata.py`-style surgery: walked the `nodes[]` array, scanned each node for `flags` / `order` / `mode`, defaulted any missing field (`flags={}`, `order=max(existing)+1`, `mode=0`). All 34 nodes now carry the required fields and ComfyUI loads the workflow cleanly. (2) Long-term shape contract: any future hand-authored node-creation Python code (e.g. `patch_workflow_sage.py` and similar future migration scripts) must seed the new node dict with `{"flags": {}, "order": <next>, "mode": 0}` alongside the geometry fields. The canonical node template now lives as a comment block in the BUG-LOCAL-069 entry below for any future hand-edit author to crib from.
- **Verify:** (a) JSON parse clean. (b) Validation script `python -c "import json; j=json.load(open('workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_full.json')); req={'flags','order','mode'}; missing=[(n['id'],n['type'],f) for n in j['nodes'] for f in req if f not in n]; print('missing:', missing if missing else 'OK')"` returns `OK` (none missing). (c) Live: ComfyUI Desktop loads the workflow without the yellow Alert; Queue Prompt is no longer blocked. (d) Regression coverage to add post-v2.0-ship: a unit test that walks every shipped workflow JSON in `workflows/` and asserts every `nodes[*]` entry has `flags`, `order`, `mode` keys present. Lives well in `tests/test_workflow_schema_contract.py` (does not yet exist) -- worth scaffolding when the workflow JSON count grows past three or when next time someone hand-edits a workflow.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Generalisable rule: when emitting a JSON record that targets a frontend that uses runtime schema validation (zod, JSONSchema, msgspec, pydantic, etc.), the canonical record shape is whatever the frontend WRITES, not what the human reads as "minimal viable". Cribbing the shape from a sibling record in the same file is the only safe seed for hand-authored edits. Subsidiary rules: (i) zod errors that name array indices (`nodes[32]`) without naming the missing fields force a grep-against-sibling-records workflow; downstream consumers of the error message should expand it to also dump the exemplar shape; (ii) workflow JSONs are de facto API contracts and should have a per-node-shape lint test that runs in CI alongside the JSON-validity test; (iii) when a script invents a new node, the function that builds the node dict should accept the canonical shape as a constant and `**defaults | overrides` so missing fields can never silently drop. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `comfyui-workflow-schema` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-069; tags `comfyui-workflow-schema`, `zod-validation`, `hand-edited-json`, `metadata-fields-missing`, `flags-order-mode`, `node-shape-contract`, `cribbing-from-sibling-record`. Suggested bug_bible_regression test: assert that `flags` / `order` / `mode` are present on every node in every shipped workflow JSON.

**Canonical ComfyUI node shape (cheat-sheet for future hand-edits):**

```python
{
    "id": int,                    # last_node_id + N
    "type": "ClassNameAsRegistered",
    "pos": [int, int],            # canvas coordinates
    "size": [int, int],           # node body width, height
    "title": "Display title",
    "properties": {
        "Node name for S&R": "ClassNameAsRegistered",
    },
    "widgets_values": [...],      # widget defaults in INPUT_TYPES order
    "inputs": [
        {"name": "model", "type": "MODEL", "link": int_or_None},
    ],
    "outputs": [
        {"name": "MODEL", "type": "MODEL", "links": [int, ...], "slot_index": 0},
    ],
    "flags": {},                  # REQUIRED -- usually empty dict
    "order": int,                 # REQUIRED -- topological order, last+1 is safe
    "mode": 0,                    # REQUIRED -- 0=ALWAYS, 2=MUTE, 4=BYPASS
    # Optional:
    "color": "#hexcolor",
    "bgcolor": "#hexcolor",
}
```

When inventing a new node in Python, build the dict from this template and override only what differs. Never start from a "minimal" subset.

- **Tags:** comfyui-workflow-schema, zod-validation, hand-edited-json, metadata-fields-missing, flags-order-mode, node-shape-contract, cribbing-from-sibling-record, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-068: Director cast-merge step appended duplicate underscored cast rows for every Director-known character, leaving voice_preset on the new (underscored) row and dialogue on the original (spaced) row -- net effect: speaking characters had `voice_preset=None` and Bark fell back to default voices, while the underscored "ghost" rows had voice_preset but never appeared in dialogue [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live FULL trial of Captain-Eris-Violet ("Signal Abyss" episode, ledger `signal_lost_signal_abyss_20260426_161737_ledger.json`) produced a 5-entry `cast` array where the 3 actual speaking characters and the 2 procedural "ghosts" had split data: `c01 ANNOUNCER lines=3 voice=v2/en_speaker_0` (correct), `c02 KAEL VAUGHN lines=1 voice=None` (no voice mapping), `c03 REI HOWARD lines=1 voice=None` (no voice mapping), `c04 REI_HOWARD lines=0 voice=v2/en_speaker_4` (voice but no lines), `c05 KAEL_VAUGHN lines=0 voice=v2/en_speaker_3` (voice but no lines). Bark synthesised the dialogue lines using its default voices because the `voice_preset` lookup at the speaking-character row returned None; the procedural-quality voice presets the Director had selected (gender-matched, age-matched) attached to never-spoken ghost rows. Same family as BUG-LOCAL-064 ("two parallel name-spaces, only one wins downstream") but a different surface: the previous bug leaked the procedural NAME into `description`; this bug splits the entire CAST ROW so neither half is complete.
- **Cause:** Director-stage cast-merge loop in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` around line 7847. `existing_cast` rows from ScriptWriter use the canonical script-side spaced form (`"KAEL VAUGHN"`) because the parser's v5 / v4 regexes accept names with spaces and emit them verbatim. The Director's `voice_assignments` dict, however, uses the underscored variant (`"KAEL_VAUGHN"`) because the procedural-name flow + JSON-key safety layer normalises spaces to underscores before serialising. The merge loop did `name_to_row = {r.get("name"): r for r in existing_cast}` then `row = name_to_row.get(key)` where `key = char_name.strip().upper()` -- but `key` was the raw (underscored) string from `voice_assignments` and `name_to_row`'s keys were the raw (spaced) strings from the ScriptWriter cast. `"KAEL_VAUGHN" != "KAEL VAUGHN"` so every `name_to_row.get(key)` returned `None`, and the `if row is None:` branch APPENDED a fresh underscored row with the voice_preset attached, leaving the original spaced row's `voice_preset` at `None`. BatchBark downstream, looking at the speaking row, found `voice_preset=None` and fell back to a default Bark voice rather than the Director's curated pick.
- **Fix:** Added a tiny normaliser `_norm_name_key(s) = s.strip().upper().replace("_", " ")` and used it on BOTH sides of the lookup at `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` lines 7847-7884. Build `name_to_row` keyed on the normalised form (so `"KAEL VAUGHN"` and `"KAEL_VAUGHN"` collapse to the same key), and pass the same normaliser over the `voice_assignments` keys before the lookup. When a brand-new character lands (Director added an atmospheric voice the script never named), the new row's `name` field is the normalised SPACED form so the ledger never carries underscored names that would re-trigger the same bug for any later consumer (BatchBark, render_flux_batch.py, render_humo_batch.py, the cowork ledger viewer artifact, the cowork production-ledger artifact, etc.).
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Python 3.12.11. (b) New regression file `tests/test_director_cast_namespace_merge.py` -- 11 tests covering: 3 reproductions of the live "Signal Abyss" failure shape (no duplicate rows, voice_preset on speaking row, no underscored survivors); 1 new-character-only path (Director adds atmospheric voice the script never named -> spaced row); 2 symmetry tests (existing-underscored + voice-assignments-spaced; existing-spaced + voice-assignments-underscored); 5 normaliser boundary tests (empty/None/whitespace/already-normalised/mixed-case/multiple-underscores/leading-trailing-whitespace). All 11 pass. (c) Full focused regression `test_director_cast_namespace_merge + test_director_cast_naming + test_widget_drift_guard + test_dropdown_guardrails (subset) + test_llm_cache_mismatch_diagnostics + test_parser_dialogue_floor + test_render_flux_batch + test_auto_title_from_spine + test_production_ledger`: 144 passed / 0 failed. Bug bible regression unchanged. (d) Live: next FULL run's ledger `cast` should contain exactly the speaking characters' rows (no underscored ghosts), each row should have BOTH `voice_preset` and `line_count > 0`, and the names should be in the script-side spaced form throughout.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate -- this is the second instance of the "two parallel name-spaces, only one wins downstream" pattern (BUG-LOCAL-064 was the first), now generalisable into a stronger rule: when two upstream stages emit a key that names the same logical entity in different forms (spaces vs underscores, ALL CAPS vs Title Case, with-honorific vs without, etc.), the merge step MUST normalise both sides via a shared helper, AND the normalised form MUST be persisted as the canonical one so no downstream consumer sees the divergent forms. Subsidiary rules: (i) lookup mismatches across name-spaces are silent by default -- they don't raise, they don't log, they just append a duplicate row that nobody reads; the only way to catch them is a regression test that exercises the merge with realistic mixed-form inputs; (ii) when an LLM emits a structured-output dict whose KEYS are derived from a free-form text upstream, audit every consumer that joins those keys against any other source -- LLMs love to swap underscores for spaces and vice versa, especially across JSON serialise / deserialise round-trips; (iii) "the canonical form is whatever the user reads downstream" -- if BatchBark / FLUX / HuMo all see the spaced form, persist the spaced form, even when the JSON-safe underscored form might feel cleaner internally. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `name-namespace-leak` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-068; tags `name-namespace-leak`, `cast-row-split`, `voice-preset-orphaned`, `merge-key-normalisation-missing`, `silent-duplicate-append`, `bark-default-voice-fallback`, `director-vs-scriptwriter-divergence`.
- **Tags:** name-namespace-leak, cast-row-split, voice-preset-orphaned, merge-key-normalisation-missing, silent-duplicate-append, bark-default-voice-fallback, director-vs-scriptwriter-divergence, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-067: Output foldering fragmented across `output/old_time_radio/`, `output/otr/`, `output/video/`, and `output/otr_stills/` -- the intended `output/otr/` super-folder design only partially applied (FLUX stills + HuMo videos + 1080p deliveries nest there, but audio episodes, ledgers, treatments, and proc-gen mp4s still wrote to `output/old_time_radio/`); media-server-style consumers had to point at multiple folders or look up several path conventions to find a single episode's assets [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live FULL trial of Captain-Eris-Violet ("Signal Abyss" episode, ledger `signal_lost_signal_abyss_20260426_161737_ledger.json`) produced a 93 MB audio-episode mp4 + l2-schema ledger that landed in `output/old_time_radio/`, while the FLUX stills (when present) + multi-clip HuMo videos + 1080p delivery files nested under `output/otr/`. The OTR super-folder design from earlier in the session ("output/otr was supposed to house everything") was only half-applied -- `render_flux_batch.py` (commit `d5772a3`), `render_humo_batch.py`, `render_episode_concat.py`, and `render_upscale_batch.py` (commit `eb9152e`) all wrote under `output/otr/`, but `nodes/video_engine.py` `OTR_SignalLostVideo`, `nodes/scene_sequencer.py` `DEFAULT_OUT`, `nodes/production_ledger.py` `_default_out_dir()`, and the QA-debug dump path in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` still hardcoded `output/old_time_radio/`. Net effect: a single episode's assets ended up split across two unrelated folder trees, OBS playlist + media-server pointers had to watch both, and downstream orchestrators (`render_humo_batch.py`) had to glob across multiple legacy locations to find the source ledger.
- **Cause:** Foldering was added incrementally over several days without a single source of truth. `output/old_time_radio/` was the v1.5 audio-episode home; in mid-late April 2026 the v2.0 video stack started landing under `output/otr_stills/` and `output/otr_videos/`; on 2026-04-26 morning the latter were renamed to `output/otr/stills/` and `output/otr/videos/<id>/` and a new `output/otr/episodes/` flat-delivery folder was added (commit `9052e46`). The audio-side write paths in `nodes/video_engine.py:1395`, `nodes/scene_sequencer.py:147`, `nodes/production_ledger.py:76`, and `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:4323` were never updated in that pass -- they still pointed at `output/old_time_radio/`. The intent ("everything under output/otr/") was documented in the ROADMAP but not enforced by code review or by a test, so the four legacy paths drifted in plain sight.
- **Fix:** One-line edits in five load-bearing write paths so every OTR write goes to `output/otr/audio/` (renamed from `output/old_time_radio/`). (a) `nodes/scene_sequencer.py:147` `DEFAULT_OUT` now uses `output/otr/audio`. (b) `nodes/production_ledger.py:76` `_default_out_dir()` returns `output/otr/audio`. (c) `nodes/video_engine.py:1395` `OTR_SignalLostVideo` writes the proc-gen episode mp4 to `output/otr/audio` and (d) `nodes/video_engine.py:1487` reports the same `subfolder` to ComfyUI's UI so the inline player widget still finds it. (e) `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:4323` writes QA debug dumps alongside the matching ledger under `output/otr/audio/`. Plus (f) `scripts/render_humo_batch.py:1004` now globs both `output/otr/audio/humo_episode/` AND the legacy `output/old_time_radio/humo_episode/` so previously-rendered HuMo clips still resolve. Existing files in `output/old_time_radio/` are NOT moved -- legacy episodes stay where they are so external consumers (OBS playlist, README references, viewer/index.html bookmarks) keep working until they're explicitly migrated.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Python 3.12.11 across all 5 touched files. (b) Full focused regression `test_widget_drift_guard + test_dropdown_guardrails (subset) + test_llm_cache_mismatch_diagnostics + test_parser_dialogue_floor + test_render_flux_batch + test_director_cast_naming + test_auto_title_from_spine + test_production_ledger`: 133 passed / 0 failed. Bug bible regression unchanged. (c) Live: next FULL run's audio episode mp4, ledger, treatment, and QA debug dump should all land in `C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\output\otr\audio\` rather than `C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\output\old_time_radio\`. After the run, `output/otr/audio/` and `output/otr/stills/` and `output/otr/videos/<id>/` should all be populated with that episode's artifacts; a media-server pointer at `output/otr/` will see every asset with one watch path.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate -- this is a textbook "intent documented but not enforced" foldering bug, generalizable to any project whose output paths are scattered across multiple write sites. Generalizable rules: (i) when a project introduces a "single super-folder" output design, every write site must be updated in the same commit, otherwise the legacy paths drift in plain sight; (ii) write paths should be centralised in a single helper module (e.g. `output_paths.py`) rather than hardcoded at each call site -- Python's text-based grep is the wrong place to enforce path conventions; (iii) a regression test should assert the write-path shape (e.g. parametrised `assert path.parts[-3:] == ("output", "otr", "audio")` for every node that exposes its output dir) so future drift trips a CI failure instead of being discovered weeks later in a live trial run; (iv) when migrating to a new folder, never move existing files unless explicitly requested -- legacy consumers (OBS, viewer html, manual scripts) need a stable read path while the new design beds in. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `output-foldering-drift` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-067; tags `output-foldering-drift`, `super-folder-half-applied`, `intent-not-enforced`, `media-server-pointer`, `legacy-path-fallback`, `centralised-path-helper-missing`.
- **Tags:** output-foldering-drift, super-folder-half-applied, intent-not-enforced, media-server-pointer, legacy-path-fallback, centralised-path-helper-missing, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-066: Three structural asymmetries in the post-Grammarian pipeline silently dropped 15 of 16 dialogue lines per FULL run -- parser v5 regex rejected underscored character names (`OSCAR_KANE`, `RUFUS_HALPERT`), Grammarian's _post_total counter was missing the bracket-shorthand pattern that _pre_total counted, and LLM_RESCUE only fired on a literal 0-dialogue ValueError, so a 16->1 silent collapse passed every safety net [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live FULL trial on `v2.0-alpha @ 4389b3f` shipped a 38-second episode containing only the ANNOUNCER cold-open monologue. Runtime log showed: WORD_EXTEND added 16 dialogue lines for OSCAR_KANE / RUFUS_HALPERT / AUTHORITY_VOICE; FORMAT_NORM saw `dialogue=19+0V` (19 bare-colon dialogue lines); Grammarian polished `19 -> 16` and printed `Success` (its 80% safety check passed); but the very next checkpoint, `DIALOGUE_TRACK: 07_AFTER_PARSE`, reported `count=1 | characters=['ANNOUNCER']`. 15 dialogue lines vanished between the Grammarian's "success" log and the parser's output, with no warning, no rescue, and no error. The episode's final ledger had OSCAR_KANE and RUFUS_HALPERT in the cast but with `line_count=0, word_count=0` -- they had been silently muted.
- **Cause:** Three independent structural asymmetries combined to make the failure invisible. (1) The parser's v5 bare-colon regex at `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:7099` was `[A-Z][A-Z0-9 ]{0,24}` -- no underscore in the character class. The production_plan path emits character names with underscores (`OSCAR_KANE`, `RUFUS_HALPERT`, `AUTHORITY_VOICE`) once the Director's CAST_GENDER_MATCH normalisation runs. Every other counter in the pipeline (`_RE_BARE_DIALOGUE` line 1624, FORMAT_NORM, Grammarian `_pre_total`, the WORD_ENFORCEMENT regex, the BUG-038 fallback-trigger regex line 7152) accepted underscores via `[A-Z][A-Z0-9_ ]`. The parser was the lone hold-out, so underscored-name dialogue was visible to every metric but silently dropped at the actual parse step. (2) Grammarian's `_post_total` counter at line 6331-6335 counted only `^[A-Z][A-Z0-9 ]{1,19}:` and `[VOICE:` -- it did NOT count the `[NAME, mood] dialogue` bracket-shorthand form. Its `_pre_total` at line 6223-6231 DID count all three forms (BUG-LOCAL-063 had added the `_pre_shorthand` counter but never updated `_post_total` to match). When the LLM converted forms during polish, the counter was asymmetric in both directions: form-to-shorthand conversion under-counted (false reject), shorthand-to-form conversion over-counted (false pass). (3) The post-parse rescue at line 4104 only fired on `if "0 dialogue lines" in str(parse_err)` -- i.e., only when the parser raised a ValueError. A parser that returned `1` dialogue token instead of `16` was a successful parse, no exception, no rescue. The combined failure mode: the parser dropped 15 of 16 lines because of (1), Grammarian's safety check passed because of (2), and rescue did not fire because of (3). Mistral-Nemo and any of its derivatives (Captain-Eris-Violet, etc.) all hit this since they all emit `OSCAR_KANE: line` form post-Director.
- **Fix:** Three coordinated edits in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` plus a regression test. (a) Parser v5 regex (line 7099) and the BUG-038 fallback-trigger regex (line 7152) gain `_` in their character class so they align with every upstream counter. (b) Grammarian's `_post_total` (line 6331-6335) now counts the bracket-shorthand pattern symmetrically with `_pre_total` -- counter is `_post_lines + _post_voice + _post_shorthand` matching the pre-side `_pre_lines + _pre_voice + _pre_shorthand`. (c) After the parser runs at line 4103, a new floor check reads `_expected_pre_parse_dialogue` from the post-Grammarian text (using all three forms) and compares against the parser's actual dialogue count. If `expected >= 6` and the parser produced `< 50%` of expected, an LLM_RESCUE pass runs against the Grammarian output and the rescued parse is adopted only if it recovers MORE dialogue than the original. The 50% threshold and the `>=6` floor avoid false rescues on naturally-short test scripts.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Python 3.12.11. (b) New regression file `tests/test_parser_dialogue_floor.py` -- 20 tests covering: 7 underscored / spaced / mixed character names exercise the v5 regex Fix A; 1 sanity check that the OLD regex failed (ensures the bug existed); 5 Grammarian counter symmetry tests across bare / shorthand / VOICE / mixed forms (covers Fix B); 6 floor-threshold tests covering the realistic post-Grammarian shape, the trigger threshold (50% of >=6), the don't-trigger zone (>=50% or expected<6), and the close-enough-to-skip-rescue cases (Fix C). All 20 pass. (c) Full focused regression `test_parser_dialogue_floor + test_llm_cache_mismatch_diagnostics + test_widget_drift_guard + test_dropdown_guardrails (subset) + test_render_flux_batch + test_director_cast_naming + test_auto_title_from_spine`: 105 passed / 0 failed. Bug bible regression: 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed (unchanged). (d) Live: next FULL run's `otr_runtime.log` should show `DIALOGUE_TRACK: 07_AFTER_PARSE` with a count that matches `AFTER_GRAMMARIAN` line count within the 50% threshold. If parsing drops far enough to miss the threshold, a `DIALOGUE_FLOOR: parsed N tokens but AFTER_GRAMMARIAN had M -- triggering LLM_RESCUE reparse` line will appear and the final dialogue count will reflect the rescued count.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate -- this is a textbook "asymmetric counter at the gate" bug, generalizable to any pipeline where multiple regex-based stages must agree on what counts as the same kind of token. Generalizable rules: (i) when a counter is added to `pre_total`, audit every downstream `post_total` site for symmetry -- the pre/post asymmetry is invisible until format drifts; (ii) if a parser regex character class is one of N regexes for the "same" thing in a pipeline, lock all N to the same character class via a shared constant or a shared compiled regex -- divergent character classes are how silent-drop bugs hide for weeks; (iii) a rescue path that fires only on exceptions misses the most common failure mode, which is "the operation succeeded but produced too little"; rescue should fire on quantity floors as well as on errors; (iv) a "Success" log line that names a count which is then secretly contradicted at the next stage is the strongest possible smell that there is an asymmetric counter in the pipeline -- audit immediately. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `pipeline-counter-asymmetry` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-066; tags `pipeline-counter-asymmetry`, `parser-regex-divergence`, `silent-dialogue-drop`, `rescue-only-on-exception`, `underscore-tolerance`, `grammarian-post-total`, `bracket-shorthand-blindness`, `llm-rescue-floor`.
- **Tags:** pipeline-counter-asymmetry, parser-regex-divergence, silent-dialogue-drop, rescue-only-on-exception, underscore-tolerance, grammarian-post-total, bracket-shorthand-blindness, llm-rescue-floor, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-065: LLM cache-mismatch between phases caused full unload + cold reload (~15-17 s thrash) every time the Story Writer crossed a phase boundary (title-gen -> Open-Close -> draft -> critique -> revise -> arc-enhancer), wasting 60-90 s wall-clock per FULL run despite all inputs (model_id, optimization_profile, context_cap) being identical [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Live FULL run on `v2.0-alpha @ 4389b3f` with a 12B Mistral-Nemo-derivative fine-tune + Pro Ultra Quality + open_close + self_critique + arc_enhancer all on. ComfyUI log showed three full cold-loads in the first ~4 minutes (L 250 initial, L 698 after title gen, L 1684 after Open-Close evaluator), each spending ~17 s on `_unload_llm()` -> `comfy.model_management.unload_all_models()` -> `_load_llm()` -> `from_pretrained` -> 4-bit re-quant -> CUDA warmup. Pattern continued through every subsequent LLM phase. The runtime log buffer (`otr_runtime.log`) confirmed the trigger: `[15:00:13] LLM cache mismatch (Context Cap: 6144) - reloading to enforce budget`. Context Cap was 6144 in both the cache and the new request -- so it was NOT the cap. The mismatch message did not name which field actually drifted, so the symptom was systemically opaque even though the log made the cycling obvious.
- **Cause:** Two interacting design weaknesses in `_load_llm()` at `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:945-977`. (1) The cache check was a single `or`-joined boolean expression -- when it tripped, the runtime log printed only "cache mismatch (Context Cap: ...)" without naming the offending field, so debugging required guessing. (2) The eviction sub-check at line 975 read `("cuda" in str(device) and "cpu" in current_model_device)` where `current_model_device = str(next(_LLM_CACHE["model"].parameters()).device)` -- i.e., it only inspected the FIRST parameter from the iterator. With bitsandbytes 4-bit quantization (the production path on 16 GB) the parameter iteration order is non-deterministic and a CPU-resident metadata buffer (quant_state, embed_tokens with offload, dequant scratch) frequently lands first, so the first param reports `cpu` even though the active inference weights are on `cuda:0`. The check then fired a false-positive eviction every phase boundary, evicted a healthy GPU-resident model, and triggered the full reload. The combined effect: 4-6 reload cycles per FULL run x ~17 s = 60-90 s of pure wall-clock thrash with zero quality benefit. CLAUDE.md's rule "Never use `force_vram_offload()` between LLM phases -- use `_flush_vram_keep_llm()`" was being honored by the call sites, but bypassed inside `_load_llm` itself by the spurious cache miss.
- **Fix:** `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:945-1010` -- (a) replaced the `next(model.parameters()).device` single-param probe with a scan of up to 8 parameters; the cache is treated as still-resident if ANY of them is on cuda. Captures `current_model_device` from param 0 (preserved for log fidelity) and a separate `any_cuda_param` flag from the scan. (b) Replaced the inline boolean cache check with explicit per-field delta collection -- each of `device`, `quantized`, `model_id`, `budget_profile`, `VERSION`, `context_cap` is compared individually and recorded as a `(name, cached, requested)` tuple in `cache_deltas`. The eviction check is appended to deltas only when `any_cuda_param is False`. (c) The runtime log line now reads `LLM cache mismatch (Context Cap: 6144) [fields drifted: device: 'cuda'->'cuda', ...]` -- naming the specific drifted field(s) so future regressions are diagnosable on first glance.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Python 3.12.11. (b) New regression file `tests/test_llm_cache_mismatch_diagnostics.py` -- 12 tests covering: 4 "no drift across LLM phases" scenarios (title-gen->openclose, openclose->draft, draft->critique->revision->arc, mistral-nemo warm-run) where every input is identical and the deltas list MUST be empty; 2 quantized-model false-positive guards (first param cpu but others cuda -> NO drift; all params cpu -> drift includes `model_evicted_to_cpu`); 5 legitimate-mismatch tests proving the fix did not mask real cache invalidations (model_id swap, profile swap, context_cap swap, VERSION bump, quantized flag flip); 1 fresh-cache test (model is None -> empty deltas). All 12 pass. (c) Full focused regression `test_llm_cache_mismatch_diagnostics + test_widget_drift_guard + test_dropdown_guardrails (subset) + test_render_flux_batch + test_director_cast_naming + test_auto_title_from_spine`: 92 passed / 0 failed. Bug bible regression: 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed. (d) Live: next FULL run's `otr_runtime.log` should show `OPENCLOSE: Evaluator done (...)` immediately followed by `Starting inference (max_new_tokens=...)` for the next phase -- no `LLM cache mismatch` line, no `Loading LLM model:` line between phases. Wall-clock from "spine done" to "draft starts" should drop from ~17 s to <1 s.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate -- this is a textbook "single-probe device check on a quantized model" bug, generalizable to any pipeline that caches a transformers model and re-validates residency on subsequent calls. Generalizable rules: (i) when checking model device residency for a quantized model loaded via bitsandbytes / accelerate / device_map, scan multiple parameters (or rely on the model's own `.hf_device_map` attribute) -- the first parameter is not load-bearing; (ii) cache-mismatch logs MUST name the drifting field, not just say "mismatch detected" -- opaque mismatches are how thrash bugs hide for weeks; (iii) inline boolean `or`-joined cache validations are a bad pattern; replace with explicit per-field delta collection so logs and tests can both target individual fields; (iv) the symptom "LLM keeps reloading between phases" generalizes -- if a single-node pipeline shows multiple `Loading model X` log entries between user-visible phase transitions, suspect a stale-cache check before suspecting an explicit unload call. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `cache-mismatch-thrash` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-065; tags `cache-mismatch-thrash`, `quantized-param-iter-nondeterminism`, `bitsandbytes-4bit`, `opaque-log-line`, `single-probe-device-check`, `boolean-or-cache-key`, `phase-boundary-reload`.
- **Tags:** cache-mismatch-thrash, quantized-param-iter-nondeterminism, bitsandbytes-4bit, opaque-log-line, single-probe-device-check, boolean-or-cache-key, phase-boundary-reload, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-064: LLMDirector procedural cast leaked a procedural last-name (e.g. "PRIAM SMITHERS") into cast.description so the visual layer disagreed with the script's character names (MEREDITH/RYAN/SEAN/VANCE), and could even contradict the gender hint (VANCE -> "NORA SATO - Male, intense, 50s") [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-26 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Astrotech FULL run on 2026-04-24 (commit `c8ef4b4`, ledger `signal_lost_astrotech_research_facility_humming_20260424_165327_ledger.json`) shipped a cast where `cast[*].name` carried the script-side names (MEREDITH, RYAN, SEAN, VANCE) but `cast[*].description` was prefixed with an entirely different procedural-flavor name picked by the random profile generator -- e.g. `MEREDITH` -> `"PRIAM SMITHERS - Female, wry, 40s"`, `RYAN` -> `"MAURICE WELLS - Male, warm, 50s"`, `SEAN` -> `"PETER KAPOOR - Male, sharp, 20s"`, `VANCE` -> `"NORA SATO - Male, intense, 50s"`. The dialogue lines, voice presets, and audio layer were all correct (script-side names won everywhere they actually mattered for audio) but the visual layer (FLUX portraits + composites) was about to render `PRIAM SMITHERS` while the audio said `MEREDITH`. Caught at FLUX-pass1 smoke-test setup before any image rendered. Bonus pathology: `_FIRST_NAMES` (line 413) is a single ungendered pool that mixes "Vance" and "Stone" alongside "Nora" and "Margot", so the procedural picker can pull a female-coded first name for a `gender_hint=male` character and emit an internally-contradictory name like "NORA SATO - Male".
- **Cause:** `LLMDirector._randomize_character_names()` in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` (line 7711). For LEMMY and ANNOUNCER the function correctly assigns `notes = profile["notes"]` (just the trait string -- "gravelly, gruff" / "Female, mature, authoritative"). For the regular-character branch (line 7813) it instead built `notes = f"{profile['name']} - {profile['notes']}"`, mixing the procedural last-name with the trait string. Downstream, `set_cast()` at line 7549-7550 takes that `notes` field verbatim as `cast.description`, so the procedural name leaked into the ledger as if it belonged to the script-named character. The procedural name was never echoed back into `cast.name`, so the script-side identity (used by Bark voice routing and dialogue attribution) stayed correct -- only the visual/description layer was poisoned. Comment at line 7812 ("The procedural name is stored in notes for the treatment file.") explicitly named the intent, but no consumer actually reads `cast.description` as a treatment file -- FLUX `render_flux_batch.py` reads it as the visual prompt and Cowork artifacts render it as the cast roster, so the treatment-file-only hypothesis was wishful thinking.
- **Fix:** One-line edit at `nodes/story_orchestrator.py:7813-7816`. Match the LEMMY/ANNOUNCER branches: `"notes": profile["notes"]` (drop the `{profile['name']} - ` prefix). The procedural name remains available on `profile['name']` for any future treatment-file consumer that genuinely wants it; it just no longer leaks into the ledger description. Also added cleanup helper `scripts/fix_cast_descriptions.py` (regex-strip `^[A-Z][A-Z]+\s+[A-Z][A-Z]+\s*-\s*` prefix, in-place + `.bak`) to repair existing already-shipped ledgers without re-running the director. Cleaned the astrotech ledger in place to unblock tonight's smoke test.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean. (b) New regression file `tests/test_director_cast_naming.py` -- 4 tests: `test_regular_character_notes_do_not_carry_procedural_name_prefix` (instantiates LLMDirector, calls `_randomize_character_names` with MEREDITH/RYAN/SEAN/VANCE + matching gender_map, asserts no notes match `^[A-Z][A-Z]+\s+[A-Z][A-Z]+\s*-\s*`); `test_regular_character_notes_look_like_trait_string` (asserts comma-separated traits + gender hint propagated); `test_voice_assignments_keyed_on_original_script_name` (asserts dict keys are still MEREDITH/VANCE, no procedural keys leaked); `test_lemmy_branch_unchanged` (sanity that LEMMY notes are still bare `profile["notes"]`). (c) Full focused regression `test_director_cast_naming + test_render_flux_batch + test_build_silent_test_episode + test_production_ledger`: 118 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed. (d) Live: next FULL run's ledger should show `cast[*].description` matching `^(Female|Male),\s+\w+,\s+\d+s$` shape (or null for cast members the LLM never described), with no two-uppercase-word prefix.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate -- this is a textbook "two parallel name-spaces, only one wins downstream" bug, generalizable to any procedural-augment-the-LLM pipeline. Generalizable rules: (i) if a generator function returns a synthetic identity (`profile["name"]`) alongside its real payload (`profile["notes"]`), assume any caller that concatenates the two will eventually leak the synthetic identity into a context where it does not belong -- compose at the call site with intent, never in the generator; (ii) when LEMMY/ANNOUNCER and "everyone else" branches diverge in the same function, audit the divergence -- the special-case branches usually got the careful review and the default branch got the cargo-culted leftovers; (iii) random-name pools must be split by gender if any caller has a `gender_hint` parameter, otherwise gender_hint will eventually contradict the chosen name (VANCE -> NORA SATO -> Male is the canary). Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `name-namespace-leak` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-064; tags `name-namespace-leak`, `procedural-augmentation`, `gender-pool-mixing`, `default-branch-cargo-cult`, `treatment-file-myth`, `cast-description`, `visual-vs-audio-divergence`.
- **Tags:** name-namespace-leak, procedural-augmentation, gender-pool-mixing, default-branch-cargo-cult, treatment-file-myth, cast-description, visual-vs-audio-divergence, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-063: Dialogue name normalizer was the promised single-point-of-truth for format drift but it did NOT recognize `[NAME, mood] text` shorthand, so WORD_ENFORCEMENT's downstream counter saw a clean Mistral script as "ZERO CHARACTER DIALOGUE DETECTED" and dumped a perfect 6-character 18-line draft into a broken rescue path that threw itself away [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-24 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Run #4 on `v2.0-alpha @ 5c22eb6` (post BUG-060/061/062 fixes) produced a rich script with six characters speaking in perfect shorthand format (screenshot shows `[MINDSY, Female, 30s, Urgent, Determined] Kane, I've crunched the numbers!`, `[KANE, Male, 40s, Stern, Skeptical] Not happening, Mindy.`, `[DALE, Male, 60s, Enthusiastic, Excitable] ...`, `[ZURI, Female, 30s, Commanding, Conciliatory] ...`, `[UNKNOWN VOICE, Static-laced, Urgent] ...`, etc. across ~18 clearly-tagged dialogue lines). WORD_ENFORCEMENT's raw-word counter then reported `0 words vs 700 target (0%) | @140wpm -> ~0.0 min [0 lines detected]` and triggered the rescue pipeline: `ZERO CHARACTER DIALOGUE DETECTED - script contains only SFX/ANNOUNCER/atmosphere. WORD_EXTEND will attempt full dialogue generation from cast roster.` WORD_EXTEND then called the LLM once more, produced 18 clean `KANE: ...` / `MINDY: ...` rescue lines, and IMMEDIATELY threw them away with `WORD_EXTEND: Only 0 valid lines - keeping original` -- leaving the final post-extension count at 0 words. The downstream episode was effectively empty despite two LLM passes having produced beautiful material.
- **Cause:** `_normalize_dialogue_names()` in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` (line ~1289) is the architectural single-point-of-truth for format normalization: its docstring promises `"All downstream consumers (word-count regex, FORMAT_NORM, PARSE) see clean text."` But its `_RE_LLM_DIALOGUE_NAME` regex only recognized four input shapes -- bare `NAME:`, `**NAME**:`, `*NAME*(emotion):`, `NAME_WITH_UNDERSCORE:` -- all of which share a mandatory trailing colon. It did NOT recognize the `[NAME, mood] text` shorthand that Mistral Nemo emits (and which BUG-LOCAL-061 taught the final parser to accept). So the three call sites that invoke this normalizer -- once just before WORD_ENFORCEMENT at line ~3735, once per-live-streaming-line at ~1895, once on WORD_EXTEND LLM output at ~6245 -- all handed downstream counters text that still contained bracket-shorthand, which the counters (`_RE_BARE_DIALOGUE` + `_RE_VOICE_TAG_DIALOGUE`) do not match. Net effect: the BUG-061 parser fix worked at the final PARSE step but every intermediate counter between ScriptBody output and PARSE was still blind, so the WORD_ENFORCEMENT safety gate flagged good scripts as empty and dumped them into the WORD_EXTEND escape hatch, which itself had matching-regex problems.
- **Fix:** Added a second regex + pass inside `_normalize_dialogue_names()` so the normalizer's own promise holds. Diff in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py`:
    1. Added `_BRACKET_STRUCTURAL_TOKENS` frozenset (ENV/SFX/MUSIC/VOICE/ACT/SCENE/BEAT/PAUSE/TRANSITION/CONTINUED/CONT/END/FADE/CUT/INT/EXT/TITLE/NOTE/TARGET/STYLE/NARRATOR/SYSTEM_SENTINEL/OPENING/CLOSING/INTERSTITIAL) to exclude structural bracketed tags from name-rewriting.
    2. Added `_RE_LLM_BRACKET_NAME_DIALOGUE = re.compile(r'^\[([A-Z][A-Z0-9_ ]{1,25})(?:,\s*[^\]]*?)?\]\s+(?=\S)', re.MULTILINE)` to match `[NAME, traits] dialogue-follows` at the start of a line.
    3. Rewrote `_normalize_dialogue_names()` to run a two-pass normalization: Pass 1 rewrites bracket-shorthand to `NAME: ` (skipping structural tokens via the frozenset check); Pass 2 is the existing colon/bold/underscore normalizer on the now-flattened text. Both passes are idempotent, so scripts already in canonical form are unaffected.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Python 3.12.11. (b) Direct smoke test against run #4's actual screenshot text: 7 bare-colon dialogue starts detected after normalization where there were 0 before (MINDSY x2, KANE, DALE, ZURI, UNKNOWN VOICE, MOCK COLON). `[SFX: ...]` and `[VOICE: GEMMA STYLE, ...]` both passed through untouched as intended. (c) Full regression: `bug_bible_regression.py + test_core.py + test_audio_byte_identical.py + test_otr_video_plan.py + test_otr_shot_duration_calculator.py + test_character_regression.py + test_dropdown_guardrails.py` = 286 passed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed / 0 failed. (d) Live: next FULL run should show `WORD_ENFORCEMENT: N words vs 700 target` with N matching the actual dialogue volume, not 0.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Three generalizable rules: (i) when a function's docstring claims to be a single-point-of-truth ("all downstream consumers see clean text"), the test battery must exercise EVERY observed LLM output format against it -- a new format the model starts emitting is ABS the normalizer silently missing and every downstream counter silently lying; (ii) parser fixes at the final stage do not propagate backwards into intermediate counters -- if you fix parser regex A to accept a new format, audit every regex between ScriptBody and PARSE for the same blind spot; (iii) rescue pipelines (WORD_EXTEND, BUG-024 fallback) with their own format regexes need the same audit -- they tend to be written once and never revisited when format support widens. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `parser-drift` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-063; tags `parser-drift`, `single-point-of-truth-failure`, `rescue-path-regex-blindness`, `mistral-nemo`, `word-enforcement`, `word-extend`, `silent-false-zero`, `audit-the-audit`.
- **Tags:** parser-drift, single-point-of-truth-failure, rescue-path-regex-blindness, mistral-nemo, word-enforcement, word-extend, silent-false-zero, audit-the-audit, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-062: ScriptBody LLM prompt actively fought Mistral's natural format and had no narration-only guard, producing 0-dialogue episodes roughly every other run and hard-failing the downstream BUG-061 parser fix [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-24 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** FULL run #2 on `v2.0-alpha @ e3d6140` (post BUG-060/061 fixes) produced a pure-narration episode: `ScriptWriter DONE: ... | 3 scenes | 0 dialogue lines | Characters: none` at line 12:15:21. The LLM emitted only `=== SCENE N ===`, `[ENV: ...]`, `[SFX: ...]`, `=== ACT N ===` markers plus 2 ANNOUNCER bookends -- no character-to-character conversation at all. The Grammarian received 2 dialogue lines, the parser had nothing to recover, BatchBark reported `Found 0 dialogue lines`, SceneSequencer rendered 1 TTS + 7 SFX + 2 ANNOUNCER, and the final MP4 was 48.5s instead of the expected ~7 min. The BUG-061 parser fix was exonerated by replaying run #1's script body through the patched parser and seeing 41 dialogue lines extracted from what previously yielded 0 -- confirming the parser works and the generation path is where the remaining failures live.
- **Cause:** A prompt audit of the full pipeline (20 LLM prompts across NewsSummary, 3 spine variants, evaluator, ScriptBody, critique, revise, normalize, grammarian, cast-extension, rescue, announcer, Director) revealed three systemic gaps:
    1. **Min-line floor only in 1 of 20 prompts.** Only `SCRIPT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` had "at least {min_lines} dialogue lines"; user prompts, spine prompts, and the spine evaluator did not re-assert it. LLMs under-weight system-prompt rules vs user-prompt rules on short calls.
    2. **ZERO prompts warned against narration-only output.** Across all 20 prompts, not one explicitly forbade "a script with only ANNOUNCER/narrator lines." The failure mode was unnamed in the prompt set, so Mistral treated it as an allowed outcome when the winning spine emphasized atmosphere.
    3. **ZERO prompts tolerated Mistral's actual format.** Every format-specifying prompt insisted on `[VOICE: NAME, gender, age, tone, energy] dialogue` with the literal `VOICE:` prefix. Mistral Nemo (`mistralai/Mistral-Nemo-Instruct-2407`) emits shorthand `[NAME, gender, age, mood] dialogue` roughly half the time, ignoring the `VOICE:` requirement. The prompts were fighting the model on a format the parser already accepted (post-BUG-061). Most acute location: the `_format_hint` block for `length=short (3 acts)` runs (`nodes/story_orchestrator.py` line ~2917), which said `"CRITICAL FORMAT RULE: Every spoken line MUST use the format 'CHARACTER_NAME: dialogue text'"` -- incorrect for Mistral's behavior, which pushes the model toward either compliance (bare-colon form) or silent refusal (narration-only fallback).
- **Fix:** SIX prompt-text edits in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` plus one two-line regex addition, spanning the full generation -> polish -> normalize chain so the pipeline is LLM-agnostic end-to-end (Mistral + Gemma + any future swap):
    1. **SCRIPT_SYSTEM_PROMPT header** (line ~2216) -- added a top-of-prompt `=== HARD REQUIREMENTS (VALIDATED AUTOMATICALLY) ===` block listing three rules: REQ-1 character dialogue is mandatory (narration-only scripts are rejected), REQ-2 dialogue may be written as EITHER `[VOICE: NAME, ...]` OR `[NAME, ...]` (parser accepts both identically), REQ-3 the NAME slot must be a character name, not a gender/descriptor word. Hard requirements land above the existing Section 1 so they are the first content the LLM attends to.
    2. **_format_hint for 3-act case** (line ~2917) -- rewritten to list BOTH accepted forms and explicitly forbid narration collapse. Old text insisted on bare-colon format which both Mistral AND Gemma ignore fractions of the time; new text embraces either form ("pick one and use it consistently") and names the failure mode by its shape so the LLM can recognize and avoid it.
    3. **eval_prompt criterion #7** (line ~4246) -- added DIALOGUE DENSITY (HARD FLOOR) as an explicit selection criterion for the spine evaluator so atmosphere-heavy narration-only outlines cannot win the 3-spine competition. Wording: "The winner MUST support character-to-character conversation across the whole runtime... If a candidate puts all the action into [SFX:] cues and leaves characters silent, penalize it heavily."
    4. **Grammarian pre-count regex** (line ~5805) -- added a third pattern `_pre_shorthand` that counts `[NAME, mood] dialogue` inline shorthand. Old regex only counted `NAME:` bare-colon and `[VOICE:` tags, so when the upstream LLM emitted 40+ shorthand lines the Grammarian's loss-check silently passed with `pre_total=0`. Now all three forms contribute to the count that Grammarian must preserve.
    5. **Grammarian prompts single + chunked** (line ~5841, ~6000) -- updated both the preservation list ("[SFX:], [ENV:], [VOICE:], [NAME,...]") and FIX rule #2 to list all three equivalent dialogue tag forms (A/B/C) with explicit "do NOT rewrite A->B or B->C; that conversion belongs to the normalizer, not here." Prevents Grammarian from silently stripping mood/age/gender metadata from shorthand tags.
    6. **normalize_prompt (FORMAT_NORM)** (line ~5502) -- added an "Accepted INPUT forms that you may need to convert" subsection explicitly listing `[CHARACTER NAME, traits] dialogue` shorthand and the `**NAME:**` markdown-bolded form (Gemma variant), with the conversion targets named. Two output forms stay (`[VOICE:...]` + `CHARACTER:`); three input forms are now recognized + converted. Added "Never drop or invent dialogue during conversion" as an explicit guardrail.

    All six edits are prompt-text only with one two-line regex addition; no control-flow changes; no new code paths. Same regression suite covers them.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean (Windows Python 3.12.11, 390904 chars, no BOM). (b) All three edit markers present in the file (`HARD REQUIREMENTS (VALIDATED AUTOMATICALLY)`, `BUG-LOCAL-062 fix (2026-04-24)`, `DIALOGUE DENSITY (HARD FLOOR)`). (c) Full regression: Bug Bible 23 passed / 1 skipped / 2 xfailed; `test_core + test_audio_byte_identical + test_otr_video_plan + test_otr_shot_duration_calculator + test_character_regression` 213 passed / 1 skipped; total 236 passed, 2 skipped, 2 xfailed, 0 failed. (d) Live verification: next FULL run should (1) not produce `0 dialogue lines` DONE telemetry, (2) the spine evaluator should not pick narration-heavy winners, (3) the downstream MP4 should clock closer to target word-count * 0.43 seconds (for 700 words = ~5 min) rather than the 48.5s degenerate case.
- **Bible promotion notes:** Bible candidate. Three generalizable rules worth Bible entries: (i) when a prompt enumerates a rigid format an LLM ignores, either accept the LLM's preferred form OR add a hard post-generation gate that rejects + regenerates -- fighting the model silently yields intermittent zero-output runs; (ii) every failure mode must be NAMED in the prompt or the model treats it as allowed; (iii) hard requirements front-load: put acceptance criteria at the top of the system prompt, not buried in numbered sections. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `prompt-engineering` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-062; tags `prompt-engineering`, `mistral-nemo`, `narration-only-fallback`, `format-drift`, `silent-failure`, `front-load-requirements`, `name-the-failure`.
- **Tags:** prompt-engineering, mistral-nemo, narration-only-fallback, format-drift, silent-failure, front-load-requirements, name-the-failure, spine-evaluator, regression-coverage-gap, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-061: Mistral Nemo's `[CHARACTER, traits] dialogue` inline form bypassed every voice-tag pattern, producing `[BatchBark] Found 0 dialogue lines` and a 48s episode instead of ~7 min [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-24 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** First post-cleanup FULL run on `v2.0-alpha` (workflow `otr_scifi_16gb_full.json`, 16 nodes, commit `7c5415f` + handoff `6d170fc`) produced a 48.5s / 38.4 MB MP4 instead of the expected ~7 min episode. Console showed: `[BUG-024] Zero character dialogue in raw script. Cast roster fallback will be used for extension. Pre-rolled cast: EDNA BEESLY, BOB KAPOOR, KARNACKI STEINER, EDWARD HALPERT`, then `[BatchBark] Found 0 dialogue lines in Canonical 1.0 format (skipped 2 ANNOUNCER lines - routed to Kokoro bus)`, then `[SceneSequencer] Pre-rendered clips: 1 TTS, 7 SFX, 2 ANNOUNCER`. Kokoro got the 2 ANNOUNCER lines, AudioGen got 7 SFX cues, and exactly one TTS clip survived from a 59-line script. The other ~50 dialogue lines were dropped silently. The four "green signals" all fired correctly (no sidecar errors, PASS2/PASS3 nonzero, BatchFluxRender processed all 9 shots), but the final episode contained almost no character dialogue.
- **Cause:** The script-canonicalizer in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` (`_parse_script_to_canonical`-style block at line ~6440-6610) had four voice-tag patterns -- `[VOICE: NAME, traits] dialogue` (v1), `[VOICE: NAME] dialogue` (v2), `[VOICE: NAME, traits]` standalone-with-lookahead (v3), and `[NAME, traits]` standalone-with-lookahead (v4 shorthand) -- but **no pattern for `[NAME, traits] dialogue` inline on a single line**. Mistral Nemo (`mistralai/Mistral-Nemo-Instruct-2407`) emits this exact shorthand-inline form for character dialogue (e.g. `[EDNA, Female, 40s, urgent] Dammit!`, `[BOB, Male, 30s, clipped] Edna, it's good to hear your voice.`). Every line of this form fell through all four patterns and landed in the v5 bare-`NAME:` matcher, which doesn't accept bracketed names, then through to the structural-direction fallback at line 6608 -- silently registered as `{"type": "direction", "text": "[EDNA, Female, 40s, urgent] Dammit!"}` instead of `{"type": "dialogue", ...}`. BatchBark's filter `if item.get("type") == "dialogue"` then skipped them. The model's revision-pass output (line 6502 of the live log) was the trigger because the Critique-Revise loop tightened format compliance toward `[CHARACTER, mood] text` rather than `CHARACTER: text` -- the cleaner mood-tagged form that humans prefer is the form the parser couldn't read.
- **Fix:** `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` -- added a new fifth voice pattern `voice_shorthand_inline_pat = re.compile(r'^\[([A-Z][A-Z0-9_ ]{1,20})(?:,\s*(.+?))?\]\s*(.+)$', re.IGNORECASE)` immediately after `voice_shorthand_pat`, plus a matcher block inserted **before** the v4 standalone block in the parser loop. The matcher rejects structural bracketed tokens (`ENV`, `SFX`, `MUSIC`, `BEAT`, `PAUSE`, `ACT`, `SCENE`, `TRANSITION`, `CONTINUED`, `CONT`, `END`, `FADE`, `CUT`, `INT`, `EXT`, `VOICE`) so that `[ACT TWO] description` and `[VOICE: ...]` don't false-match (the `[VOICE: ...]` forms are still handled by v1/v2/v3 first; the `INT`/`EXT` exclusion is for screenplay slugs). Exits cleanly with `i += 1; continue` so the parser advances normally. Same `_GENDER_WORDS` fallback to `CHAR_A`-style names if the bracket name slot contains a descriptor word like "WOMAN" instead of a real character name.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Windows Python 3.12.11. (b) Direct regex smoke: 5/5 cases pass for `[EDNA, Female, 40s, urgent] Dammit!`, `[BOB, Male, 30s, clipped] Edna, it's good to hear your voice.`, `[KARNACKI, Male, 70s, raspy, exhausted] Edna, we're losing altitude.`, `[ANNOUNCER, Female, 50s, grave] Tonight, Houston.`, `[EDNA, startled] Bob? What are you doing here?`. Each captures `(name, traits, dialogue)` correctly. (c) Bug Bible regression: 23 passed, 1 skipped, 2 xfailed (xfails pre-existing, unchanged from baseline). (d) Dropdown guardrails: 50/50 passed. (e) `tests/test_core.py + test_audio_byte_identical.py + test_otr_shot_duration_calculator.py + test_otr_video_plan.py + test_character_regression.py`: 213 passed, 1 skipped (after fixing pre-existing stale allow-list in `test_node_types_otr_or_known` to include `CheckpointLoaderSimple` and `SaveImage`). (f) Live verification: pending next FULL queue -- expect `[BatchBark] Found 50+ dialogue lines in Canonical 1.0 format` instead of the prior `Found 0`, and a final MP4 in the 6-8 minute range instead of 48.5s.
- **Bible promotion notes:** This is a **Bible candidate**. The generalizable rule: any custom-node pack that asks an LLM for free-form dialogue and then parses bracketed character tags must enumerate every form the LLM actually emits, not just the form documented in the prompt. Mistral Nemo's preferred form is `[CHARACTER, mood-list] dialogue` inline -- different from Gemma's `[VOICE: NAME, traits] dialogue` and different again from Llama's `**NAME:** dialogue`. Add a regression entry that asserts the parser produces `dialogue_count > 0` on each model family's canonical output sample. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `script-parser` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-061; tags `script-parser`, `mistral-nemo`, `voice-tag-regex`, `silent-dialogue-drop`, `bug-024-family`, `bracket-format-drift`. Promote alongside a fixture file `tests/fixtures/parser_corpus/mistral_nemo_inline_shorthand.txt` containing the canonical Mistral Nemo output that triggers this case, so the regression survives any future LLM swap.
- **Tags:** script-parser, mistral-nemo, voice-tag-regex, silent-dialogue-drop, bug-024-family, bracket-format-drift, canonical-1.0, batchbark, regression-coverage-gap, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-060: Procedural cast generator overrode the Director's correct voice_assignments because gender-hint regex only scanned for `[VOICE: NAME, gender, ...]` and not for the shorthand or for the Director's own notes field [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-24 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Same FULL run as BUG-LOCAL-061. The LLMDirector emitted a structurally clean `voice_assignments` block: `EDNA -> v2/en_speaker_2 (Female, 40s, urgent)`, `BOB -> v2/en_speaker_1 (Male, 30s, clipped)`, `KARNACKI -> v2/en_speaker_5 (Male, 70s, raspy, exhausted)` -- correct genders for all three. The procedural cast pass then **overrode all three** with rotation-driven picks: `EDNA -> v2/en_speaker_6 (NEMO BOUVIER, male, 40s, stoic)`, `BOB -> v2/en_speaker_7 (PALMER SIRIKIT, female, 20s, sharp)`, `KARNACKI -> v2/en_speaker_4 (YURI TANAKA, female, 30s, wry)`. Net result: female protagonist got a male voice, two male characters got female voices. Trigger line in the log: `[LLMDirector] Parsed 0 gender hints from script: {}`. The hint-extraction regex found nothing in the script body and let `_generate_character_profile` rotate gender procedurally without a hint to anchor it.
- **Cause:** `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` line ~7113 had `voice_tag_re = re.compile(r"\[VOICE:\s*([A-Z][A-Z0-9_ ]*?)\s*,\s*(male|female)\b", re.IGNORECASE)` -- only matched the `[VOICE: NAME, gender, ...]` canonical tag form. Mistral Nemo's revision pass had stripped the `VOICE:` prefix and emitted shorthand `[NAME, gender, ...]` instead, so the regex matched zero lines, gender_map was empty, and the procedural pass at `_randomize_character_names()` in line ~7283 fell back to seeded random gender selection per character. The Director's structurally-correct `voice_assignments` block -- which **already** contained the right genders in its `notes` field for every character -- was discarded as "placeholder presets" without consulting it. By design the Director's voice picks are treated as overridable; the bug is that the procedural-vs-Director priority fence had no fallback path to consult the Director's `notes` when the script-side regex came up empty.
- **Fix:** Extended gender-map extraction in `nodes/story_orchestrator.py` to scan **three sources in priority order** (first hit per character name wins):
