    1. **Source 1 (existing):** `[VOICE: NAME, gender, ...]` canonical tags via the original `voice_tag_re`.
    2. **Source 2 (new):** `[NAME, gender, ...]` shorthand tags via a new `shorthand_gender_re = re.compile(r"\[([A-Z][A-Z0-9_ ]{1,20})\s*,\s*(male|female)\b", re.IGNORECASE)`. Skips structural tokens (`ENV`, `SFX`, `MUSIC`, `BEAT`, `PAUSE`, `ACT`, `SCENE`, `TRANSITION`, `CONTINUED`, `CONT`, `END`, `FADE`, `CUT`, `INT`, `EXT`, `OPENING`, `CLOSING`, `INTERSTITIAL`, `TITLE`, `NOTE`, `TARGET`, `STYLE`, `NARRATOR`, `SYSTEM_SENTINEL`, `VOICE`).
    3. **Source 3 (new):** Director's own `plan["voice_assignments"][NAME]["notes"]` field, scanned with `\bfemale\b` / `\bmale\b` whole-word matches. This is the backstop that finally lets a structurally-clean Director plan win over procedural rotation.
   The new `log.info` line reports per-source hit counts: `Parsed N gender hints (voice_tags=X, shorthand=Y, director_notes=Z): {...}`. No change to `_randomize_character_names` priority logic -- only the input data widens.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on Windows Python 3.12.11. (b) Module imports without error from a fresh subprocess. (c) Bug Bible regression, dropdown guardrails, and core+character_regression+otr_video_plan+otr_shot_duration_calculator+audio_byte_identical suites: all green (286 passed across all four suites). (d) Live verification: pending next FULL queue -- expect `[LLMDirector] Parsed 4 gender hints (voice_tags=0, shorthand=N, director_notes=M): {EDNA: female, BOB: male, KARNACKI: male, ANNOUNCER: ...}` and procedural-cast log lines that match the Director's intended genders, e.g. `EDNA - voice: v2/en_speaker_X (profile: ..., female, 40s, ...)` instead of `(profile: ..., male, ...)`.
- **Bible promotion notes:** This is a **Bible candidate**. The generalizable rule: when an LLM produces structured output (e.g. JSON with character metadata) and a downstream procedural pass is meant to override only stylistic choices (voice preset, age, demeanor) while preserving load-bearing constraints (gender), the procedural pass must **consult the LLM's own structured metadata as a fallback** before reverting to seeded randomness. Don't trust regex on the raw text alone; it's brittle to format drift across LLM updates. Sister repo: add to `BUG_BIBLE.yaml` under `procedural-cast` area; legacy id BUG-LOCAL-060; tags `procedural-cast`, `gender-hint`, `voice-assignment`, `mistral-nemo`, `bug-004-family`, `priority-fence`. Promote alongside a regression entry that feeds a synthetic Director plan with shorthand-only script and asserts gender_map contains all four entries.
- **Tags:** procedural-cast, gender-hint, voice-assignment, mistral-nemo, bug-004-family, priority-fence, director-vs-procedural, regression-coverage-gap, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-059: Workflow JSON widgets_values misaligned when a node has a field named `seed` -- ComfyUI auto-inserts a hidden `control_after_generate` string slot and shifts every subsequent widget by one [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-23 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** First live run of the Option A batch-render graph (`workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_TEST.json` at commit `2e8d63b`, OTR_BatchFluxRender id=105 driving a 4-shot batch) errored at the ComfyUI validation stage with a cluster of type-mismatch messages the moment Run was clicked -- before any FLUX weights loaded, before any stderr from the node's `execute`. Error panel showed simultaneously: `scheduler: '1024' not in ['simple','sgm_uniform',...]`, `Failed to convert an input value to a FLOAT value: guidance, cinematic 35mm film still, dimly lit starship bridge, ...`, `cfg, euler, could not convert string to float: 'euler'`, `sampler_name: 'simple' not in (list of length 44)`, `height: Value 3 smaller than min of 256`. Pattern: the values stored in `widgets_values` were each being read one slot to the right of where the node expected them -- `sampler_name`'s position was being read as `cfg`, `scheduler`'s position as `sampler_name`, and so on. The `3` in the height error was the integer truncation of `3.5` (the guidance value, read where height was expected).
- **Cause:** ComfyUI's frontend auto-injects a hidden `control_after_generate` string widget into every node that declares an `INT` widget literally named `seed` (values `"randomize" | "fixed" | "increment" | "decrement"`). The injection happens in the frontend's widget registration pass, not in the node author's `INPUT_TYPES()` -- so the author never sees it and the declared widget count is off-by-one from what the scheduler actually expects. The `widgets_values` array in the workflow JSON is purely positional and has no schema -- `[0]..[N-1]` are mapped one-to-one against the frontend-registered widget list at load time. When I wrote the JSON patch for OTR_BatchFluxRender I enumerated the positional values in `INPUT_TYPES()` declaration order (`script_json, batch_limit, seed, steps, cfg, sampler_name, scheduler, width, height, guidance, fallback_prompt, style_suffix, freeze_seed`) and produced a 13-entry array. ComfyUI's loader expected 14 entries -- the hidden `control_after_generate` slot is at index 3 (right after seed), which shifted `steps → [4]`, `cfg → [5]`, `sampler_name → [6]`, ... `freeze_seed → [13]`. Every subsequent widget validated against the wrong value. The shift was silent because type coercion produced legible error messages downstream (`"euler"` as cfg, `1024` as scheduler) instead of naming the real root cause. Same class of bug potentially affects any future OTR custom node that exposes a `seed` widget -- and also any PR that edits a stock-node-containing workflow JSON by hand.
- **Fix:** `workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_TEST.json` -- inserted `"randomize"` at `widgets_values[3]` so every downstream widget slot recovers its correct index. Commit `ebd5702`. Verified the full 14-entry layout: `[0]script_json="" [1]batch_limit=4 [2]seed=1 [3]control_after_generate="randomize" [4]steps=20 [5]cfg=1.0 [6]sampler_name="euler" [7]scheduler="simple" [8]width=1024 [9]height=1024 [10]guidance=3.5 [11]fallback_prompt=... [12]style_suffix=... [13]freeze_seed=false`. Also recovered from a OneDrive sync race that truncated the trailing `"version": 0.4` line during the Edit roundtrip.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean; JSON parses with 9 nodes, 9 links, `last_node_id=105`. (b) Regression: `test_core.py + test_dropdown_guardrails.py + test_workflow_json_guardrails.py` = 150 passed / 23 skipped. (c) Node-level visual check: in ComfyUI Desktop the Batch FLUX Render node shows correct values on every widget (`euler` on sampler_name, `simple` on scheduler, `1024` on width/height, `3.5` on guidance), no red Error badge. (d) Graph validation no longer reports any type-mismatch errors. (e) Actual 4-shot render pending; this fix only clears the graph-validation gate.
- **Bible promotion notes (when verified via a clean live run):** The generalizable rule is worth a standalone Bible entry: **any node whose INPUT_TYPES declares an `INT` widget named literally `"seed"` will silently receive a ComfyUI-injected `control_after_generate` string slot immediately after it in `widgets_values`; the JSON-author must account for this slot or every downstream widget will validate against the wrong value.** Should be promoted to the Bible alongside a schema-validation test (see below). Legacy id: BUG-LOCAL-059. Tags: `comfyui-frontend`, `widgets_values`, `seed-auto-inject`, `control_after_generate`, `positional-widget-array`, `workflow-json`, `schema-drift`, `silent-validation-error`.
- **Missing regression (followup task):** The existing `tests/test_workflow_json_guardrails.py` checks that `widgets_values` *exists* on each node but does NOT cross-check it against the node class's declared `INPUT_TYPES()` plus ComfyUI's auto-injected hidden widgets (the `seed -> control_after_generate` injection being the primary example, but the same injection applies to any widget named `noise_seed`). This bug shipped because the test has no semantic coverage. Proposed followup: add `tests/test_widget_value_alignment.py` that for every OTR_* node referenced in every `workflows/*.json`:
    1. Import the node class and read `INPUT_TYPES()`.
    2. Build the expected positional schema: required widgets (non-linked) in declaration order, plus auto-injected slots for any `seed`/`noise_seed` INT, plus optional widgets.
    3. For each entry in the node's `widgets_values`, assert the stored value's Python type matches the declared spec (INT -> int, FLOAT -> float, STRING -> str, dropdown -> value-in-list, BOOLEAN -> bool) and falls within any declared min/max.
    4. Assert the array length equals the expected schema length.
  Promote to the Bible alongside the BUG-LOCAL-059 entry. Sister test in the survival-guide repo should carry the same regression so the guardrail travels with any new node pack derived from the OTR template.
- **Tags:** comfyui-frontend, widgets_values, seed-auto-inject, control_after_generate, positional-widget-array, workflow-json, schema-drift, silent-validation-error, widget-alignment, otr-batch-flux-render, bible-candidate, missing-regression

### BUG-LOCAL-058: OneDrive sync race truncates `"version": 0.4` from workflow JSON tail during Edit roundtrip [FIXED, MONITOR]
- **Date:** 2026-04-23 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** no (environmental)
- **Symptom:** Mid-session Edit of `workflows/otr_scifi_16gb_TEST.json` (single-line change: insert `"randomize"` in `widgets_values`) left the file parseable by the Edit tool's preview but broken on disk. `json.load()` in bash sandbox raised `Expecting ',' delimiter: line 518 column 4 (char 19824)`. Tail of file showed 517 lines ending at the `}` that closes `extra.info`, missing the expected trailing `,\n  "version": 0.4\n}\n`. Read tool had earlier shown the file ending at line 519/520 with `"version": 0.4` present -- so the Edit produced a valid in-memory result but the OneDrive filesystem landed a truncated copy.
- **Cause:** The OTR repo lives under `C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\custom_nodes\ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio`, which is inside Jeffrey's OneDrive-synced `Documents` folder. OneDrive's file replica layer occasionally commits partial writes during rapid roundtrips between sandbox VFS, Windows filesystem, and the OneDrive sync daemon. The Edit atomic-write completed on the Windows side but the sync daemon truncated the replica copy before the final chunk flushed -- leaving bash (which reads through the sandbox VFS) pointing at a stale / partial copy of the file while the Read tool cached the full version. Specific to environments where a git-managed repo lives under an active cloud-sync tree.
- **Fix:** Recovery script reattached the missing tail programmatically (`\n  },\n  "version": 0.4\n}\n`) then re-verified JSON parses cleanly. Did not attempt to "disable OneDrive" or move the repo out of the synced tree -- that's Jeffrey's decision, not a bug fix.
- **Verify:** (a) File parses; 9 nodes, 9 links, `last_node_id=105`. (b) Next git commit diff showed only the intended `"randomize"` insertion, no phantom tail change.
- **Monitor:** If this recurs when editing large JSON under the OTR tree, options are: (1) pause OneDrive sync during edits (`Pause syncing 1 hour` in OneDrive tray), (2) move the ComfyUI repo tree to `C:\ComfyUI\...` outside OneDrive, (3) use Desktop Commander `edit_block` instead of the sandbox Edit tool for JSON changes so the write lands Windows-native without the sandbox VFS hop.
- **Tags:** onedrive, filesystem-race, partial-write, editor-roundtrip, json-truncation, workflow-file, environmental, not-bible

### BUG-LOCAL-057: flux_anchor silent-zero-render -- per-shot exceptions suppressed by meta.json overwrite + no stderr logging [INSTRUMENTED 2026-04-22]
- **Date:** 2026-04-22 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** TEST workflow smoke run on commit `acb5d3b` (after BUG-LOCAL-056 path fix): FLUX loads cleanly (`[flux_anchor] loaded pipeline load_mode=fp8_torchao`), no exception in stderr, `[flux_anchor] pipe released` fires, video_stack chain proceeds through ltx_motion + wan21_loop, all three inter-stage barriers show flat `vram_free=14973MB / 16303MB` (identical across three stages = no GPU work happened). VisualRenderer sees 9 empty shot subdirs with only `meta.json` (backend=wan21_loop, error=still_missing) and falls back to procedural video. STATUS.json overwritten with `video_stack READY: 9 shots, motion_promoted=0, missing=9, wall_clock_s=37.6` -- 37.6s total is far too fast for any of FLUX/LTX/Wan to have denoised. Job `vs_7e1d81712d41`, 22:33:55 2026-04-22. End-to-end chain "ran" but produced zero visuals.
- **Cause:** Two compounding defects mask the real failure: (1) `flux_anchor._render_real` logs NOTHING to stderr inside the `for i, shot in enumerate(shots)` loop -- no entry, no per-shot start, no exception handler logging, no summary. Per-shot errors are written ONLY to `<shot_id>/meta.json` via the `except Exception` handler. (2) The chained video_stack pipeline has each backend (`flux_anchor` -> `ltx_motion` -> `wan21_loop`) write to the same shared `<shot_id>/meta.json`; `wan21_loop` overwrites flux_anchor's meta with its own `error: "still_missing"` when no upstream render.png is present. Net result: if every FLUX shot raises an exception (OOM, torchao deserialization, autocast/device mismatch, anything), flux_anchor writes 9 meta.jsons with the real error -- then wan21_loop overwrites all 9 with "still_missing" -- then video_stack writes the final STATUS -- and nothing ever reached stderr. Forensic trail wiped between the bug firing and us reading the filesystem. Not a VRAMCoordinator lock issue (run wall clock was 37.6s, far below the 1800s timeout). Not a shotlist-empty issue (9 shots present, `_build_prompt` reads `env_prompt` + `camera` correctly). Not BUG-LOCAL-046 family stub fallback (`load_mode=fp8_torchao` logged, not "stub"). Real root cause still unknown because the logs don't survive the run.
- **Fix (Step 1 of 2 -- INSTRUMENTATION):** `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py::_render_real` -- added stderr logging at every transition: `_render_real entered` (with shot count + load_mode + out_dir), `VRAMCoordinator acquired`, per-shot `start` (with prompt_len + seed), per-shot `OOM` / `ERRORED` with elapsed time + exception class + message, per-shot `rendered in Xs`, and `_render_real done` summary with rendered/oom/errored counts. Additionally duplicated each per-shot meta.json write to a stage-scoped `<shot_id>/flux_anchor.meta.json` file that downstream backends do NOT overwrite. Next time this bug fires we'll see exactly which exception class + message for which shot.
- **Fix (Step 2 of 2 -- ROOT CAUSE):** PENDING -- requires one diagnostic run with the new instrumentation to reveal the actual per-shot exception. Candidates to investigate once we have the exception class: (a) `out.images[0]` IndexError if FluxPipeline returned empty list under FP8 torchao path, (b) generator device mismatch (`torch.Generator(device="cuda")` under a torchao-offloaded pipeline that moved transformer to CPU), (c) FluxPipeline rejecting the prompt string somehow, (d) hardcoded `num_inference_steps=20, guidance_scale=3.5` incompatible with the torchao FP8 checkpoint version (deprecation warning stored_version=1 vs current_default_version=2 was already logged as non-fatal, but could correlate).
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on patched `flux_anchor.py`. (b) Regression green: `tests/test_flux_anchor.py tests/test_core.py tests/test_dropdown_guardrails.py tests/test_workflow_json_guardrails.py` = 181 passed / 2 skipped. (c) Next live TEST workflow run should show per-shot stderr lines like `[flux_anchor] shot 1/9 s01_01 prompt_len=NNN seed=XXX start` followed by either `rendered in Xs` or `ERRORED after Xs: SomeException: msg...`. (d) `flux_anchor.meta.json` files in each shot dir survive the wan21_loop overwrite and can be inspected post-run.
- **Related:** Architectural insight from `feedback_comfyui_native_over_diffusers_torchao.md` -- the sidecar's Diffusers + torchao + accelerate path is exactly where this class of invisible failure keeps surfacing. ComfyUI-native `CheckpointLoaderSimple` path rendered the same FLUX FP8 model in 44.62s tonight (Prompt executed in 44.62 seconds, 11350 MB peak) with zero BUG-046-family fragility.
- **Tags:** flux-anchor, silent-stub, meta-overwrite, stderr-logging, observability, video-stack, chained-backend, torchao, fp8, diagnostic-instrumentation, bug-046-family, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-056: Phantom `custom_nodes\io\` module -- `_OTR_ROOT` path arithmetic off-by-one puts the sidecar exchange dir one level too high [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-22 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** ComfyUI startup logs a non-fatal but noisy custom-node import failure on every launch: `FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: 'C:\\Users\\jeffr\\Documents\\ComfyUI\\custom_nodes\\io\\__init__.py'` followed by `Cannot import C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\custom_nodes\io module for custom nodes` and `0.0 seconds (IMPORT FAILED): C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\custom_nodes\io`. Filesystem check confirmed a populated `C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\custom_nodes\io\` directory containing 13 historical visual sidecar jobs worth of data (visual_in/vs_*, visual_out/vs_*, STATUS.json, render.png, _temp_clip.mp4 etc) -- sidecar artifacts were being written one directory up from where the design doc puts them. ComfyUI's custom-node loader scans every top-level folder under `custom_nodes/` for an `__init__.py`, finds none, logs the failure, and moves on; nothing else breaks because the sidecar code writes and reads from the same (wrong) location consistently. Cosmetic on the surface, but the real `ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio/io/` directory gets ignored entirely -- any Phase E disk-hygiene sweep scoped to the repo root misses the actual working set.
- **Cause:** Every module under `visual/` that computes the repo root used `Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent`. That is one `parent` too many: `visual/poll.py` lives at `custom_nodes/ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio/visual/poll.py`, so `.parent` is `visual/`, `.parent.parent` is the repo root `ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio/`, and `.parent.parent.parent` overshoots to `custom_nodes/`. Affected files: `visual/poll.py:34`, `visual/bridge.py:41`, `visual/renderer.py:32`, `visual/vram_coordinator.py:60`, `visual/wedge_probe.py:343`. The `_IO_IN = _OTR_ROOT / "io" / "visual_in"` and related constants all resolved to `custom_nodes/io/...` instead of `custom_nodes/ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio/io/...`. The downstream `comfyui_venv = _OTR_ROOT.parent.parent / ".venv"` computation in `bridge.py:426` was coincidentally *broken the same amount in the opposite direction* (resolved to `Documents/.venv` which doesn't exist) -- fixing the root correctly restores it to `Documents/ComfyUI/.venv/Scripts/python.exe`, so the fix self-heals a second latent bug without extra edits. Related patterns in `visual/worker.py`, `visual/anchor_gen.py`, `visual/backends/_base.py` that start from `job_dir` (itself rooted at `io/visual_in/<job_id>/`) correctly use `parent.parent.parent` -- those are 3 levels deep so the `parent` count is right; do not touch them.
- **Fix:** Changed `Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent` to `Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent  # repo root (ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio/)` in all five files. Deleted the accumulated stale `C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\custom_nodes\io\` tree (13 `vs_*` jobs + `vs_pipeguard` / `vs_testjob` / `vs_testjob2`) via `rmdir /s /q` through Desktop Commander. The real `ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio/io/{hyworld_in,hyworld_out,visual_in,visual_out}/` was already present and untouched -- next sidecar run will populate it correctly.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on all five patched files. (b) Regression green on `test_core.py + test_dropdown_guardrails.py + test_workflow_json_guardrails.py` -- 163 passed / 1 skipped in 0.96s after the edit. (c) Phantom directory is gone: `if exist C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\custom_nodes\io (dir) else (echo NOT_PRESENT)` now prints `NOT_PRESENT`. (d) Next ComfyUI restart should log the OTR node count (`[OldTimeRadio] OK - All 21 nodes loaded successfully`) *without* the `custom_nodes\io module` import failure stanza, and any live sidecar run should create its `vs_*` directory under `ComfyUI-OldTimeRadio/io/visual_in/` (inside the repo, git-ignored).
- **Tags:** path-arithmetic, parent-parent-parent, visual-sidecar, bridge, poll, renderer, vram-coordinator, wedge-probe, comfyui-loader, custom-nodes, phantom-module, startup-noise, self-healing-fix, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-055: Fourth ImportError on `uintx_weight_only` -- auto-discovery snake_case can't split acronym-to-word boundary (`UIntX` + `Weight`) [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-21 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** First live `flux_smoke.json` workflow run after the new 2-node smoke harness landed (job `vs_29a6268c992f`, 21:31:42 2026-04-21, backend=flux_anchor) returned `status=READY` in 7.65s with `detail=flux_anchor stub READY: 1 shots (load_error[fp8_torchao]:ImportError:cannot import name 'uintx_weight_only' from 'torchao.quantization' (C:\Users\jeffr\Documents\ComfyUI\.venv\Lib\site-packages\torchao\quantization\__init__.py))`. The BUG-LOCAL-054 auto-discovery shim fired, but FLUX still stub-fell a fourth time. Confirmed via the one-liner check: `torchao: 0.16.0`, `uintx_weight_only present: False`, classes in torchao.quantization matching `/intx/i`: `['GemliteUIntXWeightOnlyConfig', 'Int8DynamicActivationIntxWeightConfig', 'IntxOpaqueTensor', 'IntxUnpackedToInt8Tensor', 'IntxWeightOnlyConfig', 'UIntXWeightOnlyConfig', 'intx_quantization_aware_training']`. Relevant class is `UIntXWeightOnlyConfig`.
- **Cause:** The BUG-LOCAL-054 auto-discovery shim uses a single regex pass `([a-z0-9])([A-Z])` to CamelCase-to-snake_case every `*Config` class. That regex correctly splits at lower-to-upper boundaries but cannot split an acronym-to-word boundary where both characters are uppercase -- `UIntX` + `Weight` has the transition `X→W` (upper→upper), which the regex leaves untouched. Applied to `UIntXWeightOnlyConfig`, the shim strips `Config`, then splits only on the lower-to-upper transitions (`t→X` and `t→O`), producing `UInt_X_Weight_Only` → lowercase → `u_int_x_weight_only`. The FLUX.1-dev-torchao-fp8 checkpoint was pickled against a legacy torchao that exported this helper as the single token `uintx_weight_only` (because "UIntX" is a quantization format name, not a compound word), so pickle's `module.name` lookup for `torchao.quantization.uintx_weight_only` fails even after the shim installed `u_int_x_weight_only` as an alias. Pure regex snake_case has no dictionary to know that `UIntX` is one token; extending the regex with a second acronym-boundary pass `([A-Z])([A-Z][a-z])` would produce `U_IntX_Weight_Only` → `u_intx_weight_only` (still wrong), and no purely-mechanical CamelCase→snake_case can produce `uintx_weight_only` from `UIntXWeightOnlyConfig` without hard-coding the tokenization. Same family as BUG-LOCAL-051/053/054 -- the checkpoint's legacy helper naming is outside what mechanical tokenization can cover.
- **Fix:** `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py::_install_torchao_compat_shim()` -- added an `_EXPLICIT_ACRONYM_ALIASES: dict[str, str]` table mapping known pickled legacy-helper names to their current Config class names (`"uintx_weight_only": "UIntXWeightOnlyConfig"`), applied as a second pass after the auto-discovery loop. Each explicit alias follows the same native/installed/missing contract as auto-discovered ones. Added `uintx_weight_only` to the `canonical` tuple so the shim's structured return tag now surfaces its status (`shim:installed=N;native=M;...;uintx_weight_only=I/N/-`). Kept auto-discovery as the first pass -- it still handles 17+ helpers cleanly -- and the explicit table is scoped to genuine acronym edge cases. Extend the table when a new edge case surfaces; the pattern is stable.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on patched `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py`. (b) Live unit check on torchao 0.16.0: shim returns a tag containing `uintx_weight_only=I` and post-call `hasattr(torchao.quantization, "uintx_weight_only")` is True. (c) Non-comfy regression: `bug_bible_regression.py + test_core.py + test_audio_byte_identical.py + test_dropdown_guardrails.py` -- pending. (d) Next live `flux_smoke.json` run should show the shim tag with `uintx_weight_only=I`, then `[flux_anchor] loaded pipeline load_mode=fp8_torchao` with no ImportError, smoke-log `detail=` carrying `load_mode=fp8_torchao` or similar, and the run should take ~60-120s instead of 7.65s (real diffusion, not stub).
- **Tags:** torchao, fp8, uintx-weight-only, compat-shim, pickle, checkpoint-version-mismatch, auto-discovery, camelcase-acronym, flux-anchor, diffusers, silent-stub, bug-046-family, bug-054-extension, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-054: Third ImportError on `int4_weight_only` -- pivot torchao shim from hand-list to auto-discovery [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-20 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** After BUG-LOCAL-053 shipped (commit 9e79da4) a live run (job `vs_8e8fc13cd23a`, sidecar PID 71724 / worker PID 69900, 21:53:18 2026-04-20) fired the expanded shim correctly (`[flux_anchor] torchao compat shim: shim:installed=float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight,float8_static_activation_float8_weight,float8_weight_only`) and progressed to `[flux_anchor] attempting FluxPipeline.from_pretrained load_mode=fp8_torchao`, but still failed FLUX load with a *third* ImportError: `cannot import name 'int4_weight_only' from 'torchao.quantization'`. The FLUX.1-dev-torchao-fp8 checkpoint mixes FP8 tensors with int4-quantized tensors (some MLP/embedding layers), so pickle resolves the int4 helper name next. flux_anchor fell to stub mode a third time while Wan2.1 I2V continued looping over placeholder stills -- the offload flip from BUG-LOCAL-052 is visibly working (Wan2.1 progressed past `num_frames - 1 has to be divisible by 4` 5+ times, which marked the prior wedge point), but the underlying visuals are still not real FLUX keyframes.
- **Cause:** The hand-maintained list in the 053 shim only covered three float8 helpers (`float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight`, `float8_static_activation_float8_weight`, `float8_weight_only`).  torchao 0.16 removed *every* lowercase quantization helper in the same deprecation wave -- eighteen Config classes currently exported, with at least ten matching legacy helper names no longer present: `int4_weight_only`, `int8_weight_only`, `int8_dynamic_activation_int4_weight`, `int8_dynamic_activation_int8_weight`, `int8_static_activation_int8_weight`, `intx_weight_only`, `uint_x_weight_only`, `float8_dynamic_activation_int4_weight`, `float8_dynamic_activation_float8_semi_sparse_weight`, `gemlite_u_int_x_weight_only`.  Any of them could surface next depending on which layers the checkpoint stores quantized -- continuing to hand-maintain the list is whack-a-mole and will keep regressing with every run that exercises a new layer type.
- **Fix:** `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py::_install_torchao_compat_shim()` -- pivoted from a hand-maintained list to auto-discovery.  Walks every attribute of `torchao.quantization` that (a) ends in `Config`, (b) starts uppercase, (c) is an actual `type`.  For each, derives the expected snake_case helper name via `re.sub(r'([a-z0-9])([A-Z])', r'\1_\2', name[:-len('Config')]).lower()` (CamelCase->snake_case with `_config` stripped), and if that helper is not already present, aliases it to the class.  Spurious aliases for non-quant classes (e.g. `Float8MMConfig` -> `float8_mm`) are harmless because pickle only resolves the exact names the checkpoint references.  Returns a structured tag `shim:installed=N;native=M;float8_..=I/N/-;int4_weight_only=I/N/-;...` tracking the five canonical helpers explicitly so the stderr log is greppable.  Single implementation handles all current and future renames as long as torchao keeps its `*Config` naming convention.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on patched `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py`. (b) Live unit check on torchao 0.16.0: shim returns `shim:installed=18;native=0;float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight=I;float8_static_activation_float8_weight=I;float8_weight_only=I;int4_weight_only=I;int8_weight_only=I` and post-call `hasattr(torchao.quantization, name)` is True for all five canonical helpers. (c) Non-comfy regression -- pending. (d) Next live run should show the expanded shim tag, then `[flux_anchor] loaded pipeline load_mode=fp8_torchao` with no ImportError, and every `meta.json` should carry `load_mode="fp8_torchao"` instead of `"stub"`.  If a *fourth* symbol surfaces, it means torchao has introduced a Config class that doesn't follow the standard naming pattern; the shim will log it via `state[h] = "-"` and future-Jeffrey can add it explicitly to the canonical list.
- **Tags:** torchao, fp8, int4-weight-only, compat-shim, pickle, checkpoint-version-mismatch, auto-discovery, flux-anchor, diffusers, silent-stub, bug-046-family, bug-051-extension, bug-053-pivot, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-053: torchao shim only covered the dynamic helper -- checkpoint also references `float8_static_activation_float8_weight`, FLUX keeps stub-falling-back [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-20 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Immediately after BUG-LOCAL-051 + 052 landed (commit 373fcf3), a live `video_stack` run (job `vs_c24ada264401`, sidecar PID 45192, 21:31:27 2026-04-20) still produced 9 still-image shots. `sidecar_stderr.log` shows the 051 shim firing correctly (`[flux_anchor] torchao compat shim: shim_installed:aliased_to_Float8DynamicActivationFloat8WeightConfig`), but immediately after that FLUX load failed again with a *different* ImportError: `cannot import name 'float8_static_activation_float8_weight' from 'torchao.quantization'`. `flux_anchor` fell through to stub mode as per the BUG-LOCAL-046 family, Wan2.1 then looped I2V over solid-color stills, and the BUG-LOCAL-052 offload flip *did* work (Wan2.1 progressed past `num_frames - 1 has to be divisible by 4` three times, which marked the prior wedge point) -- so the video actually ran end-to-end, it was just built on placeholder keyframes.
- **Cause:** The `diffusers/FLUX.1-dev-torchao-fp8` checkpoint's config references *multiple* legacy torchao lowercase helper functions, not just the dynamic one. torchao 0.16 removed several of them in the same deprecation wave (`float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight`, `float8_static_activation_float8_weight`, `float8_weight_only`), replacing each with a corresponding `*Config` class. BUG-LOCAL-051 only aliased the dynamic variant because that was the symbol the initial ImportError named -- but unpickling resolves symbols one-at-a-time as it walks the config tree, so fixing one only reveals the next. Shim was incomplete.
- **Fix:** `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py::_install_torchao_compat_shim()` -- rewrote the shim from a single-symbol alias into a loop over `(legacy_helper_name, replacement_cls_name)` pairs covering all three known legacy helpers: `float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight -> Float8DynamicActivationFloat8WeightConfig`, `float8_static_activation_float8_weight -> Float8StaticActivationFloat8WeightConfig`, `float8_weight_only -> Float8WeightOnlyConfig`. Each pair is independently checked: if torchao natively exports the helper the alias is skipped (`native=...`); if the helper is missing but the Config class is present a `setattr` alias is installed (`installed=...`); if both are missing the helper is recorded as `missing_cls=...` and the shim moves on. Return tag is now structured (`shim:installed=...;native=...;missing_cls=...`) so the stderr log tells the future archaeologist exactly which symbols were repaired on this run. Idempotent -- safe to call multiple times.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on patched `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py`. (b) Live unit check on torchao 0.16.0: `_install_torchao_compat_shim()` returns `shim:installed=float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight,float8_static_activation_float8_weight,float8_weight_only` and post-call `hasattr(torchao.quantization, name)` is True for all three helpers. (c) Non-comfy regression: `tests/test_flux_anchor.py tests/test_ltx_motion.py tests/test_backend_dispatch.py tests/test_dropdown_guardrails.py tests/test_core.py tests/test_audio_byte_identical.py` -- pending. (d) Next live run should log the full `shim:installed=...,static_...,weight_only` tag then `[flux_anchor] loaded pipeline load_mode=fp8_torchao` with no ImportError, and every `meta.json` should carry `load_mode="fp8_torchao"` instead of `"stub"`.
- **Tags:** torchao, fp8, float8-static-activation-float8-weight, compat-shim, pickle, checkpoint-version-mismatch, flux-anchor, diffusers, silent-stub, bug-046-family, bug-051-extension, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-052: Wan2.1 I2V with enable_model_cpu_offload() PCIe-thrashes on 16 GB; flip to sequential offload as primary [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-19 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** After BUG-LOCAL-050 (pipe-leak teardown) and BUG-LOCAL-051 (torchao shim) landed, a chained `video_stack` run still wedged on the Wan2.1 1.3B I2V loop stage. Sidecar stderr log `vs_4564e2329104\sidecar_stderr.log` shows Wan2.1 pipeline loaded cleanly (5/5 components, 10.6s wall clock) and then logged `num_frames - 1 has to be divisible by 4. Rounding to the nearest number.` as the last line before a 215s hang. LHM snapshot during the hang: GPU Core 100%, GPU Memory Controller < 10%, VRAM near-zero free, D3D Shared Memory spilled to system RAM -- textbook PCIe-thrash signature. `_release_pipe()` teardown from BUG-LOCAL-050 is doing its job (flux_anchor and ltx_motion release cleanly, barrier logs show ~15 GB free going into wan21_loop), so this is not a pipe-leak regression -- Wan2.1's own working set does not fit the 14.5 GB OTR ceiling with `enable_model_cpu_offload()`.
- **Cause:** `visual/backends/wan21_loop.py::_try_load_wan_pipeline` preferred `pipe.enable_model_cpu_offload()` (one-module-at-a-time, fast path) and only fell back to `enable_sequential_cpu_offload()` if the first call *raised*. On the RTX 5080 Laptop the model-offload path does *not* raise -- it succeeds, but once the Wan2.1 transformer + VAE + scheduler state all land on-device for a diffusion step the working set exceeds what a single module-grained offload window can page in and out of 14.5 GB. The result is the offload scheduler degenerating into PCIe ping-pong -- same external signature as BUG-LOCAL-049 FLUX BF16 but with a different trigger. Model-offload is the correct path on hardware with more VRAM headroom, so leaving it as an option for future installs (RTX 5090 / A-series) is fine, but on the current 16 GB Blackwell ceiling sequential offload is the only path that actually fits.
- **Fix:** `visual/backends/wan21_loop.py::_try_load_wan_pipeline` -- flip offload priority in both the `WanImageToVideoPipeline` I2V branch (lines ~329-347) and the `WanPipeline` T2V fallback (lines ~364-378). Primary call is now `pipe.enable_sequential_cpu_offload()`; if that raises (older diffusers or hardware without the submodule-grained path), the except clause falls to `pipe.enable_model_cpu_offload()` as the escape. Added BUG-LOCAL-052 comment block above the flip explaining the VRAM-budget rationale so future archaeologists don't "optimize" the flip back. No API contract changes -- shotlist schema, STATUS.json, and meta.json surface are identical; only the internal paging strategy changed.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on patched `visual/backends/wan21_loop.py` (27314 B, 677 lines, no BOM, no CRLF). (b) `grep -c "enable_sequential_cpu_offload" visual/backends/wan21_loop.py` = 5 (two primary calls + two fallback references + BUG comment). (c) Non-comfy regression: `tests/test_flux_anchor.py tests/test_ltx_motion.py tests/test_backend_dispatch.py tests/test_dropdown_guardrails.py tests/test_core.py tests/test_audio_byte_identical.py` = 192 passed / 22 skipped. (d) Next live run should process past the `num_frames - 1 has to be divisible by 4` log line and emit per-shot `wan21_loop render` entries into sidecar stderr. LHM VRAM should stay well under 14.5 GB throughout the Wan2.1 stage; throughput will be slower than model-offload but will actually converge.
- **Tags:** wan21-loop, cpu-offload, sequential-offload, pcie-thrashing, vram-ceiling, blackwell, 16gb, c-constraint-violation, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-051: FLUX torchao FP8 checkpoint fails to unpickle on torchao 0.16 -- `float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight` helper removed, only Config class remains [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-19 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** After BUG-LOCAL-049 swapped FLUX BF16 for the pre-quantized torchao FP8 checkpoint (`models/diffusers/FLUX.1-dev-torchao-fp8`), a chained `video_stack` run in sidecar `vs_4564e2329104` logged `[flux_anchor] fp8_torchao load failed: ImportError: cannot import name 'float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight' from 'torchao.quantization'` and silently fell through to stub mode. Downstream Wan2.1 then ran I2V loops on top of procgen placeholder stills -- exactly the silent-stub failure mode BUG-LOCAL-046 tracks. Because `_log_stderr` was in place from BUG-LOCAL-047 the error was visible in `sidecar_stderr.log`; without it the run would have read green at the status level.
- **Cause:** The `diffusers/FLUX.1-dev-torchao-fp8` checkpoint on HuggingFace was saved with `Float8DynamicActivationFloat8WeightConfig` serialized against an older torchao release that exported a lowercase helper function `torchao.quantization.float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight(...)` which returned a `Float8DynamicActivationFloat8WeightConfig` instance. torchao 0.16 removed the helper; only the Config class remains (see torchao issue #2649 -- deprecation warning `Stored version is not the same as current default version of the config: stored_version=1, current_default_version=2`). When `FluxPipeline.from_pretrained` unpickles the config metadata it resolves the callable by fully-qualified `module.name`, hits `torchao.quantization.float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight`, and the import fails. Re-quantizing would require re-downloading ~53 GB of BF16 weights we deliberately recycled in BUG-LOCAL-049, so an in-process compatibility shim is the clean fix.
- **Fix:** `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py` -- added `_install_torchao_compat_shim()` that does: (1) `import torchao.quantization as _q`, (2) if `_q.float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight` already exists, return `"native"` (no-op for older torchao or a future re-introduction), (3) else `getattr(_q, "Float8DynamicActivationFloat8WeightConfig", None)`; if present, `setattr(_q, "float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight", cfg_cls)` so unpickling resolves to the Config class. The class's `__init__` accepts the same kwargs (`activation_dtype=..., weight_dtype=...`) the old helper took, so any deserialization call site that does `float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight(...)` now gets an equivalent Config instance. Wired into `_try_load_pipeline()` *before* `FluxPipeline.from_pretrained` so the name is present at unpickle time; state tag (`native` / `shim_installed:...` / `shim_skipped:no_config_class` / `torchao_import_failed:...`) is logged via `_log_stderr`. Idempotent and safe to call on every sidecar start.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse clean on patched `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py` (22747 B, 567 lines, no BOM, no CRLF). (b) Live unit check: with torchao 0.16 installed, pre-shim `hasattr(q, 'float8_dynamic_activation_float8_weight')` = False, post-shim = True, and calling with kwargs returns a `Float8DynamicActivationFloat8WeightConfig` instance. (c) Next live run should show `[flux_anchor] torchao compat shim: shim_installed:aliased_to_Float8DynamicActivationFloat8WeightConfig` then `[flux_anchor] loaded pipeline load_mode=fp8_torchao` -- no ImportError, no stub fallback. (d) `meta.json.load_mode = "fp8_torchao"` on every shot instead of `"stub"`.
- **Tags:** torchao, fp8, float8-dynamic-activation-float8-weight, compat-shim, pickle, checkpoint-version-mismatch, flux-anchor, diffusers, silent-stub, bug-046-family, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-050: Chained-backend pipe leak — flux_anchor / ltx_motion / wan21_loop all resident in same sidecar, PCIe thrash at stage 2-3 [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-19 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** Even after BUG-LOCAL-049 landed the torchao FP8 FLUX swap, a live run on `v2.0-alpha` PCIe-thrashed again: sidecar `vs_392f4b84b18a` loaded FLUX successfully, then LTX-2.3 on top, then Wan2.1 on top of that — all inside a single subprocess spawned by `VisualBridge._spawn_sidecar`. LHM snapshot at wedge: GPU Core load 100%, VRAM used 16,014 MB of 16,303 MB (~200 MB free), GPU power ~1 W (idle-range), core clock pinned at boost. `STATUS.json` reported `wan21_loop real mode: N shots, I2V loops from FLUX stills` then froze; six minutes of zero forward progress. Same signature as BUG-LOCAL-048 (starved-spawn thrash) but at a different origin: the spawn itself was clean, the starvation happened *between stages*.
- **Cause:** `visual/backends/video_stack.py::VideoStackBackend.run()` chains three backends sequentially in the same sidecar process: `flux_anchor.FluxAnchorBackend().run(job_dir)` → `ltx_motion.LtxMotionBackend().run(job_dir)` → `wan21_loop.Wan21LoopBackend().run(job_dir)`. Each backend called `enable_model_cpu_offload()` on its pipeline, which installs accelerate `OffloadHook`s on every submodule. When `run()` returned, the local `pipe` variable went out of scope — but accelerate's hooks held references to the submodules, keeping them resident. The next backend's `from_pretrained` call stacked its own pipeline on top of the previous pipeline's held-hook tensors. By Wan2.1's load the combined working set crossed the 14.5 GB ceiling and the offload scheduler degenerated into PCIe ping-pong. `del pipe` at Python level was insufficient because the hook graph kept live tensor pointers alive.
- **Fix:** Three-part teardown across `visual/backends/`: (1) `flux_anchor.py`, `ltx_motion.py`, `wan21_loop.py` — each gained a `_release_pipe(pipe)` helper that calls `pipe.remove_all_hooks()` (accelerate's documented hook teardown), moves `transformer` / `text_encoder` / `text_encoder_2` / `vae` / `scheduler` / `tokenizer` / `tokenizer_2` (+ `image_encoder` for Wan2.1) to CPU via `mod.to("cpu")`, then `del pipe` / `gc.collect()` / `torch.cuda.empty_cache()` / `torch.cuda.ipc_collect()`. Each backend's `run()` / `_render_real()` wraps the render loop in `try: ... finally: _release_pipe(pipe)` so teardown runs even on exception. (2) `video_stack.py` gained `_between_stage_barrier(stage_just_finished)` which runs `gc.collect()` + `torch.cuda.synchronize()` + `empty_cache()` + `ipc_collect()` and logs free VRAM via `torch.cuda.mem_get_info()` to sidecar stderr. Called after every stage (`flux_anchor`, `ltx_motion`, `wan21_loop`). Belt-and-suspenders layered on the per-backend `_release_pipe()` so any residual accelerate / diffusers cache is zeroed before the next pipeline's `from_pretrained` begins. (3) Updated flux_anchor.py docstring to explain the rationale for future archaeologists.
- **Verify:** (a) AST parse + no-BOM check on all 4 touched files: `flux_anchor.py` (19926 B), `ltx_motion.py` (24304 B), `wan21_loop.py` (26246 B), `video_stack.py` (12737 B) — all clean. (b) Backend unit tests pass: `tests/test_flux_anchor.py` (10) + `tests/test_ltx_motion.py` (29) + `tests/test_backend_dispatch.py` (14) = 53 passed in 0.60s. (c) Non-comfy regression: `tests/test_dropdown_guardrails.py tests/test_core.py tests/test_audio_byte_identical.py` = 139 passed / 22 skipped in 0.67s. (d) Next live run should log `[flux_anchor] pipe released (del + gc + empty_cache)`, `[video_stack] barrier after flux_anchor: vram_free=XXXX MB`, etc., in `sidecar_stderr.log`. LHM peak VRAM should stay under 14.5 GB across all three stages, and power should climb from ~1 W to 100-150 W when each stage is actually computing (not thrashing PCIe).
- **Tags:** accelerate, offload-hooks, remove_all_hooks, cpu-offload, pipe-leak, chained-backend, video-stack, flux-anchor, ltx-motion, wan21-loop, pcie-thrashing, vram-ceiling, c-constraint-violation, gc, empty-cache, ipc-collect, bible-candidate

### BUG-LOCAL-049: FLUX BF16 + enable_model_cpu_offload PCIe-thrashes even after pre-spawn flush — swap to pre-quantized torchao FP8 checkpoint [FIXED]
- **Date:** 2026-04-19 | **Phase:** 0 | **Bible candidate:** yes
- **Symptom:** After BUG-LOCAL-048 shipped the `_pre_spawn_vram_flush()` hook and the sidecar started with a cleanly flushed device, FLUX BF16 loaded via the BUG-LOCAL-047 ladder (`torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16` + `enable_model_cpu_offload()`) still wedged during denoise steps. Round-robin consult (ChatGPT gpt-4.1 + Gemini 2.5 Pro, transcript under `docs/2026-04-19-flux-bf16-thrashing-fix__*.md`) converged: BF16 FLUX transformer is 23 GB of weights on disk — even with `enable_model_cpu_offload()` paging at the module level, peak hot-path residency crosses the 14.5 GB OTR ceiling on a 16 GB card because VAE + text_encoder_2 + one transformer block all need to be resident together for a forward step. With no headroom above the ceiling, the offload scheduler degenerates into PCIe ping-pong. The BUG-LOCAL-047 claim of "BF16 + offload ~= 12-13 GB peak" did not reproduce on this machine — reality was 14-16 GB and thrash.
- **Cause:** Two compounding issues. (1) `enable_model_cpu_offload()` (fast path, one-module-at-a-time) is too coarse-grained for the FLUX transformer's internal block dependencies on 16 GB; `enable_sequential_cpu_offload()` (per-submodule paging) is the intended rescue but halves throughput. (2) FP8 via runtime `torch_dtype=torch.float8_e4m3fn` was already blocked by BUG-LOCAL-047 — torch 2.10 / diffusers cannot deserialize `Float8_e4m3fnStorage` from safetensors. The clean solution is a checkpoint that is *already quantized on disk* (pickled `.bin` with `float8_weight_only` applied by torchao at upload time), which sidesteps the safetensors storage-type problem and shrinks the transformer to ~11 GB — comfortably under the 14.5 GB ceiling with `enable_model_cpu_offload()`.
- **Fix:** Three-part swap on `v2.0-alpha`. (1) Download `diffusers/FLUX.1-dev-torchao-fp8` (official HuggingFace team, Diffusers-folder layout) to `models/diffusers/FLUX.1-dev-torchao-fp8` (~17 GB with bundled text encoders + VAE). HF_TOKEN read from HKCU\Environment via winreg. (2) Patch `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py::_try_load_pipeline` to a `(pipe, reason, load_mode)` 3-tuple with two-stage ladder: first try the FP8 torchao folder via `FluxPipeline.from_pretrained(str(path), use_safetensors=False, local_files_only=True)` — no `torch_dtype` argument because the weights carry their own dtype; belt-and-suspenders fallback to `FluxPipeline.from_pretrained(_BF16_MODEL_PATH, torch_dtype=torch.bfloat16, ...)` + `enable_sequential_cpu_offload()` (not `enable_model_cpu_offload`). Added `load_mode` (`fp8_torchao` / `bf16_sequential` / `stub`) stamped onto every `meta.json` and surfaced in STATUS strings. (3) Delete the now-redundant BF16 FLUX checkpoint (53.91 GB) plus LTX-2 19B/22B folder (93.51 GB, never fit 16 GB VRAM) and orphaned LTX-2 19B LoRAs (7.45 GB) — all sent to Recycle Bin (reversible).
- **Verify:** (a) Download complete: `_dl_fp8_done.marker` present, `size_bytes=18232863995` (16.98 GB, 50 files including `.cache` blobs, self-contained diffusers layout with own `text_encoder`, `text_encoder_2`, `tokenizer`, `tokenizer_2`, `vae`, `scheduler`). (b) AST parse clean on patched `visual/backends/flux_anchor.py` (482 lines, 19186 bytes). (c) Regression: `tests\test_dropdown_guardrails.py tests\test_core.py tests\test_audio_byte_identical.py` — 160 passed, 1 skipped, EXIT=0 in 112.75s. (d) Disk reclaim: ~155 GB (LTX-2 folder 93.51 GB + FLUX.1-dev BF16 folder 53.91 GB + two orphan LoRAs 7.45 GB) recycled. (e) Next live run should show `[flux_anchor] loaded pipeline load_mode=fp8_torchao` in `sidecar_stderr.log`, `meta.json.load_mode="fp8_torchao"`, and LHM peak VRAM < 14.5 GB during the FLUX denoise window.
